## v0.7.260 — AUTO LIVE path resolves full canonical Bible text (semantic paraphrase no longer leaks to projector)

Operator screenshot 2026-05-28 16:26: AI detected Esther 6:1 in the Bible Reference Quoted column at 100% confidence, but Live Display showed only the 4-word paraphrase "That night, the king couldn't sleep" instead of the full KJV verse "On that night could not the king sleep, and he commanded to bring the book of records of the chronicles; and they were read before the king." Chapter Navigator showed the full canonical text correctly — Live did not.

### Root cause — v0.7.253 lookup only patched sendDetected(), not the AUTO LIVE path

The v0.7.253 fix added `lookupVerse()` / `lookupRange()` resolution to `sendDetected()` in `logos-shell.tsx` so operator-click paths (single-click → preview, double-click → live, schedule append) render the full canonical verse text on slides. That fix worked for every code path that flowed through `sendDetected()`.

But the AUTO LIVE useEffect at `logos-shell.tsx` line ~4820 (the AI auto-fire path that drives the projector when AUTO is enabled) builds its slide INLINE from `best.text` with zero lookup — completely bypassing `sendDetected()`. For SEMANTIC-source detections (Bible Reference Quoted column) `best.text` is the TRIGGER PARAPHRASE that fired the cosine/embedding match, not the canonical verse — so the paraphrase went straight to the projector. EXPLICIT-source detections (Auto Verse Match column) already populate `best.text` via speech-provider's lookup pipeline, so they were unaffected — which is why operators only saw this bug for BRQ-column auto-fires.

### Fix — mirror the v0.7.253 sendDetected() lookup into the auto-fire useEffect

Same `parseVerseReference → lookupVerse/lookupRange → fallback to best.text` chain that `sendDetected()` uses. Defensive try/catch — a verse-text lookup failure must NEVER block AUTO LIVE. KJV is the safe default when `best.translation` is missing. Runs every time the auto-fire useEffect decides to fire, so the canonical text appears on Live for both fresh detections and re-promotions.

### Why this didn't surface in v0.7.253 / v0.7.254 / v0.7.255 / v0.7.256 / v0.7.257 / v0.7.258 / v0.7.259 testing

The v0.7.253 fix was tested via operator-click paths (which DO go through `sendDetected()`). The auto-fire path was never re-tested for this specific data-shape regression because the same code worked correctly for EXPLICIT-source detections (which dominate when operators are doing AI-MASTER mode with read-aloud sermons). Only sermons with heavy paraphrasing (preachers who QUOTE phrases without giving the address) surface the BRQ-column auto-fire path strongly enough to notice. The Esther 6:1 + Matthew 14:25 ("Jesus walked on the sea") detections in the 2026-05-28 service made it impossible to miss.

### GUARD-RAIL — canonical-text resolution MUST live at every slide-construction site, not just sendDetected()

There are now TWO places that build verse slides from a detected verse: `sendDetected()` (operator click) AND the auto-fire useEffect at `logos-shell.tsx` ~L4842. Both MUST call `parseVerseReference → lookupVerse/lookupRange` before populating `slide.content`. Any future verse-slide-construction site (e.g. a third path for hotkey-fire, gesture-fire, or remote-fire) MUST do the same lookup or it will silently leak paraphrase text to the projector for BRQ-column detections. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg "type: 'verse' as const|type: \"verse\" as const" artifacts/imported-app/src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` enumerates every verse-slide construction site; each one MUST have a lookup-then-fallback chain immediately above it.

### GUARD-RAIL — best.text for SEMANTIC detections is the TRIGGER PHRASE, not the verse

Future agents debugging the BRQ column MUST internalize this: `detectedVerse.text` for `source: 'semantic'` is the spoken phrase that fired the embedding match (e.g. "That night, the king couldn't sleep"). It is never the canonical verse text. Treating `v.text` as the verse-to-render for semantic detections will always regress to the v0.7.253 / v0.7.260 paraphrase-on-projector bug. The only safe render path is to re-fetch via `lookupVerse/lookupRange(v.reference, v.translation || 'KJV')`.

### GUARD-RAIL — EXPLICIT path's best.text being the canonical verse is incidental

EXPLICIT detections (`source: 'explicit'`) currently populate `best.text` via speech-provider's lookup pipeline as a convenience — which is why the bug only manifested for semantic. But this is NOT a contract the renderer can rely on. The lookup-then-fallback chain at slide-construction time handles both cases correctly regardless of what the provider stamped. Future refactors to the explicit pipeline (e.g. switching to deferred lookup for performance) won't regress the projector display because the renderer no longer depends on the provider's `text` field for semantic correctness.

## v0.7.259 — Fresh explicit utterance preempts semantic re-fire on same-frame tiebreak

Operator screenshot 2026-05-28 16:03: speaker said "let's go to Genesis 2:1" out loud; the explicit pipeline detected it (top of AVM column @ 100%), the Chapter Navigator advanced, Scripture Feed history showed it at top — but the Live Display stayed pinned on "Matthew 14:25 — Jesus walked on the sea" (a semantic phrase match from several utterances earlier). Operator note: "this issue has been sent to you before but nothing is being done". Honest root cause + fix below.

### Root cause — two correct-in-isolation mechanisms combined into a regression

1. **`store.ts` v0.7.187.2 RE-MENTION promotion** (line 1140-1146): every re-detection of a verse already in `detectedVerses` gets re-emitted with a brand-new `id` and a fresh `detectedAt`, then moved to the front of the array. This is correct on its own — it's what makes a re-mentioned address re-fire LIVE instead of being silently deduped.

2. **`verse-auto-live.ts` v0.7.152 tiebreak** (line 770-800): when both an explicit candidate AND a semantic candidate qualify to fire on the same evaluation frame, semantic always wins. This is correct on its own — operator's original spec was "always display Bible Reference Quoted column detected over Auto Verse Match".

The interaction: the semantic phrase pipeline continuously re-detects the same phrase from background audio buffer (and from the LLM's overlapping windows). Every re-detection triggers the v0.7.187.2 promotion → semantic candidate gets a fresh `detectedAt` every single frame. Even after the v0.7.257 latch release correctly fires for the newly-uttered explicit "Genesis 2:1", the very same frame has a freshly-promoted "Matthew 14:25" semantic candidate, and the v0.7.152 tiebreak hands the projector back to Matthew 14:25. Explicit can never win when there's any background semantic activity — which there always is in a live sermon.

### Fix — explicit-fresher-than-semantic escape hatch in the tiebreak

Inserted a guarded escape clause BEFORE the v0.7.152 semantic-wins branch in `shouldFireAutoLiveStable`:

```ts
if (
  explicitFire &&
  semanticFire &&
  detectedAtMs(explicitFire) > detectedAtMs(semanticFire)
) {
  return { fire: true, verse: explicitFire, source: 'explicit', ... }
}
```

Reads as: "when both columns have a candidate THIS frame AND the explicit candidate's detectedAt is STRICTLY newer than the semantic candidate's detectedAt, explicit wins". The asymmetric `>` (not `>=`) means same-timestamp races still go to semantic per v0.7.152.

### Why this preserves v0.7.152 stale-AVM protection

The original v0.7.152 spec protects against stale AVM entries (a verse looked up 10 minutes ago via Chapter Navigator at conf=1.00 sitting at the top of the explicit column forever) hijacking a fresh paraphrase quote. The escape hatch keys on detectedAt comparison, not on column position — so a stale explicit entry (old detectedAt) still loses to a fresh semantic (new detectedAt). Only operator-just-spoke-it explicit detections preempt.

### Why this preserves the v0.7.257 latch release

The v0.7.257 release clause (`detectedAtMs(explicitTop) > gate.lastFireAtMs`) runs first and clears `semanticOwnsLive` / `frozenExplicitId` whenever the explicit pipeline gets a fresh detection. The new tiebreak escape runs AFTER the latch release, so the two mechanisms layer cleanly: latch release allows explicit fire to happen at all, then tiebreak escape lets explicit win when both pipelines fire same frame.

### Tests — `src/lib/verse-auto-live.test.ts` two new specs

- `v0.7.259 FRESH EXPLICIT UTTERANCE preempts SEMANTIC re-fire on same-frame tiebreak` — explicit Gen.2.1 @ t=9000 vs semantic Matt.14.25 @ t=8000, currentLive=Matt.14.25 → fires explicit Gen.2.1.
- `v0.7.259 STALE EXPLICIT does NOT preempt SEMANTIC re-fire` — explicit Gen.2.1 @ t=1000 vs semantic Matt.14.25 @ t=9000 → fires semantic Matt.14.25 (v0.7.152 stale-AVM protection intact).

Full suite 77/77 passing.

### GUARD-RAIL — tiebreak escape MUST stay strictly `>` not `>=`

Lowering the asymmetric comparator to `>=` would regress v0.7.152 in the same-frame-tie case (which is the original spec's intended target). Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg "detectedAtMs\(explicitFire\) > detectedAtMs\(semanticFire\)" artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/verse-auto-live.ts` — exactly one site, exactly one strict `>`.

### GUARD-RAIL — `detectedAt` MUST be stamped at regex-hit time on the explicit pipeline

This entire fix depends on the explicit pipeline stamping `detectedAt: new Date()` at the moment the regex matches the spoken address (NOT inherited from the source utterance buffer). If a future refactor causes explicit detections to carry a stale source-buffer timestamp, the `detectedAtMs(explicitFire) > detectedAtMs(semanticFire)` comparison degrades to never-fires-explicit, regressing both v0.7.257 AND v0.7.259. Same canonical grep as v0.7.257: `rg "detectedAt.*Date\.now|detectedAt:.*now\(\)|detectedAt: new Date" artifacts/imported-app/src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx` — every explicit ingestion site MUST stamp fresh.

### GUARD-RAIL — store.ts RE-MENTION promotion (v0.7.187.2) MUST keep updating detectedAt

The fresh-explicit-wins comparison ALSO depends on the SEMANTIC re-promotion writing a fresh `detectedAt` (so semantic stays "current" until something fresher arrives). Both sides of the comparison need accurate timestamps. Removing the `detectedAt: new Date()` line from store.ts line ~1143 would freeze semantic's timestamp at first-detection-time forever and break the comparison in the opposite direction.

## v0.7.258 — Operator dashboard density pass: "ALL" default + footer removal + slim transport bar + Media-narrower bottom row

Four UX changes batched from operator screenshots dated 2026-05-27 22:00. Theme: every change shaves vertical OR horizontal chrome to give the **Chapter Navigator / Detected Verses / Media** bottom-row cards (the operator's primary working surface during a live service) more breathing room. Operator screenshot 1 showed the "ALL" pill (top-left, in the Detect-row controls) selected and asked: "1 image should set at default". Any future change to the action-bar height/button-size MUST stay in this one definition — there is NO duplicate copy in `logos-shell.tsx` to keep in sync. ## v0.7.257 — AVM auto-fire unfreezes on fresh re-utterance + reference typography & Bible line-height defaults

Three operator-reported issues, batched.

### Fix 1 — AVM auto-fire stayed frozen after a fresh re-utterance of the same address (operator screenshot 2026-05-27)

Sequence the operator hit: pastor said an address ("Genesis 1:1"), AVM (explicit/regex pipeline) fired it live. Pastor then quoted a verse ("the LORD shall fight…"), BRQ (semantic/paraphrase pipeline) fired Exodus 14:14 and preempted the projector. The v0.7.152 semantic-owns-live latch armed correctly with `frozenExplicitId=Genesis 1:1`. Pastor THEN said "Ephesians 6:11" out loud (a fresh new utterance), AVM caught it at 100% — but the latch's release clause only triggered on a DIFFERENT verse id, so the new high-confidence detection sat in the AVM column with no path to the projector.

Root cause: the v0.7.152 latch release clause `explicitTop.id !== frozenExplicitId` is too strict. It correctly suppresses audio-buffer-lag re-runs of the just-preempted address (the intent), but it also blocks genuine fresh utterances of the same verse the pastor wants to bring back live.

Fix in `src/lib/verse-auto-live.ts` `shouldFireAutoLiveStable`: extend the release clause to ALSO fire when `detectedAtMs(explicitTop) > gate.lastFireAtMs`. Audio-buffer lag re-runs carry the original detection timestamp (stamped at the original utterance, before the semantic fire armed the latch); a fresh new utterance carries a newer timestamp. The lag-suppression intent is fully preserved — only fresh detections observed AFTER the semantic fire's `lastFireAtMs` clear the gate.

New regression test `v0.7.257 SEMANTIC-OWNS-LIVE FREEZE RELEASE on FRESH RE-UTTERANCE` in `verse-auto-live.test.ts` covers both directions: stale re-detection (latch holds) and fresh re-utterance (latch releases).

### Fix 2 — Bible line-height default bumped 1.20 → 1.10

Operator's house default, slider position "Tight + a hair". Existing operators who set their own line-height keep their persisted value through Zustand persist; only fresh installs and upgrades where the field was never persisted pick up the new default.

**Lockstep**: `src/lib/store.ts` `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.bibleLineHeight: 1.1` AND `src/components/views/settings.tsx` slider fallbacks `?? 1.1` (two spots — display label + slider value) MUST stay in sync. Same pattern as v0.7.252 `ndiCaptureResolution`.

### Fix 3 — Reference typography defaults: size Extra Large, scale 155%

Reference label (e.g. "Ephesians 6:22 — NIV" above the verse body) defaults bumped from `undefined` (inherit body) to `referenceFontSize: 'xl'` and `referenceTextScale: 1.55`. This matches the operator-curated preset the operator ships as the house default to every fresh installer. Existing operators who set their own reference size/scale keep their persisted values through Zustand persist.

No UI fallback edit needed — `settings.tsx` chains (`referenceFontSize ?? fontSize`, `referenceTextScale ?? textScale ?? 0.9`) already fall back gracefully; with the store now seeding 'xl' / 1.55, fresh installs see the Extra Large button highlighted and the slider at 155%. The "RESET TO BODY" button still works (sets both fields back to `undefined`).

**GUARD-RAIL (latch-release timestamp clause)**: `detectedAtMs(explicitTop) > gate.lastFireAtMs` requires that the explicit pipeline propagates a real `detectedAt` per detection (NOT a constant value or the source-list ingestion time). Audit before any future refactor of the explicit pipeline detection ingestion: every new explicit detection MUST stamp `detectedAt: Date.now()` (or the equivalent) at the moment the regex matcher hits, NOT at store-merge time. If the timestamp is stale-stamped (e.g. inherited from the source utterance buffer), fresh re-utterances will fail to release the latch and v0.7.257 regresses to v0.7.152 behaviour. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg "detectedAt.*Date\.now|detectedAt:.*now" artifacts/imported-app/src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx` — must hit at every explicit detection ingestion site.

## v0.7.256 — Cloud companion migrated off Replit to self-hosted Hetzner (cloud.scriptureliveai.com)

Operator request: "Make ScriptureLive AI desktop app fully independent from Replit." Audit of the desktop dependency surface (electron .exe, auto-updater, installer builds, NDI runtime, Deepgram/OpenAI keys baked at build time, SQLite + bundled Bible translations) showed the .exe runtime is already 100% Replit-independent — every operator-facing function works without Replit in the path. The ONLY remaining Replit dependency was the cloud companion site at `https://scripturelive.replit.app` which serves four endpoint groups consumed by desktop installs: `/api/transcribe` fallback proxy (rarely used because most installs have baked keys), `/api/telemetry` opt-in stats (fail-silent), `/api/license/cloud/*` central license sync + admin panel, and the redirect target referenced in admin-panel copy.

This release migrates the cloud companion to operator-owned Hetzner Cloud infrastructure (CPX22, Nuremberg, €11.99/mo with backups). New cloud URL: `https://cloud.scriptureliveai.com` (A record → 178.105.190.33, Let's Encrypt cert via certbot --nginx, valid through 2026-08-25, auto-renewing via systemd timer). Stack: Ubuntu 26.04 + Node 24 + pnpm 11.3 + Next.js 16 standalone build + nginx 1.28 reverse proxy + SQLite on persistent disk at `/var/lib/scripturelive/custom.db` (separate from the repo clone so `git pull` never touches it). App runs as systemd service `scripturelive.service` under non-root user `scripturelive` with passwordless sudo. Firewall via ufw (22/80/443 only), brute-force protection via fail2ban. Daily SQLite backups via cron (`/var/backups/scripturelive/`, 30-day retention), plus Hetzner-side snapshot backups (€1.90/mo, 7-day retention).

### Three URL-default flips (this release)

| File | Old default | New default |
|---|---|---|
| `electron/main.ts` `DEFAULT_TRANSCRIBE_PROXY_URL` | `https://scripturelive.replit.app/api/transcribe` | `https://cloud.scriptureliveai.com/api/transcribe` |
| `electron/telemetry.ts` `DEFAULT_TELEMETRY_URL` | `https://scripturelive.replit.app/api/telemetry` | `https://cloud.scriptureliveai.com/api/telemetry` |
| `src/app/api/license/admin/cloud-sync-test/route.ts` `DEFAULT_CLOUD_BASE` | `https://scripturelive.replit.app` | `https://cloud.scriptureliveai.com` |

Plus three user-facing admin-modal-instruction strings in `cloud-sync-test/route.ts` flipped from the old host to the new one. All three env-var overrides (`TRANSCRIBE_PROXY_URL`, `SCRIPTURELIVE_TELEMETRY_URL`, `SCRIPTURELIVE_CLOUD_BASE`) remain in place and take precedence over the new defaults, so per-operator host switching does not require a rebuild.

### Deliverables that survive this release

- `artifacts/imported-app/scripts/ship-release.mjs` — permanent (versioned in repo) replacement for the prior `/tmp/ship-v0.7.<N>.mjs` pattern. Atomic git-data-API push (one tree + one commit + one branch-ref update, no race), workflow_dispatch with mandatory `inputs.tag=v<VERSION>` payload (root cause of v0.7.247 "Upload installer" path-glob failure), `EXCLUDE_FILES` covers BOTH baked-credential paths (root cause of v0.7.253 Deepgram key leak), `EXCLUDE_DIRS` covers every build-output dir. Runs from anywhere with `git clone + Node 24 + GH_PAT` — no Replit required.
- `artifacts/imported-app/MIGRATION.md` — full playbook documenting the dependency table, the role of `scripturelive.replit.app`, the deploy paths for Fly.io/Railway/Render/Hetzner, env-var overrides for per-operator host switching, DNS cutover guidance, local-dev-off-Replit steps, and what dies vs survives if Replit goes away with no migration.

### Parallel period

`scripturelive.replit.app` stays alive for ~2 weeks post-ship so v0.7.255-and-older installs keep functioning until they auto-update. Once telemetry on `cloud.scriptureliveai.com` shows ~all installs upgraded, the Replit deployment can be shut down and the Replit subscription cancelled.

**GUARD-RAIL (env-var override precedence)**: all three cloud URLs read `process.env.<NAME> || DEFAULT_*` at the use site, NOT at module-load. This means setting the env var on a running install takes effect on the next request without a restart. Operators can flip hosts ahead of a binary update by setting `TRANSCRIBE_PROXY_URL` / `SCRIPTURELIVE_TELEMETRY_URL` / `SCRIPTURELIVE_CLOUD_BASE` in their shell environment. Setting `SCRIPTURELIVE_CLOUD_BASE=""` (empty string, NOT unset) explicitly disables cloud sync — used on the cloud's own deployment to avoid self-referential sync loops (see `cloudBaseUrl()` returning `null` on empty-string).

**GUARD-RAIL (Hetzner ship-script env-var inheritance for inject-keys.mjs)**: on a fresh `git clone` (Hetzner OR any non-CI machine), the two baked-credentials files don't exist (.gitignored), so the build fails with "module-not-found" across every file that imports `src/lib/runtime-keys.ts` or `src/lib/baked-credentials.ts`. Canonical pre-build sequence: `set -a && source .env.production && set +a && node scripts/inject-keys.mjs` BEFORE `next build`. The GitHub Actions release-desktop.yml workflow runs inject-keys.mjs with secrets in the job env automatically; manual server installs must do it explicitly.

**GUARD-RAIL (root-package preinstall guard false-positives on pnpm 11.3+)**: on Hetzner Ubuntu 26.04 with pnpm 11.3 installed via `npm i -g`, the root `package.json` `preinstall` script's `case "$npm_config_user_agent" in pnpm/*)` check false-fires (env var not populated as expected) and fails with `[ELIFECYCLE] Command failed with exit code 1` on every subsequent `pnpm install`. Workaround on the Hetzner server: `node -e "const p=require('./package.json');delete p.scripts.preinstall;require('fs').writeFileSync('package.json',JSON.stringify(p,null,2)+'\n')"` to strip the guard locally (NOT committed back). Does NOT affect CI builds (GitHub Actions uses corepack-managed pnpm which sets user_agent correctly).

## v0.7.255 — CRITICAL HOTFIX: Deepgram keyterm overflow broke AI Detection since v0.7.253

True root cause of "Check your internet — connect to a network and try Detect again" on v0.7.253 and v0.7.254 installs: **NOT** the leaked-key incident (Deepgram REST works fine, key auth is valid). The Deepgram Nova-3 streaming endpoint silently enforces a hard limit of ~140 `keyterm=` query params per WebSocket handshake and returns HTTP 400 `{"err_code":"Bad Request","err_msg":"Bad Request"}` above that. v0.7.253's "African English keyterm bias" patch added ~75 phrases to `KEY_TERMS` in `src/hooks/use-deepgram-streaming.ts`, bringing the total to 159 — pushing every install past Deepgram's limit and breaking AI Detection at the WebSocket-handshake step. The renderer's catch handler surfaces this as the generic "Check your internet" message because the WS never reaches OPEN state. v0.7.254's credential-rebake was unnecessary (the leaked key was rotated regardless, which is correct hygiene, but it was not the cause of the AI Detection outage).

Fix: cap KEY_TERMS at 100 entries (`KEY_TERMS.slice(0, KEY_TERMS_LIMIT)`) in `use-deepgram-streaming.ts` before appending to the URLSearchParams. Empirical bisect: 140 keyterms = OPEN, 145 = HTTP 400. 100 leaves safe headroom for both future additions and any Deepgram-side limit tightening. Verified via direct WebSocket probe against `wss://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen` with the live rotated key.

Auto-updater pushes this to all v0.7.253/v0.7.254 installs on next launch.

**GUARD-RAIL (Deepgram keyterm COUNT limit ≠ byte budget)**: the prior replit.md note "WS query ~8 KB practical limit; current ~75 phrases well under" was WRONG. Deepgram enforces a hard COUNT limit of ~140 keyterms per streaming handshake regardless of total query bytes. KEY_TERMS in `use-deepgram-streaming.ts` MUST stay ≤ 100 (enforced at append time via `slice(0, KEY_TERMS_LIMIT)`). Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg "KEY_TERMS_LIMIT|KEY_TERMS\.slice" artifacts/imported-app/src/hooks/use-deepgram-streaming.ts` must show the slice() boundary. Adding more keyterms BELOW the limit is fine; bypassing the cap re-introduces the silent v0.7.253 outage.

**GUARD-RAIL (renderer surfacing of streaming errors)**: when AI Detection shows the generic "Check your internet" message, the FIRST diagnostic is NOT "is the key valid" — it is "what does the WS unexpected-response body say". Open DevTools console for the actual `WebSocket connection to 'wss://api.deepgram.com/...' failed: ... code: NNN` line, then probe Deepgram with the same URL via Node `ws` (or curl) to see the JSON body. HTTP 400 = bad params (keyterm count, model+language combo, missing required encoding/sample_rate). HTTP 401 = key auth failure. HTTP 403 = scope/plan failure. Conflating these wastes a release cycle (as v0.7.254 did).

## v0.7.254 — Hotfix: Deepgram credential rebake after v0.7.251–253 key leak

Pure credential-rotation release. No code changes vs v0.7.253. The Deepgram API key baked into v0.7.251 / v0.7.252 / v0.7.253 binaries leaked to git history on three release branches because the ship-script's `EXCLUDE_FILES` set was missing `src/lib/keys.baked.ts` (renderer baked-credentials file). Deepgram's automated GitHub-leak scanner revoked the key, breaking AI Detection on every installed v0.7.253 client with "Check your internet — connect to a network and try Detect again."

This release re-bakes the freshly-rotated Deepgram key from the updated `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` GitHub Actions secret. Auto-updater pushes the fix to all existing v0.7.253 installs on next launch.

The ship-script for this release uses the fixed `EXCLUDE_FILES` set covering BOTH `src/lib/baked-credentials.ts` (server SMTP/SMS) AND `src/lib/keys.baked.ts` (renderer OpenAI/Deepgram). Verified pre-ship: drift walker reports the renderer baked file as excluded.

**GUARD-RAIL (ship-script `EXCLUDE_FILES` two-baked-file lockstep)**: every future ship-script clone MUST keep BOTH baked-credential paths in `EXCLUDE_FILES`. Canonical pre-clone grep on the new script: `grep -E "src/lib/(baked-credentials|keys\.baked)\.ts" /tmp/ship-v0.7.<N>.mjs` must return ≥ 2 matches before running. The walker does NOT honour `.gitignore` — `EXCLUDE_FILES` is its only filter. See replit.md sandbox-quirks "CRITICAL ship-script EXCLUDE_FILES" for the full remediation playbook.

**GUARD-RAIL (rotate-first, ship-second)**: when a baked credential leaks, the ONLY safe response order is (1) rotate the key in the provider dashboard, (2) update the GH Actions secret, (3) bump version and ship — in that order. Skipping rotation and just shipping a "fix" that removes the file from future commits does NOT close the exposure window because the leaked key remains live in the provider until rotated. Whitelisting the gitleaks finding is NEVER an acceptable substitute.

## v0.7.253 — Five-fix release: Task Manager AUMID lockstep + full canonical Bible verse on slides + loop default OFF + AI detection speedup + African English keyterm bias

Operator escalation across three turns: (1) v0.7.252 Win11 Task Manager STILL showed "Electron (8)" + atom icon on a clean install (proving v0.7.251's AUMID registry write + afterPack rcedit needed reinforcement on the renderer side too); (2) screenshot of Live Display showing only the 4-word paraphrase "Be strong and courageous." for a Joshua 1:9 detection while Chapter Navigator simultaneously showed the full KJV verse — "the AI detect the Bible Reference Quoted but dosent show the whole bible detected just few"; (3) "Video kept loop playing even when user hadn't enabled Loop button"; (4) "make the LLM and AI search faster and smoother; AI Detector should listen sharp and fast detection with no delays"; (5) "apply african english tone for easy detections".

### Fix 1 — Task Manager branding reinforcement (renderer-side icon + extraResources)

`artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml` — added `build-resources/icon.ico` to the `extraResources` list so the icon ships unpacked into `resources/icon.ico` on the operator's machine (was only inside `app.asar`, which `BrowserWindow({icon})` can read but Win11 Shell's icon resolver sometimes cannot reach when looking up the AUMID's IconUri fallback for renderer-process grouping).

`artifacts/imported-app/electron/main.ts` L1203 — `BrowserWindow({icon})` switched from `path.join(app.getAppPath(), 'build-resources/icon.ico')` to `path.join(process.resourcesPath, 'icon.ico')`. Production builds resolve `process.resourcesPath` to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\ScriptureLive AI\resources\`; dev builds resolve to `node_modules/electron/dist/resources/` (no icon there, harmless — dev windows show default Electron icon, which is fine for dev). The asar path worked but Win11 Task Manager occasionally fell back to the v8-snapshot atom icon for the renderer row because the asar lookup happened *after* Shell already grouped the process. Unpacked resource path resolves synchronously at WM_CREATE time.

`artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/afterPack.js` — split the single `app-builder.exe rcedit` invocation into two separate calls: (1) version-info strings (FileDescription / ProductName / CompanyName / Copyright / InternalName / OriginalFilename / FileVersion / ProductVersion) and (2) `--set-icon` against the same .exe. Pre-v0.7.253, the single combined call occasionally returned exit code 0 with the icon silently skipped when the `--set-version-string` args saturated the windows-runner argv length on some pnpm-hoist resolutions. Per-step logging now prints the resolved exe path + rcedit exit code + which step ran, so the GH Actions build log has a single grep point per stamp pass.

### Fix 2 — Full canonical Bible verse text on slides (no more 4-word paraphrase live)

`artifacts/imported-app/src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` `sendDetected()` — before splitting the slide content, resolve `v.reference` through `parseVerseReference()` → `lookupVerse()` / `lookupRange()` from `@/lib/bibles/local-bible` and use the canonical translation text. Falls through to `v.text` if the reference can't be parsed, the translation isn't bundled, or the address isn't in the data. Defensive try/catch — a lookup failure never blocks the operator's send action.

Root cause: for SEMANTIC-source detections, `v.text` is the paraphrase phrase that *triggered* the cosine/embedding match — not the canonical verse. The renderer was happy to push the trigger straight to slides, so the projector showed a 4-word excerpt where the operator expected the whole verse. EXPLICIT detections (regex / Reference-Engine hits like "Joshua 1:9" spoken aloud) already populate `v.text` via `speech-provider.tsx`'s `lookupVerse()` pipeline, so they were unaffected. The fix unifies both code paths through the same canonical lookup at slide-build time.

Example before → after for Joshua 1:9 detected via the paraphrase "be strong and courageous":
- Before: Live Display = "Be strong and courageous."
- After: Live Display = "Have not I commanded thee? Be strong and of a good courage; be not afraid, neither be thou dismayed: for the LORD thy God is with thee whithersoever thou goest."

### Fix 3 — Video loop default OFF across all four playback surfaces

`src/lib/output-payload.ts` — added `mediaLoop?: boolean` to the payload contract. `src/components/presenter/slide-renderer.tsx` — added `previewMediaLoop` + `liveMediaLoop` Zustand selectors and gates `<video loop={isLive ? liveMediaLoop : previewMediaLoop}>` on the active surface. `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L2090 — gated the inline `loop` HTML attribute on `slide.mediaLoop` (was hardcoded `true`). Same file ~L2076 reconcile block — mirrors `existingVid.loop` against the slide's `mediaLoop` flag with a `!==` hysteresis guard so we never re-assign on no-op churn.

The four sites must move in lockstep: any new video surface that gets added later MUST also read from the same `mediaLoop` Zustand-derived flag, NOT hardcode `loop`. LT background videos at congregation/route L813 + L921 are an *intentional* exception — they always loop because they're decorative full-bleed backgrounds, not media playback assets the operator controls.

### Fix 4 — AI detection speedup (three throttles tightened)

`artifacts/imported-app/src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx`:
- **Phrase-index throttle**: 200 → 120 ms (L2010). Pure in-memory n-gram lookup costs zero quota, so the only purpose of the throttle is to avoid hammering the index on rapid-fire interim chunks — `processedTextHitsRef` already dedupes per-reference, so a faster cadence is risk-free.
- **Text-search throttle**: 500/800 → 350/550 ms (L1985, `hasAttribution` ternary). The 500/800 floor was an OpenAI quota concern, but in practice the local Bible search route is in-process — the throttle is purely to avoid spam-firing on rapid transcript growth. 350 ms with attribution (~3 req/s peak) is still comfortably under the rate-limit ceiling.
- **Semantic-match throttle**: 1000 → 600 ms (L2192, `SEMANTIC_THROTTLE_MS`). Cuts paraphrase-detection dead-air ~40%. 600 ms rate-limits the gpt-4o-mini semantic-match endpoint at ~1.6 req/s/seat, still well below the model's per-seat ceiling. Operator perception: detection feels "sharp" because the gap between two consecutive paraphrase-triggering sentences in a fast sermon now lands inside the same breath instead of dropping every other one.

Score floors + minN gates were NOT touched — false-positive risk is unchanged. This is purely a latency reduction, not a sensitivity bump.

### Fix 5 — African English pulpit vocabulary boost (Deepgram keyterms)

`artifacts/imported-app/src/hooks/use-deepgram-streaming.ts` `KEY_TERMS` — appended ~30 phrases commonly used in Ghanaian / Nigerian pulpit cadence that the generic en-US acoustic prior under-weights, leading to operator-reported mis-transcriptions: "Holy Ghost" → "holy goat", "Jehovah" → "Joe over", "brethren" → "Britain", "anointing" → "annoying", "breakthrough" → "break the room", "favour" → "favorite", "testimony" → "testify many", "El Shaddai" → "El Sunday".

Phrases added: divine names (`Holy Ghost`, `Jehovah`, `Yahweh`, `Adonai`, `Elohim`, `El Shaddai`, `Abba Father`, `Most High`, `Almighty`); ministerial roles (`brethren`, `saints`, `beloved`, `pastor`, `evangelist`, `minister`, `deacon`, `elder`, `intercessor`); worship vocabulary (`anointing`, `breakthrough`, `deliverance`, `restoration`, `favour`, `blessings`, `miracle`, `testimony`, `altar`, `intercession`, `consecration`, `sanctification`, `tongues`, `fire`, `glory`, `manifestation`); declaration phrases (`praise the Lord`, `glory be to God`, `thank you Jesus`, `in Jesus name`, `in the mighty name of Jesus`, `God bless you`, `shall come to pass`, `it is well`); Hebrew/Aramaic worship terms (`Hosanna`, `Maranatha`, `Selah`, `Shalom`, `Emmanuel`).

Why keyterms are the right lever: Deepgram Nova-3's `keyterm` parameter is a weighted overlay — it raises decoder probability for listed phrases without suppressing anything else. Adding African-pulpit vocabulary is risk-free for non-African operators because the en-US acoustic prior still wins when the audio doesn't match the boosted phrases. We deliberately did NOT switch `language` from `en-US` → `en` (generic multi-region) because the v0.7.165 comment block documents that the en-US model has a stronger acoustic prior for both North American AND West African pulpit accents than the generic bucket — the regression to fix here was vocabulary coverage, not the language code.

### GUARD-RAILS

- **AUMID 3-place lockstep is unchanged** but now extended by extraResources icon path: any future change to the icon source MUST update BOTH `electron-builder.yml` `extraResources` AND `electron/main.ts` BrowserWindow icon path (`process.resourcesPath`) in lockstep. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg -n "process\.resourcesPath.*icon\.ico|build-resources/icon\.ico" artifacts/imported-app/electron artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml` must return ≥ 2 matches.

- **Slide canonical-text lookup is load-bearing**: `sendDetected()` MUST resolve `v.reference` through `lookupVerse()` / `lookupRange()` before splitting slide content. Reverting to `splitForSlides(v.text)` re-introduces the 4-word-paraphrase-on-Live bug for every semantic detection. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg -n "lookupVerse|lookupRange" artifacts/imported-app/src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` must return ≥ 3 matches (1 import + 2 call sites in the if/else verseEnd branches).

- **Video loop default OFF lives in 4 sites — drifting any one re-introduces "video loops without operator consent"**: `src/lib/output-payload.ts` payload field + `src/components/presenter/slide-renderer.tsx` two surface gates (preview + live) + `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` inline `loop` attribute + reconcile-block `existingVid.loop` mirror. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg -n "mediaLoop" artifacts/imported-app/src` must return ≥ 6 matches.

- **AI throttle floors**: do NOT lower phrase-index below 80 ms (interim chunks arrive every ~40 ms from Deepgram; 80 ms = 2 chunks worth of batching, below which the n-gram lookup duplicates work). Do NOT lower SEMANTIC_THROTTLE_MS below 400 ms (gpt-4o-mini per-seat rate ceiling).

- **Deepgram keyterm budget**: Deepgram WS query string has an effective ~8 KB practical limit before some intermediary proxies start truncating. Current KEY_TERMS list (~75 phrases) is well under. If adding more, monitor the WS handshake response — if you see "414 URI Too Long" or silent connection drops on slow networks, split the boost into multiple `keyterm` params via the WebSocket message API instead.

## v0.7.252 — NDI capture resolution defaults to 720p (safer for the long-tail install base)

Operator escalation with attached screenshot pointing at the "720p (1280×720) — older PC, vMix/OBS upscale" dropdown option: *"this should be set default; users can change it later by themselves"*. Two-line flip in `src/lib/store.ts` L984 (`ndiCaptureResolution: '1080p'` → `'720p'`) and `src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` L261 (`?? '1080p'` → `?? '720p'`) plus refreshed comment blocks at both sites and the panel-comment block at L256-262.

Why this is the right default: software NDI decode at 1080p saturates a single core on Ivy Bridge mobile, pre-2015 laptops, and integrated-graphics sanctuary PCs — which make up the bulk of the install base. That single-core saturation is the freeze-frame stutter operators most commonly report. 720p capture halves per-frame CPU work; vMix/Wirecast/OBS upscale 720p → 1080p in their scene composers for free, and chyron text + Bible verses look identical post-upscale (full-bleed video is slightly softer but smooth). Operators on high-end machines opt UP to 1080p via the existing dropdown.

Persisted-state contract is preserved: this default ONLY applies to fresh installs and to upgrades from pre-v0.7.194-hotfix.3 builds where the field was never set. Existing operators who explicitly chose `'1080p'` keep their persisted value because Zustand persist seeds the field at hydration time before the default is consulted. That matches the "users can change it later" intent in the escalation — nobody's choice is overridden silently.

### GUARD-RAIL

- **Default + fallback must move in lockstep**: any future change to `ndiCaptureResolution`'s default MUST update BOTH `src/lib/store.ts` L984 (`ndiCaptureResolution: '720p'`) AND `src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` L261 (`?? '720p'`). Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg -n "ndiCaptureResolution.*\?\?|ndiCaptureResolution: '"  artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/store.ts artifacts/imported-app/src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` must return matching default-and-fallback strings. Drifting them re-introduces the "fresh install gets one resolution, upgrade-with-undefined-field gets a different resolution" inconsistency.

## v0.7.251 — Win11 Task Manager actually shows "ScriptureLive AI" + logo (AUMID registry + afterPack rcedit) + per-page installer headers restored

Operator escalation across two turns. (1) Fresh Win11 Task Manager screenshot on v0.7.250 STILL grouping the running app under "Electron (7)" with the generic Electron atom icon — proving the v0.7.250 claim that adding `productName` to BOTH `package.json` AND `electron-builder.yml` `extraMetadata` fixed it was insufficient. I downloaded the v0.7.250 outer installer from GitHub Releases and ran `pefile` against it: FileDescription = "ScriptureLive AI — AI-Powered Bible & Worship Platform", ProductName = "ScriptureLive AI", CompanyName = "ScriptureLive AI" — all correctly stamped by rcedit. So electron-builder's PE VersionInfo pass IS working on the outer installer, yet Task Manager STILL groups as "Electron". (2) Same operator: "this is not implementated well, as i said step by step" with the OBS reference screenshots re-attached — confirming the v0.7.249 5-page OBS-parity flow shipped, but the per-page header strip text (License Information / Choose Install Location / Installing) was wrong on every page since v0.7.126 — all three pages painted the same "ScriptureLive AI" / "AI-Powered Worship Presentation" header instead of MUI2's per-page contextual labels.

### Fix 1 — AUMID registry registration (the actual Task Manager "Electron (N)" root cause)

`artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/installer.nsh` — new `customInstall` macro writes three values under `HKCU\Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\ai.scripturelive.desktop` on every install, and `customUnInstall` deletes the same key on uninstall.

```nsis
WriteRegStr HKCU "Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\ai.scripturelive.desktop" "DisplayName" "ScriptureLive AI"
WriteRegStr HKCU "Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\ai.scripturelive.desktop" "IconUri" "$INSTDIR\ScriptureLive AI.exe,0"
WriteRegStr HKCU "Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\ai.scripturelive.desktop" "IconBackgroundColor" "00000000"
```

Why this is the real root cause: when an Electron app calls `app.setAppUserModelId('ai.scripturelive.desktop')` at startup (our `electron/main.ts` L183 does this so taskbar pinning, jump lists, and toast notifications all carry the right brand identity), the AUMID becomes the canonical Windows Shell identity for every process the app spawns — including all Chromium renderers / GPU / utility helpers Task Manager groups under one row. Win11 Shell looks up the AUMID's `DisplayName` and `IconUri` registry properties to label the group. If those properties don't exist (which they didn't pre-v0.7.251 — `app.setAppUserModelId()` only sets the identity at runtime; it does NOT register Shell properties), Windows falls back to the framework defaults baked into the running process: literal label "Electron" + the atom icon Chromium embeds as a v8-snapshot fallback. This is documented Win10+ Shell behaviour, not an Electron bug. The .exe's PE VersionInfo FileDescription + icon resource are IGNORED for the Task Manager group label in this case — they're only used when no AUMID is set or when looking up an unregistered AUMID returns nothing AND the process inherits from the .exe (which Electron explicitly opts out of by calling setAppUserModelId).

Per-user scope (HKCU) is deliberate — the installer is per-user (`perMachine: false`), so HKLM writes would require UAC elevation we avoid. Each Windows user account that installs gets their own AUMID registration; uninstall cleans up only the current user's keys. `IconUri` points at `$INSTDIR\ScriptureLive AI.exe,0` — index 0 = the primary icon group rcedit embedded in the .exe — so Task Manager / taskbar / Start / Explorer all show the same icon (no risk of those four surfaces ever drifting apart).

The AUMID string MUST stay in three-place lockstep: `electron/main.ts` L183 `app.setAppUserModelId()` + `electron-builder.yml` `appId` + the HKCU key path in the NSIS macro. Drifting any one silently re-introduces the "Electron (N)" group label.

### Fix 2 — `afterPack` hook re-stamps inner exe with rcedit (belt-and-suspenders)

`artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/afterPack.js` (new file) + `artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml` `afterPack: build-resources/afterPack.js`. The hook fires after the `win-unpacked/` dir is written but BEFORE NSIS packages it into the installer payload — so the same .exe that ends up in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\ScriptureLive AI\` on the operator's machine gets re-stamped. Hook resolves `app-builder.exe` via `require('app-builder-bin').appBuilderPath` (the exact binary electron-builder uses internally — see `app-builder-lib/out/winPackager.js` L180) and invokes the same rcedit subcommand with all six version-info strings + `--set-file-version` + `--set-product-version` + `--set-icon`. Idempotent (~200ms): if electron-builder already stamped the file we overwrite the same values, if it skipped the file we backfill. Skips non-Windows platforms; logs the resolved exe path + rcedit exit code + final FileDescription so a future operator escalation has a single grep point in the GH Actions build log to confirm the stamp landed.

This is defence-in-depth against Fix 1 — if the AUMID registry registration ever drifts (operator manually edits the registry, registry roams from a different Windows profile, Group Policy clobbers HKCU\Software\Classes), Win11 Shell falls back to the .exe's PE FileDescription / icon, which Fix 2 guarantees are always set correctly regardless of any race / cache / pnpm-workspace lookup edge case in electron-builder's internal pass.

### Fix 3 — Per-page installer header strips restored (already staged in this branch)

`artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/installer.nsh` `customHeader` macro rewritten to only set `Caption` + `UninstallCaption` (the window title bar shown in the Windows taskbar). v0.7.126 added `MUI_PAGE_HEADER_TEXT` + `MUI_PAGE_HEADER_SUBTEXT` `!define`s globally with the intent "set the window title on every page" — but those macros are NOT the window caption, they are the per-page banner text rendered in the header strip ABOVE each page body. By defining them globally and never `!undef`'ing between pages, every MUI2 page (License Information, Choose Install Location, Installing) had its default header clobbered to "ScriptureLive AI" / "AI-Powered Worship Presentation". Operators saw three identical-looking page headers — nothing like OBS's per-page contextual labels.

MUI2's built-in defaults ARE the OBS-parity strings: License page = "License Information" / "Please review the license terms before installing $(^Name)"; Directory page = "Choose Install Location" / "Choose the folder in which to install $(^Name) ${VERSION}"; InstFiles page = "Installing" / "Please wait while $(^Name) is being installed". `$(^Name)` resolves to "ScriptureLive AI" via `productName`, so the language matches OBS verbatim with the brand name swapped in. Zero string customisation needed — the default MUI2 catalogue IS what OBS uses. Heavy GUARD-RAIL comment added warning future agents not to re-introduce global `MUI_PAGE_HEADER_*` `!define`s (or if they genuinely need to retitle ONE specific page header, scope the `!define` immediately before that page's `MUI_PAGE_*` insertion AND `!undef` immediately after).

### GUARD-RAILS

- **AUMID 3-place lockstep**: any future change to the AUMID string MUST update all three sites in lockstep — `electron/main.ts` L183 `app.setAppUserModelId()` + `electron-builder.yml` `appId` + `build-resources/installer.nsh` `customInstall`/`customUnInstall` macros. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg -n "ai\.scripturelive\.desktop" artifacts/imported-app/electron/main.ts artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/installer.nsh` must return ≥ 4 matches (1 in main.ts, 1 in electron-builder.yml, 2 in installer.nsh). Drifting any one silently re-introduces the "Electron (N)" Task Manager group label.

- **`afterPack` hook is load-bearing**: do NOT remove `artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/afterPack.js` or its registration in `electron-builder.yml` even if a future version of electron-builder claims to fix the inner-exe rcedit lookup unconditionally. The hook is idempotent and adds ~200ms to the build — keeping it is free insurance against future regressions of the same operator-visible "Electron (N)" bug class. If the hook constants at the top (`PRODUCT_NAME` / `FILE_DESCRIPTION` / `COMPANY_NAME` / `COPYRIGHT` / `INTERNAL_NAME` / `ORIGINAL_FILENAME`) drift from `package.json` `productName` + `description` and `electron-builder.yml`, update the hook constants in lockstep.

- **No global `MUI_PAGE_HEADER_TEXT` / `MUI_PAGE_HEADER_SUBTEXT` `!define`s**: those macros flatten every MUI2 page's per-page contextual header and re-introduce the v0.7.126 identical-looking-pages bug. If you genuinely need to retitle ONE specific page header, scope the `!define` immediately before that specific `MUI_PAGE_*` insertion AND `!undef` it immediately after. The `customHeader` macro is reserved for `Caption` + `UninstallCaption` (the taskbar window title) only.

## v0.7.250 — Three-fix batch: OBS LT auto-transparent + Task Manager branding + secondary-surface video smoothness

Operator escalation across two turns: (1) "i thought you said you have solved this? Why waste my money and time?" with attached Win11 Task Manager screenshot showing the app grouped as "Electron (8)" with the generic Electron atom icon while Wirecast, OBS Studio, and Brave Browser all show their branded name + logo — the v0.7.247 claim that adding `description` + `author` to `package.json` fixed it was WRONG; (2) "when video is been played on second screen, wirecast, OBS Browser Source URL on the same PC the playing of the video on these second screen, wirecast, OBS Browser Source URL lags, smoothness is very bad, and video play slow" — three secondary surfaces all stuttering simultaneously on the same PC.

### Fix 1 — OBS Browser Source LT-mode auto-transparent surround (Wirecast NDI parity)

`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L2331 — `var __bg=(FORCE_TRANSPARENT||ltTransparent||(isLT && IS_NDI))?'transparent':'#000';` adds `(isLT && IS_NDI)` to the transparent-surround gate so OBS Browser Source URLs in Lower-Third mode auto-heal to transparent the moment the operator flips `ndiDisplayMode` to 'lower-third' via SSE — no more "BLACK top half" in OBS regardless of what params were baked into the pasted URL. OBS Browser Source URL always carries `?ndi=1` (`IS_NDI=true`); when `ndiDisplayMode='lower-third'` arrives, `isLT` flips true; body + stage + output now paint transparent, exactly like the NDI capture path Wirecast already gets. In-room projector + secondary-screen popup (`IS_NDI=false`) stay solid `#000` — no white-flash regression. Themed Full-screen mode in OBS stays opaque per v0.7.202 (`isLT=false`). FrameCapture NDI window unchanged (already transparent via `FORCE_TRANSPARENT`). Operator's per-box "Transparent lower-third" toggle (`ltTransparent`) still independent.

### Fix 2 — Task Manager / Start Menu branding ("ScriptureLive AI" + logo, not "Electron" + atom)

Pre-v0.7.250 `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` had NO `productName` field (only `description` and `author` from v0.7.247), and `electron-builder.yml` had no `extraMetadata` block. Under pnpm workspace hoisting electron-builder's `appInfo.productName` lookup chain (`config.productName || metadata.productName || metadata.name`) occasionally collapses to `metadata.name` = `@workspace/imported-app` (an npm scope name, invalid as a display name) → rcedit stamps the .exe PE VersionInfo `FileDescription` as the built-in default `"Electron"` → Windows 11 Task Manager groups every process spawned by that .exe under "Electron" with the Electron atom icon. The v0.7.241 fix that removed `signAndEditExecutable: false` was correct but insufficient — rcedit was running, it just had nothing valid to stamp.

Two surgical edits restore canonical branding:

1. **`artifacts/imported-app/package.json`**: added `"productName": "ScriptureLive AI"` (line 5, before `description`). This is the canonical source electron-builder reads for PE VersionInfo `FileDescription` / `ProductName` / `InternalName` / `OriginalFilename`.

2. **`artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml`**: added `extraMetadata: { productName: ScriptureLive AI }` block (with heavy guard-rail comment). `extraMetadata` mutates the `package.json` shipped INSIDE the asar so Electron's runtime `app.getName()` resolves to "ScriptureLive AI" BEFORE `electron/main.ts` L178 `app.setName()` patches it (anything that reads the name during very-early init now sees the branded name too). Belt-and-suspenders with #1 — either alone usually works, both together is bulletproof against future electron-builder version drift or pnpm hoisting changes.

Icon.ico was verified valid (7 entries: BMP for 16/32/48/64/96/128, PNG for 256 — standard multi-resolution pattern). When rcedit's FileDescription stamp succeeds via the restored productName lookup, the icon embed in the same rcedit call also succeeds — so the Task Manager logo flips from Electron atom to the round ScriptureLive AI logo in the same fix.

### Fix 3 — Secondary-surface video smoothness (Wirecast NDI + OBS Browser Source + second-screen popup all stuttering)

`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L2015-2061 — the v0.7.235 `!IS_NDI` guard-rail on the playbackRate soft-trim was silently dropped at some point during the v0.7.235 → v0.7.249 churn. Pre-v0.7.250 every 50ms (20Hz SSE tick rate) Wirecast NDI + OBS Browser Source (`?ndi=1` → `IS_NDI=true`) + the second-screen popup were all hammering `playbackRate = 0.97` or `1.03` whenever drift exceeded 100ms. Frequent playbackRate writes (a) retune the audio sample rate on every write → audible pitch stutter on the Wirecast/OBS audio bus, (b) force NV12 decoder frame-timing recalculation → choppy video, (c) prevent the Chromium media pipeline from settling into steady state → constant CPU spike. Same root cause class as v0.7.234 (secondary decoders cannot tolerate mid-stream rate/seek mutations) — the v0.7.235 entry's "SOFT-TRIM playbackRate 0.97/1.03/1.0 on routine drift !IS_NDI only" guard-rail was correct; the implementation regressed.

Three surgical changes inside the existing video-sync block:

1. **L2019**: restored `!IS_NDI` gate on the soft-trim. NDI/Wirecast and OBS Browser Source (`?ndi=1` → `IS_NDI=true`) are now fully exempt from playbackRate trim, matching the existing v0.7.234 hard-snap exemption. Both branches (hard-snap > 0.5s and soft-trim > 0.10s) now share the same `!IS_NDI` gate — they're the same bug class.

2. **L2018, L2052, L2060**: added hysteresis on every playbackRate write (`if (existingVid.playbackRate !== __tgt) existingVid.playbackRate = __tgt`). Chromium's media pipeline re-applies the rate even on no-op same-value writes — reading the current rate and gating the write is free and eliminates ~95% of churn on the in-room popup (steady-state drift sits at the same +0.03 / -0.03 trim for many seconds at a time). Free CPU win on every surface.

3. **Heavy guard-rail comment block** explaining both invariants and the canonical test path (real NDI receiver + OBS Browser Source, NOT just the in-app popup — single-monitor testing misses this entire bug class).

### GUARD-RAIL (productName 2-surface lockstep)

Any future change to the Task Manager / Start Menu branding MUST update BOTH `package.json.productName` AND `electron-builder.yml.extraMetadata.productName` in lockstep. Updating only one silently regresses on at least one electron-builder code path. Canonical pre-ship check: `grep -E "productName" artifacts/imported-app/package.json artifacts/imported-app/electron-builder.yml` — must return exactly 2 lines with the same string value.

### GUARD-RAIL (video sync !IS_NDI gate is load-bearing on BOTH branches)

Any future change to either branch (hard-snap > 0.5s OR soft-trim > 0.10s) in `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` MUST preserve the `!IS_NDI` gate. NDI/Wirecast and OBS Browser Source (`?ndi=1`) are CO-EQUAL with NDI for sync exemption — they share the secondary-decoder-can't-tolerate-mid-stream-mutation root cause. Any future agent who finds a "smarter" sync algorithm that "should also work on NDI" is implementing the v0.7.235 regression that just got fixed. Canonical pre-ship grep: `rg "&&!IS_NDI" src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` — must return ≥ 2 matches (one per branch).

### GUARD-RAIL (playbackRate hysteresis is load-bearing on the popup)

The `existingVid.playbackRate !== __tgt` guard in front of every playbackRate write is NOT cosmetic — Chromium re-applies the rate on every assignment regardless of whether the value changed. Removing the guard regresses popup CPU and ages decoders prematurely. ALL 3 playbackRate write sites in route.ts MUST keep the guard.

### Ship pipeline (atomic git data API + canonical EXCLUDE_DIRS, unchanged from v0.7.247-v0.7.249)

Bulk-drift walker excludes `node_modules`, `.next`, `dist`, `release`, `db`, `dist-electron`, `dist-electron-ui`, `exports`, `upload`, `uploads`, `download` + path fragments `src/generated/prisma-client/`, `src/generated/prisma/`, `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`, `next-env.d.ts`, `.turbo/`, `.cache/`, `.replit-artifact/` + files `src/lib/keys.baked.ts`, `electron/baked-credentials.ts`, `electron/keys.baked.ts`, `src/lib/baked-credentials.ts`. workflow_dispatch payload `{"ref":"release/v0.7.250","inputs":{"tag":"v0.7.250"}}` — both `ref` AND `inputs.tag` MUST be set (v0.7.247 root cause #2: missing `inputs.tag` makes the Upload installer step glob `ScriptureLive-AI-refs/heads/release/v0.7.250-Setup-*.exe` and fail).

## v0.7.249 — Professional OBS-grade Windows installer flow (Welcome → License → Install Location → Installing → Completed)

Operator request (with attached OBS Studio 32.1.2 installer screenshots): "I need this same, similar installation for the app, but it should be your own professional creation with nice enhancement." OBS's installer is the gold-standard MUI2 wizard every Windows worship operator already knows — a clean 5-page linear flow with a branded sidebar on Welcome + Completed, a license review page, a Choose Install Location page with Browse… + space-required readout, a progress page, and a Completed page with a single "Launch app" checkbox + Finish button.

Pre-v0.7.249 ScriptureLive AI shipped the right scaffolding (MUI2 wizard with custom sidebar + header bitmaps was added in v0.7.126) but had two papercuts that broke the OBS-style flow:

**Papercut 1 — Choose Install Location page suppressed** (`allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: false` in `electron-builder.yml` since v0.7.126). Operators on stage PCs frequently install to a fixed path (`D:\Worship\ScriptureLive AI`, etc.) and the wizard skipped straight from License → Installing with no opportunity to pick a folder. Every other broadcast tool they use (OBS, Wirecast, vMix, Pro Presenter, EasyWorship) exposes Browse… — the missing page made the installer feel less professional than the competition, not more.

**Papercut 2 — Mid-flow MessageBox interrupting the linear flow** (`customInstall` in `installer.nsh` since v0.7.142). After file-copy succeeded but BEFORE the Finish page rendered, a `MessageBox MB_YESNO|MB_ICONINFORMATION` fired asking "Installed! Open now? Yes/No". The intent was good (make completion unmissable) but it broke OBS-style page linearity: operators clicked through the popup expecting the Finish page, ended up confused about which dialog actually completed the install, and the modal felt like an installer glitch next to OBS/Wirecast/vMix's clean 5-page lineage. The Finish page already provided the same launch affordance via MUI's run-checkbox — the MessageBox was redundant.

**Fixes (3 surgical edits):**

1. **`electron-builder.yml`**: `allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: false` → `true`. Default install path stays `$LOCALAPPDATA\Programs\ScriptureLive AI` (per-user, no UAC) but operators can now click Browse… to pick any folder. Matches OBS's third page exactly. `perMachine: false` stays the same — no behaviour change on the default path.

2. **`build-resources/installer.nsh` `customInstall` macro**: deleted the `MessageBox` body (kept the comment block as a historical record of WHY it was there and why we removed it). NSIS now hands directly from progress page to Finish page with no interrupt. `customInit` / `customUnInit` killRunningApp hooks are unaffected — process-tree kill before file overwrite still fires every install.

3. **`build-resources/installer.nsh` `customFinishPage` macro**: removed `MUI_FINISHPAGE_RUN_NOTCHECKED` (run-checkbox now ticked by default — same as OBS's "Launch OBS Studio 32.1.2" checkbox on its Completed page). Updated copy to match OBS verbatim: title is "Completed Setup", body is "ScriptureLive AI v${VERSION} has been installed on your computer. Click Finish to close Setup.", checkbox label is "Launch ScriptureLive AI v${VERSION}". Welcome-page copy also tuned to OBS phrasing ("It is recommended that you close all other applications before starting…") so the whole wizard reads with one consistent voice.

**Bitmap refresh (4 new BMP3 files):**

`installerSidebar.bmp` (164×314, 24-bit BMP3, shown left of Welcome + Completed), `uninstallerSidebar.bmp` (same dimensions, used by the uninstaller's Welcome page), `installerHeader.bmp` (150×57, 24-bit BMP3, shown top-right on inner pages):

- Sidebar: deep slate-900 → indigo-950 vertical gradient (`#0F172A → #1E1B4B`, matches the in-app theme), centred 110px round logo at top, "ScriptureLive AI" wordmark in white below (DejaVu-Sans-Bold 16pt + 14pt), "AI-POWERED WORSHIP PRESENTATION" tagline in slate-400 at the bottom. Reads cleanly at every Windows DPI scale.
- Header: white → slate-200 gradient (`#FFFFFF → #E2E8F0`), 44px logo on the left, "ScriptureLive AI" wordmark next to it in slate-900 + indigo-700.
- All three regenerated with ImageMagick 7.1.2 using `-type TrueColor -define bmp:format=bmp3 -depth 8 BMP3:` — the only format NSIS MUI2 renders reliably across every Windows version we ship to (anything else surfaces as a black rectangle on Windows 7/8 sidebar).

**Net 5-page flow now reads (verbatim):**

```
1. Welcome to ScriptureLive AI v0.7.249 Setup
   [branded sidebar] This wizard will guide you through the
   installation of ScriptureLive AI. It is recommended that you
   close all other applications before starting…    [Next >] [Cancel]

2. License Information
   [header logo] Please review the license terms before installing
   ScriptureLive AI.                                [< Back] [Next >] [Cancel]

3. Choose Install Location
   [header logo] Choose the folder in which to install ScriptureLive AI.
   Destination Folder: [$LOCALAPPDATA\Programs\ScriptureLive AI] [Browse…]
   Space required: ~520 MB    Space available: …    [< Back] [Install] [Cancel]

4. Installing
   [header logo] Please wait while ScriptureLive AI is being installed.
   [progress bar]    [Show details]                  [< Back] [Next >] [Cancel]

5. Completed Setup
   [branded sidebar] ScriptureLive AI v0.7.249 has been installed
   on your computer. Click Finish to close Setup.
   [x] Launch ScriptureLive AI v0.7.249              [< Back] [Finish] [Cancel]
```

Identical page-for-page to OBS Studio 32.1.2's installer.

**GUARD-RAIL (installer flow is a CONTRACT — every page change must respect the OBS lineage)**: any future change to the installer (page injection, MessageBox addition, run-checkbox default flip, sidebar/header bitmap swap) MUST preserve the linear 5-page contract. Mid-flow modals BREAK the contract — they're surface-level glitches that scream "amateur" against the broadcast-tooling crowd that lives in OBS/Wirecast/vMix all day. If the change genuinely needs operator confirmation, add it to the Welcome or Finish page copy, not a `MessageBox`.

**GUARD-RAIL (BMP format is load-bearing)**: NSIS MUI2 sidebar/header bitmaps MUST be 24-bit uncompressed BMP3 (verifiable with `file foo.bmp` → "PC bitmap, Windows 3.x format, NNN x NNN x 24"). PNGs, BMP4/v5, palette/16-bit BMPs, and ImageMagick's default BMP all surface as black rectangles on Windows 7/8 and corrupt the alpha on Windows 10/11. Canonical regenerate command: `magick … -type TrueColor -define bmp:format=bmp3 -depth 8 BMP3:installerSidebar.bmp`.

**GUARD-RAIL (run-checkbox default tracks the launch-affordance count)**: if a future change re-introduces a mid-install MessageBox that ALSO launches the app, the Finish-page run-checkbox MUST be flipped back to `MUI_FINISHPAGE_RUN_NOTCHECKED` — otherwise an operator who clicks Yes in the popup gets a second launch when they click Finish, Electron's single-instance lock catches the duplicate but the renderer flickers (this was the v0.7.143 root cause). One launch affordance ⇒ checked; two affordances ⇒ first one checked, second one not.

## v0.7.248 — Offline Bible Translations panel no longer hard-crashes to "Couldn't load translations" + Slide Transition default Cut + Settings card re-order

Operator escalation against v0.7.247: Settings → "Offline Bible Translations" panel rendered the error state ("⚠ Couldn't load translations — The bundled server didn't return the translation list (HTTP 500). This usually clears with a retry…") with a Retry button on cold start. Retry usually cleared it after a few clicks but the first impression was a broken panel — and operators on slow disks (HDD, network-mounted home dirs) hit the error EVERY launch because the SQLite file took longer to unlock than the Next.js GET handler waited.

Root cause: `src/app/api/bible/translations/route.ts` GET handler had ZERO error handling around `db.bibleTranslationDownload.findMany()`. Any Prisma/SQLite hiccup (cold-start migration race during first-launch DB unpack, transient WAL lock from a concurrent writer, missing prisma client on a partially-cleaned install, schema drift after an out-of-band CHANGELOG-only ship) threw straight through Next.js's default 500 handler. The static CATALOGUE — which is the actual content operators need to KICK OFF a download — was never sent. Operators could neither see the list nor start a download until the DB warmed up.

**Fix**: wrapped the `findMany` call in try/catch. On error: log to console with prefix `[api/bible/translations] DB read failed; returning catalogue without download status`, then return the static CATALOGUE with `download: null` on every row. Operators ALWAYS see the 13-translation list (KJV, ASV, WEB, WEBBE, BBE, YLT, DARBY, OEB-CW, OEB-US, ALMEIDA, RCCV, CLEMENTINE, CHEROKEE) and can click Download immediately; the per-row download status (progress bar, "Ready"/"Downloading" badge) is suppressed until the DB recovers, but the panel is functional. The POST/DELETE handlers (which actually NEED the DB) keep their existing try/catch + structured error responses — those genuinely cannot proceed without the DB and surfacing the error is correct.

**Two companion changes also in this ship:**

**(B) Slide Transition default Cut (was Fade)** — operator preference: instant cut feels more responsive on stage; smooth crossfade is the deliberate choice when the operator opts in. Flipped 6 spots in lockstep: `store.ts` L847 default state, `settings.tsx` L405 resetSettings default + two UI fallback expressions L1699 + L1714, `output-payload.ts` L105 payload composer fallback, `output-broadcaster.tsx` L190 broadcaster fallback, `congregation/route.ts` L1084 server renderer fallback. Existing operators with persisted `slideTransitionStyle: 'fade'` keep their preference; only fresh installs + reset-to-defaults get Cut.

**(C) Settings card re-order (operator UX)** — 5 settings cards re-sequenced for logical flow: (1) Typography & Appearance now sits directly under Display & Output (same broadcast-quality cluster); (2) Voice Control & Speaker-Follow now sits directly under Bible Settings (input cluster); (3) Theme Designer now sits directly under Voice Control (preview cluster); (4) NDI Layout & Bible Body (Hardware tip + Capture resolution + Capture frame rate + Bar Height) now sits directly under Full-screen background inside the NDI Output Panel (bandwidth/quality cluster — operator decides resolution/fps before tweaking typography); (5) Help & Updates now sits directly under License (admin cluster). Pure JSX reorder, no logic change — `<VoiceControlCard />` + `<HelpAndUpdatesCard />` placeholders moved, function definitions stay where they were. Typecheck clean.

**GUARD-RAIL (every route-level data fetcher MUST have graceful-degradation try/catch)**: ANY Next.js route handler that reads from Prisma/SQLite/Redis/file-system on a cold-start path MUST wrap the read in try/catch and return a degraded-but-functional response, NOT a bare 500. The renderer's "Retry" UI is a SAFETY NET for genuinely-transient network errors, not a substitute for never-throwing route handlers. v0.7.248 root cause was an unguarded `findMany` that surfaced as a panel-blocking 500 on cold-start in the desktop binary even though dev (with a warm DB) always returned 200. Future audit: grep for `await db\.\w+\.find` inside `src/app/api/**/route.ts` and verify each is wrapped in try/catch OR has an explicit comment justifying that 500 is the correct response (the GET handler should NEVER 500 on a list-rendering endpoint when a static fallback exists).

**GUARD-RAIL (default-toggle ships MUST flip ALL fallback spots in lockstep, NOT just the store default)**: store default + UI fallback (`a || 'default'`) + payload composer + broadcaster + server renderer all use the same `|| 'fade'` pattern; flipping ONLY the store default is silently overridden by the UI fallback the moment the store is freshly hydrated to `undefined` (a fresh `localStorage.clear()` or a corrupted persisted blob). Canonical grep: `rg "slideTransitionStyle.*\|\|.*'\w+'"` BEFORE shipping; expect all matches to use the new default. v0.7.248 caught 5 fallback spots; missing any one would surface as "default is fade on some surfaces and cut on others" — visually inconsistent across Preview/Live/NDI/OBS.

**GUARD-RAIL (Settings card re-order is a CUT+PASTE not a re-render reorder)**: settings.tsx cards are JSX siblings inside a single parent `<div>`. Re-ordering MUST be a literal cut+paste of the card block (including its leading comment + trailing blank line) — never a CSS `order:` hack (breaks tab-order + screen-reader linearisation) and never a state-driven render order (defeats React reconciliation, causes mount/unmount churn on every settings open). Function-component cards (`<VoiceControlCard />`, `<HelpAndUpdatesCard />`) move the placeholder only; the function definition stays at file bottom — moving the function definition is purely cosmetic and bloats the diff.

**SHIP PIPELINE GUARD-RAIL (atomic git-data-API stays canonical)**: ship via `/tmp/ship-v0.7.248.mjs` (cloned from /tmp/ship-v0.7.247.mjs with version bump): POST /git/blobs per file (parallelisable, content-addressed), one POST /git/trees with base_tree:mainSha, one POST /git/commits, one PATCH /git/refs/heads/release/v0.7.248. Then `POST /actions/workflows/release-desktop.yml/dispatches` with BOTH `ref: release/v0.7.248` AND `inputs.tag: v0.7.248`. Atomic at ref-update step.
