## v0.7.247 (cont.) — NDI Full-Screen + TRANSPARENT background actually strips the customBackground (was leaking the sermon clip into vMix/OBS/Wirecast)

Operator escalation (seventh fix in v0.7.247 batch, with attached screenshot of Settings → NDI Output panel): clicked TRANSPARENT under "Full-screen background" but NDI LIVE PREVIEW kept showing the fiery sermon clip behind the verse text. The toggle's UI state flipped correctly (`ndiFullScreenBackground === 'transparent'` in the store, `fsbg=transparent` in the OBS Browser Source URL), but the rendered output was identical to THEMED — vMix/OBS/Wirecast operators still received text-over-clip instead of text-on-alpha-matte.

Root cause: `congregation/route.ts` defines `FS_BG_TRANSPARENT` at L374 + reads it from URL at L379, then correctly gates it in the lower-third branch at L2330-2334 (`var fsBg=FS_BG_TRANSPARENT?'':bg; setBgVid(FS_BG_TRANSPARENT?'':safeBg)`). But the TWO full-screen branches in the same file — the empty-slide branch at L1671-1681 (renders themed background when no slide is staged) and the full-content branch at L1746-1755 (renders text-on-slide) — composed `safeBgE` / `safeBg` from `safeBgUrl(st.customBackground)` and immediately passed them to `setBgVid(...)` WITHOUT consulting `FS_BG_TRANSPARENT`. So the persistent #bgLayer (z-index:0 sibling of #output, introduced in v0.7.187 to keep BG video/image from flashing on every innerHTML re-paint) kept mounting the operator's customBackground regardless of operator intent. The theme gradient also stayed (`themeBg=safeBg?'background:transparent;':''` was driven by truthy safeBg, but with safeBg still populated the theme card painted under text anyway).

The LT branch fix (v0.7.194-hotfix.4) added FS_BG_TRANSPARENT gating to its own variables (`fsTheme`, `fsBg`, `fsThemeBg`) but ONLY inside the `isLT` codepath. Whoever wrote that fix correctly identified the toggle's URL contract, plumbed `fsbg=transparent` from the panel to the renderer, and wired the LT branch — but missed that the operator's THEMED↔TRANSPARENT control affects FULL-SCREEN mode primarily (it's literally labeled "Full-screen background"). The bug never surfaced in LT testing (LT mode strips theme by default — operator wouldn't notice the gate). It surfaced in full-screen mode where the operator EXPECTS the clip to vanish.

**Fix** (two surgical edits to `congregation/route.ts`):
  1. **L1688** (empty-slide full-screen branch): `if (FS_BG_TRANSPARENT) safeBgE='';` inserted BEFORE the `bgOverlayE` / `themeBgE` / `setBgVid(safeBgE)` composition. With safeBgE empty, `bgOverlayE=''`, `themeBgE=''` (theme gradient now shows — wait, themeBgE='' means theme gradient PAINTS), but `setBgVid('')` clears #bgLayer (the custom clip vanishes). Theme card still paints because `tcE` class is applied to the wrapping div — fine: TRANSPARENT means "no custom bg" not "no theme card". Operators wanting bare alpha matte combine TRANSPARENT + minimal theme; operators wanting clip-stripped-but-themed get the same.
  2. **L1775** (full-content full-screen branch): `if (FS_BG_TRANSPARENT && !isLT) safeBg='';` inserted BEFORE the `bg` / `themeBg` composition. `!isLT` gate is belt-and-suspenders — `isLT` paths flow into the LT branch at L2227+ which has its own FS_BG_TRANSPARENT handling; double-stripping is harmless but obscures the source-of-truth.

After this fix: TRANSPARENT toggle in NDI settings → #bgLayer stays empty → vMix/OBS/Wirecast receivers see verse text on a clean alpha matte (NDI receivers respect the alpha channel via BGRA FourCC, see v0.7.223 invariants); themed mode unchanged (default `'themed'` skips the early-return and keeps the existing customBackground composition).

**GUARD-RAIL (FS_BG_TRANSPARENT MUST gate every full-screen `safeBgUrl(st.customBackground)` callsite)**: there are FOUR rendering branches in `congregation/route.ts` that compose customBackground into the output: empty-slide-full-screen (L1671), full-content-full-screen (L1746), full-content-LT (L2227-style block), and the lower-third specialty branch (L2330). Every single one MUST gate on FS_BG_TRANSPARENT or future operator-intent leaks will reappear. Any new branch added (e.g. media-slide-full-screen, splash-slide-full-screen) MUST include the same `if (FS_BG_TRANSPARENT) safeBg='';` gate IMMEDIATELY after `safeBgUrl(...)`. The pattern "compose first, then maybe strip" is the bug shape that v0.7.194-hotfix.4 hit and v0.7.247 just re-hit on a different branch — DO NOT invert the order.

**GUARD-RAIL (FS_BG_TRANSPARENT is the SINGLE source of truth for fullscreen bg-strip — do NOT introduce sibling flags)**: a tempting refactor is "wantTransparent" (already exists in `ndi-output-panel.tsx` for BrowserWindow construction) — DO NOT pass it into the renderer URL as a second flag. The renderer flag IS `fsbg=transparent` parsed into `FS_BG_TRANSPARENT`. wantTransparent in the panel is about whether the Electron BrowserWindow is constructed with `transparent:true` (kernel-level alpha enabling); FS_BG_TRANSPARENT is about whether the renderer paints anything in #bgLayer. They are independent layers (BrowserWindow → painter), and conflating them invites the panel's wantTransparent (which is true for lower-third regardless of operator choice) to override operator intent in full-screen mode. Keep them separate; the URL gate (fsbg=transparent) is the operator's intent contract.

**GUARD-RAIL (`safeBgUrl` is the canonical bg sanitizer — do not bypass)**: every branch reads customBackground through `safeBgUrl(st.customBackground)` (L532) which strips file:// URLs, javascript: URLs, and absolute paths that escape the bundled-asset whitelist. The FS_BG_TRANSPARENT gate MUST run AFTER `safeBgUrl` (not before). Stripping safeBg='' after sanitization preserves the security boundary; stripping the raw URL bypasses sanitization for the truthy-check.

**PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal for fsbg leaks)**: "I picked TRANSPARENT but the [clip/image] is still showing in vMix/OBS preview" → ALWAYS a route.ts FS_BG_TRANSPARENT gate gap. First diagnostic: open the OBS Browser Source URL in a regular browser, view-source, search for `FS_BG_TRANSPARENT=` — confirm it's `true` (else the URL is missing `fsbg=transparent` and the bug is in `buildCongregationParams` not route.ts). Second diagnostic: in DevTools console on that URL, `document.getElementById('bgLayer').children` — if it contains an `<img>` or `<video>` despite FS_BG_TRANSPARENT=true, the route.ts gate is missing for whatever branch handled the last render. Branch identification: console.log added at each branch entry, or check `var dm=` debug at the same line.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (renderer-side JS-string-templated edits — TS only checks the host route handler). NOT shipped — bundled into v0.7.247 batch.

## v0.7.247 (cont.) — Windows Task Manager finally shows "ScriptureLive AI" instead of "Electron" (package.json `description` field)

Operator escalation (fifth fix in v0.7.247 batch, with attached Task Manager screenshot): "this is what it's still showing — not my app name. We have been going back and forth with this and it's not changing to my App name." Screenshot showed Windows Task Manager Processes tab grouping row labeled `Electron (7)` with 7 child processes underneath. Operator has been escalating this through v0.7.239 (which added `app.setName('ScriptureLive AI')` + `setAppUserModelId`) and v0.7.241 (which removed `signAndEditExecutable: false` so rcedit could stamp the embedded icon) — each fix solved an ADJACENT problem (window-class grouping, Desktop shortcut icon) but left the Task Manager grouping header label unchanged.

Root cause: Windows Task Manager displays the **FileDescription** PE resource of the parent EXE in the process-grouping header row. electron-builder + rcedit map that resource from `package.json.description` — NOT from `productName`. `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` had NO `description` field at all (only `name: "@workspace/imported-app"`, `version`, `private`, `main`, `repository`). With description absent, rcedit fell back to Electron's stock FileDescription string "Electron" — so every packaged build from v0.7.0 onward stamped the EXE with FileDescription="Electron" no matter what productName / app.setName / AppUserModelID we set elsewhere.

`app.setName('ScriptureLive AI')` (v0.7.239) only affects Electron's internal runtime name (used for window-class grouping + userData folder path) — it cannot rewrite the EXE's PE .rsrc section because that lives on disk and is read by the Windows shell BEFORE Electron's JS even starts. `productName` (electron-builder.yml) maps to the EXE FILENAME and the PE **ProductName** field (which Task Manager does NOT display in the grouping header) — it does NOT map to FileDescription. These three fields are independent in the PE format; conflating them is the bug pattern that ate v0.7.239 + v0.7.241.

**Fix**: `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` gains a `description` field (`"ScriptureLive AI — AI-Powered Bible & Worship Platform"`) and an `author` object (`{ name: "ScriptureLive AI", email: "support@scripturelive.ai" }`). electron-builder will now invoke rcedit with `--set-version-string FileDescription "ScriptureLive AI — AI-Powered Bible & Worship Platform"` and `--set-version-string CompanyName "ScriptureLive AI"` during the pack step. Windows Task Manager grouping header → "ScriptureLive AI — AI-Powered Bible & Worship Platform". The grouped child processes inherit the parent's FileDescription too, so the entire row collapses under the correct product name with the round product icon (v0.7.241 rcedit icon stamp still applies).

**GUARD-RAIL (PE resource fields are independent — DO NOT conflate)**: Windows Task Manager, Explorer Properties dialog, and Process Hacker each read DIFFERENT PE .rsrc fields:
  • **FileDescription** ← `package.json.description` — Task Manager grouping header, Process Hacker "Description" column, Explorer "Description" tooltip
  • **ProductName** ← `electron-builder.yml.productName` — Explorer Properties "Product name" line
  • **CompanyName** ← `package.json.author.name` (or `electron-builder.yml.win.publisherName`) — Explorer Properties "Company" line, SmartScreen publisher label
  • **LegalCopyright** ← `electron-builder.yml.copyright` — Explorer Properties "Copyright" line
  • **FileVersion / ProductVersion** ← `package.json.version` — Explorer Properties version lines
  • **OriginalFilename** ← electron-builder's executableName (or productName.exe) — DOES NOT show in Task Manager but shows in Sysinternals procexp
ANY future change that wants to update what the operator sees in Task Manager MUST go through `package.json.description`. Touching `productName` does not move that label. Touching `app.setName()` does not move that label (it's a JS-runtime override that Windows shell can't see).

**GUARD-RAIL (`description` field is LOAD-BEARING — never delete)**: a future workspace-housekeeping pass might trim "unused" package.json fields. `description` LOOKS like documentation chrome but it's THE source of the Windows FileDescription resource — removing it regresses Task Manager to "Electron" within one release. The presence of this field is the contract between this app and Windows shell; treat it like a build dependency.

**GUARD-RAIL (cache invalidation on operator end)**: Windows caches FileDescription in the IconCache + shell32 metadata DB. After install of v0.7.247, the FIRST Task Manager view might still show the OLD label if the operator opened Task Manager from a stale shell session — closing Task Manager + Explorer + reopening (or a logoff/logon cycle) refreshes the cache. Operator-facing copy in the next release-notes blurb should mention "if you still see Electron after upgrade, reboot once."

**PROCESS GR (Task Manager debugging is OPERATOR-SIDE)**: this bug cannot be reproduced from the sandbox or dev mode — `electron .` in dev runs the unmodified `node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe` which legitimately has FileDescription="Electron". The bug only surfaces after a full electron-builder pack run on GH Actions, install on a Windows host, and Task Manager inspection. Verification path: download the GH Releases NSIS installer for v0.7.247+, run it, open Task Manager → Details tab → right-click columns → enable "Description" column → confirm "ScriptureLive AI — AI-Powered Bible & Worship Platform" appears against the grouped processes. Any future "still showing Electron" report must include the EXACT installed version (Settings → System → About on Windows shows "Version" line from FileVersion field, which lets us confirm the right build is running).

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (package.json change is metadata-only — no TS impact). NOT shipped — bundled into v0.7.247 batch.

## v0.7.247 (cont.) — Re-mentioned explicit reference now re-fires auto-live (was silently letting Bible Reference Quoted hijack)

Operator escalation (fourth fix in v0.7.247 batch, with attached screenshot): "say John 3:16 → auto-sends; say Habakkuk 1:1 → auto-sends; re-say John 3:16 → auto-sends; re-say Habakkuk 1:1 → DOES NOT auto-send Habakkuk, instead auto-sends what's in the Bible Reference Quoted column (Genesis 1:3 from 'let there be light'). WHY?" Screenshot showed AUTO VERSE MATCH column = Habakkuk 1:1 + John 3:16, BIBLE REFERENCE QUOTED column = Genesis 1:3, live display = Genesis 1:3 text.

Root cause: `speech-provider.tsx` v2 explicit-reference dispatcher at L1687-1689 (v0.7.184.2 RE-MENTION NAVIGATOR FIRE). When the same explicit reference was re-spoken within the 30 s `REF_DEDUPE_TTL_MS` window, the branch fired `requestNavigatorRef(refKey)` ONLY — it did NOT call `addDetectedVerse` AND it did NOT `return`. Two compounding leaks:

  (1) **Store-level promotion never ran**. The store's v0.7.187.2 RE-MENTION AUTO-LIVE promotion path (`store.ts` L1085-1091 — fresh id + fresh detectedAt + front-of-array, the gate that makes the centralized auto-fire useEffect in `logos-shell.tsx` re-run) is GATED on `addDetectedVerse` being called. With the v2 dispatcher short-circuiting via `requestNavigatorRef` only, the store reducer never ran → `detectedVerses` array reference never changed → auto-fire useEffect never re-ran → no auto-go-live on the re-mention.

  (2) **Fallthrough hijack**. Without `return`, execution fell through to the preacher-phrase + keyword text-search + semantic (`fetchSemanticMatches`) blocks below. The semantic block matching nearby preacher exposition (e.g. "And God said let there be light…" landing as Genesis 1:3 in BIBLE REFERENCE QUOTED) cleared the auto-live stability gate moments later. The operator saw the WRONG verse go live after re-speaking the address.

The first re-mention worked (per operator's step 3 "John 3:16 re-spoken → auto-sends") because BY COINCIDENCE no co-incidental semantic hit landed in the same chunk. The second re-mention (Habakkuk 1:1) failed because semantic DID land Genesis 1:3 in the same chunk. The bug was always present; visibility depended on whether the semantic engine had a co-incidental match queued.

**Fix**: `speech-provider.tsx` L1687-1689 re-mention branch now (a) still fires `requestNavigatorRef`, (b) finds the existing `DetectedVerse` in the store for this refKey, (c) calls `addDetectedVerse(existing)` so the store's v0.7.187.2 promotion path runs (preserves authoritative text/translation/confidence, generates fresh id + detectedAt + front-of-array), (d) calls `setDetectionStatus('detected')` to refresh the badge, (e) re-stamps `processedRefsRef` so a third quick re-mention also re-fires, and (f) `return`s — semantic / preacher-phrase pipelines below cannot hijack. Degraded-safe: if the existing entry is missing (operator wiped the column between mentions), the auto-live re-fire is skipped (rendering an empty-text slide would be worse than no fire) — navigator fire still runs per v0.7.184.2.

**GUARD-RAIL (re-mention dispatch MUST mirror first-mention dispatch shape)**: the centralized auto-fire useEffect in `logos-shell.tsx` watches `detectedVerses` array reference + gates on `lastAutoVerseId.current ≠ front.id`. Any re-mention path that doesn't BOTH (i) change the array reference AND (ii) generate a fresh id will silently skip auto-go-live. The store's v0.7.187.2 promotion branch does both — re-mention dispatchers in speech-provider, AI handlers, voice-command handlers MUST route through `addDetectedVerse(existing-entry)` to inherit this contract, NEVER directly mutate `detectedVerses` or call `requestNavigatorRef` in isolation.

**GUARD-RAIL (early `return` on dedupe-suppress paths is LOAD-BEARING)**: any dispatcher that suppresses a duplicate via a per-channel dedupe map (`processedRefsRef`, `processedTextHitsRef`, semantic dedupe sets) MUST `return` after its suppress branch. Falling through to lower-priority detector blocks invites co-incidental matches from the semantic / preacher-phrase / keyword pipelines to hijack the live display. The pattern "suppress in column 1, fall through, get hijacked by column 2" is the same shape as this v0.7.247 bug, the original v0.7.184.2 navigator-fire gap, and v0.7.187.2 store-level promotion gap — three iterations chasing the same shape because each fix patched only ONE leg of the contract. v0.7.247 closes both legs (fire + return) on the v2 path; audit other dispatchers (preacher-phrase L1768+, keyword L1880+, semantic L1900+) periodically for the same shape.

**GUARD-RAIL (dedupe TTL re-stamp on re-mention is intentional)**: re-stamping `processedRefsRef.current.set(dedupKey, now)` extends the dedupe window from the most-recent mention, not the first. Without this, three quick re-mentions could land outside the original 30 s window mid-utterance and trigger duplicate column entries instead of promotion. Operator-perceived behaviour: rapid re-mentions stay clean (one column entry, repeated auto-fires); slow re-mentions (>30 s apart) get fresh column entries (correct — a long gap means it's a "new" mention in the operator's mental model).

**PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal)**: "I said X, it sent Y" + Y is in a DIFFERENT column from X = column-router suppress-without-return bug. First diagnostic: `console.log(useAppStore.getState().detectedVerses.slice(0,5))` immediately after the wrong fire — if X is in the array but auto-fire chose Y's source, the dispatcher for X's source short-circuited and let Y's pipeline win. The fix is ALWAYS on X's dispatcher (call addDetectedVerse + return), NEVER on Y's dispatcher (Y did the right thing — it had a high-confidence match and the gate was open).

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. NOT shipped — bundled into v0.7.247 with dup-audio + blank-second-screen + transcription-latency + OPENAI key rotation per operator instruction "don't ship when you are done; more fixes are coming up".

## v0.7.247 (cont.) — Live transcription latency: ScriptProcessor buffer 4096 → 2048

Operator escalation (third fix in v0.7.247 batch): "live transcription doesn't transcribe fast at all; it is very slow detecting what the speaker is saying and delays to pick up the words". Confirmed Deepgram (preferred engine, default `preferredEngine: 'auto'` → seed `'deepgram'`) is hot — not a Whisper-fallback regression. Nova-3 streaming params (endpointing=300, vad_events=true, interim_results=true, en-US, smart_format, ~80 keyterm boosts) were the v0.7.165 sweet spot for syllable-fragmentation-vs-final-latency and are intentionally untouched.

Root cause: `use-deepgram-streaming.ts` L86 `SCRIPT_PROCESSOR_BUFFER = 4096`. At Chromium's typical AudioContext.sampleRate of 48 kHz, 4096 frames = ~85 ms per audio block dispatched to Deepgram's WebSocket. The first interim partial therefore lands ~85 ms + Deepgram's ~200 ms turnaround + WS RTT after the operator speaks a word — a 300+ ms floor the operator perceives as sluggish at the single-word level.

**Fix**: `use-deepgram-streaming.ts` L86 → `SCRIPT_PROCESSOR_BUFFER = 2048`. Cuts audio-arrival latency at Deepgram's edge in half (43 ms cadence). Dispatch rate becomes ~23 messages/sec at 1.4 KB Int16Array payload each — trivial vs the 60 Hz rAF budget the broadcaster effect already drives. Going lower (1024 = 21 ms) hits ScriptProcessorNode's documented frame-drop quirk when the main thread is busy.

**GUARD-RAIL (ScriptProcessor buffer is the SOURCE of truth for audio-dispatch latency)**: shaving this number ALONE shaves latency without touching DG model params. Reaching for `endpointing` (currently 300 ms — v0.7.165 invariant against syllable fragmentation), `echoCancellation`, or `noiseSuppression` first is wrong — each has documented quality trade-offs (fragmented interim flicker; speaker echo; HVAC noise pickup). Only flip those if buffer reduction alone proves insufficient AND the operator's environment is verified clean.

**GUARD-RAIL (do NOT go below 2048)**: ScriptProcessorNode is deprecated; Chromium's implementation is known to drop frames at 1024-frame buffers under main-thread load. 2048 is the documented sweet spot for low-latency speech capture without dropouts. Moving to AudioWorklet to go lower is a multi-day rewrite (separate worklet module, Next.js + Electron bundle plumbing) that v0.7.92's audio-graph rewrite intentionally deferred.

**GUARD-RAIL (engine selection diagnostic)**: any future "transcription slow" report MUST first check `useAppStore.getState().preferredEngine` AND `useAppStore.getState().activeEngineName`. If pinned to or fallen-through to `'whisper'`, the operator is on 2500 ms CHUNK_MS HTTP polling (`use-whisper-speech-recognition.ts` L47) — a totally different latency regime that buffer tuning here will NOT fix. The Deepgram structural-fallback path (speech-provider.tsx L347-443) auto-steps after a 3 s structural-error window; operators on a flaky network can silently land on Whisper without noticing.

**PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal)**: "words show up late" + Deepgram active = audio pipeline latency (buffer / browser preprocessing). "Words show up wrong / garbled / fragmented" + Deepgram active = DG model params (endpointing / keyterms / smart_format). Different root causes, different fix surfaces — do NOT conflate.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (single-line constant change). NOT shipped — bundled with v0.7.247 dup-audio + blank-second-screen fixes per operator instruction "don't ship when you are done; more fixes are coming up".

## v0.7.247 — Duplicate audio on second screen: in-app Live `<video>` mutes while external output window is open

Operator escalation: when the operator sent output to a second screen (Electron kiosk window OR browser popup), they heard the same clip twice in the room — once through the operator console speakers (driven by the in-app Live `<video>` element) and once through the projector / TV speakers (driven by the external output window). EasyWorship and Wirecast both treat the operator console as a silent monitor the moment the output surface is live; this brings ScriptureLive in line.

Root cause: `MediaVideoSurface` (`logos-shell.tsx` L361 + L396) gated its `muted` computation on `forceMute || globalMuted || (surface==='live' && !liveBroadcastAudio)`. With `liveBroadcastAudio=true` (the default — the broadcast feed MUST be hot or the receiver / NDI / OBS get silence), the in-app Live `<video>` always played audio. The external congregation window (`/api/output/congregation` route.ts L1039-1049 `applyAudio`) ALSO unmutes its `<video>` when `broadcastEnabled`, so once both surfaces were active in the same room, the operator heard the clip twice. NDI was already exempt (separate native capture path, no `<audio>` element).

### Fix

Track external-output-window lifecycle in the store and add it as a fourth mute predicate to the live-surface gate.

1. **Store** (`src/lib/store.ts` L778 decl + L1707 impl): new `outputWindowOpen: boolean` (not persisted, default `false`). Wired by:
   - **Electron path**: `main.ts` `createKioskOutput` now calls `broadcastCongregationOpen()` on every kiosk open AND close. That helper iterates `kioskWindows`, counts those whose URL contains `/api/output/congregation` (excludes the stage-display popout — operator monitor, no broadcast audio), and `webContents.send('output:congregation-open', isOpen)` to every non-kiosk `BrowserWindow`. Preload (`electron/preload.ts` L213-217) exposes `desktop.output.onCongregationOpenChanged(cb)` returning an unsubscribe fn. `EasyWorshipShell` (`easyworship-shell.tsx` L649-660) subscribes once at mount and mirrors into the store flag.
   - **Browser path**: `openOnScreen` (`easyworship-shell.tsx` L630-637) calls `setOutputWindowOpen(true)` immediately on popup success (no first-poll-tick delay) and polls `w.closed` at 1 Hz, flipping back to false + clearing the interval when the popup is gone.

2. **MediaVideoSurface** (`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx`):
   - L313: new selector `const outputWindowOpen = useAppStore((s) => s.outputWindowOpen)`.
   - L361 + L396: `muted` computation appends `|| (surface === 'live' && outputWindowOpen)`. Preview surface intentionally untouched — Preview still honours its own `previewAudio` flag.
   - L398: deps array gains `outputWindowOpen`.

3. **Type bridge** (`src/lib/use-electron.ts` L101): `onCongregationOpenChanged?: (cb: (open: boolean) => void) => () => void` added to the `output` shape.

### Why this gate is necessary alongside `liveBroadcastAudio`

`liveBroadcastAudio` controls whether the broadcast feed is HOT (receiver, NDI, OBS, the kiosk-window `<video>`). Forcing the operator to flip it OFF every time they open a second screen would silence the room — wrong tool. The new gate is independent: the broadcast stays hot, the in-app `<video>` mutes to stop the double-feed. When the operator closes the external window, the in-app speaker comes back automatically without their intervention.

### GUARD-RAIL (Electron broadcast must fire on BOTH open and close)

`broadcastCongregationOpen()` is called from TWO sites in `createKioskOutput`: after `kioskWindows.add(win)` (open) and inside `win.on('closed', () => { kioskWindows.delete(win); broadcastCongregationOpen() })`. Dropping either site strands the flag at the wrong value — open-only and the in-app stays muted forever after a close; close-only and the in-app keeps playing until the next operator-initiated close. Future changes to the kiosk-window lifecycle (e.g. crash-recovery re-spawn, display-removed re-home) MUST trigger this broadcast in lockstep.

### GUARD-RAIL (`/api/output/stage` is excluded by URL substring match)

`broadcastCongregationOpen()` filters by `url.includes('/api/output/congregation')` — the stage-display popout (`/api/output/stage`) is an OPERATOR MONITOR, not a broadcast surface, and carries no `<audio>` element. Counting it would mute the in-app speaker whenever the operator opened their own teleprompter, which is the opposite of what they want. If a future route is added that DOES carry broadcast audio, append it to the substring match.

### GUARD-RAIL (browser-popup poll MUST use `w.closed`, NOT `pagehide` / `beforeunload`)

`pagehide` / `beforeunload` on the popup do not bubble to the opener window for cross-document popups, and Chrome will GC the popup reference before either fires on tab-close. `w.closed` is the only reliable cross-browser signal. 1 Hz cadence is the operator-perception ceiling (sub-second mute response after the popup closes); raising the interval delays mute restoration, lowering it wastes CPU for no perceptible benefit.

### GUARD-RAIL (Preview surface stays untouched)

Only the `surface === 'live'` branch gets the new predicate. Preview audio is the operator's pre-flight check — they MUST be able to hear what they're about to send live regardless of whether an external window is open. Adding the gate to Preview would silently break the "Wirecast preview" workflow (cue → listen → punch live).

### PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal)

Operator-reported "I hear the video twice in the room" or "the sound is doubled / echoing" when a second screen is connected is ALWAYS this class of bug. First diagnostic: open the second screen, play a media clip, then in DevTools console on the operator window run `useAppStore.getState().outputWindowOpen` — `true` means the broadcast happened and this fix is engaged; `false` means the lifecycle subscription failed to wire (check that `desktop?.output?.onCongregationOpenChanged` exists in the preload type AND the runtime bridge). Browser popup operators can also `console.log(useAppStore.getState().outputWindowOpen)` 2s after open — should be `true`.

## v0.7.247 (cont.) — Blank second screen when video is playing: `pushCurrentSlide` now force-enables `outputEnabled`

Operator escalation (same turn): "when video is playing and sends to second screen output is it blank fix it". Reproducible whenever the operator had previously hit the ON-AIR panic-stop button (toolbar top-right `easyworship-shell.tsx` L752: `st.setOutputEnabled(false)`) OR any other affordance that flips the store's `outputEnabled` flag off. After panic-stop, the operator plays a new video → `liveSlide` direct-ref is set correctly, `liveSlideIndex` stays -1 → `buildOutputPayload` reads `s.outputEnabled` at `output-payload.ts` L223 and short-circuits to `{type:'clear', slide:null, ...}` regardless of what's loaded. The v0.7.245 `openOnScreen` `before.liveSlide` branch preserves the direct ref, and v0.7.246 fixed `pushCurrentSlide` to go through `buildOutputPayload` — but neither addressed the `outputEnabled=false` short-circuit that nukes the slide payload at the composer level. Kiosk's `pollOnce` + EventSource both get `slide:null` → applyRender clears `#output` → BLANK.

### Fix

`easyworship-shell.tsx` `pushCurrentSlide` (L561-576) gains a `setOutputEnabled(true)` call BEFORE the `buildOutputPayload` composition. Symmetrical to the existing `if (!outputActive) toggleOutput()` line directly above it: that one re-engages the local NDI/output destination toggle, this one re-engages the store-level broadcast-enabled gate. Both must be ON for the secondary screen to receive a real slide.

```ts
if (!useAppStore.getState().outputEnabled) {
  useAppStore.getState().setOutputEnabled(true)
}
```

### GUARD-RAIL (`outputEnabled` short-circuit in `buildOutputPayload` is load-bearing — do NOT remove)

`output-payload.ts` L223 `s.outputEnabled ? slide-payload : clear-payload` is the store-wide kill switch the ON-AIR panic button depends on. Removing it would make the panic button silently no-op for already-open kiosks (they'd keep painting the slide that was active at kill time). Instead, every CALLER that opens a new output surface or composes a fresh broadcast for a freshly-connected receiver MUST re-enable the flag at the call site if it's off. `pushCurrentSlide` is the canonical entry; any future "send to X" path (cast to AirPlay, push to remote receiver, NDI-2 output) MUST do the same re-enable dance.

### GUARD-RAIL (re-enable BEFORE `buildOutputPayload`, not after)

The order is: (1) flip `outputEnabled=true` in the store, (2) THEN call `buildOutputPayload(useAppStore.getState())`. Reversing the order would compose the payload while `outputEnabled` is still false, producing the `clear` shape; the subsequent store write would change nothing until the NEXT broadcaster tick. The kiosk pollOnce at connect-time fires once and reads whatever is in the store NOW — if that's the stale `clear`, the kiosk paints blank for one full broadcaster cycle (16ms+ debounce + 1.5s SSE settle) and the operator sees a visible flash of black before the video appears. Re-enable first.

### PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal)

"Played a video, sent to second screen, screen is blank but in-app Live pane shows the video" with NO prior panic-stop in the session → liveSlide vs slides[liveSlideIndex] divergence (v0.7.246 territory). Same symptom WITH a recent panic-stop → this v0.7.247 territory. Diagnostic to disambiguate: `useAppStore.getState().outputEnabled` in DevTools — `false` = this bug, `true` = look at the liveSlide pipeline instead. Pairs with v0.7.246's `liveSlide` console.log diagnostic.

### Tests

`pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean across the workspace. Store flag is already a typed boolean with a typed setter (store.ts L599/L600); no new types.

### GUARD-RAIL (DO NOT ship yet — more fixes inbound per operator)

Per operator request mid-investigation: "Don't ship when you are done; more fixes are coming up." v0.7.247 is version-bumped + CHANGELOG'd + typechecked but the GH Contents API PUT loop + `workflow_dispatch` on `release-desktop.yml` deliberately NOT run this turn. Hold release until operator gives the all-clear so multiple fixes ride a single binary (avoids the operator having to reinstall once per micro-version, which is the exact pain point that surfaced this report — they're testing on production hardware).

### Tests

- `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Manual: not verifiable in the sandbox (Electron kiosk window requires a packaged build + connected display); the lifecycle wiring is deterministic and the gate is a single boolean OR on a flag that's exclusively set / cleared by the lifecycle.

---

## v0.7.246 — Second-screen video mirror fix, completion (`pushCurrentSlide` now honours `liveSlide`)

Operator escalation against v0.7.245 — architect code review caught a residual leak after the `openOnScreen` branch fix. The fix in v0.7.245 stopped the `setLiveSlideIndex` call from clobbering `liveSlide`, but `pushCurrentSlide()` (called the next line) still built its own POST payload that read `slides[liveSlideIndex]` only and ignored `s.liveSlide`. When `liveSlide={video}` and `liveSlideIndex=-1`, that branch produced `slide:null` and POSTed it to `/api/output` BEFORE the next broadcaster tick — so the secondary screen / NDI / OBS Browser Source still cleared mid-video, just from a different code path. In-app Live Display still looked right because it reads renderer state, masking the bug from single-monitor reproduction (same masking pattern as v0.7.245).

### Fix

`easyworship-shell.tsx` `pushCurrentSlide` (L548-570) replaces the hand-rolled payload with `buildOutputPayload(useAppStore.getState())`:

```ts
const payload = buildOutputPayload(useAppStore.getState())
await fetch('/api/output', { method:'POST', headers:{...}, body: JSON.stringify(payload) })
```

`buildOutputPayload` is the v0.7.127 single-source-of-truth helper at `output-payload.ts` L19-170. Its first line (L28-30) honours `s.liveSlide` precedence:

```ts
const baseCur = s.liveSlide
  ? s.liveSlide
  : (s.liveSlideIndex >= 0 ? s.slides[s.liveSlideIndex] : null)
```

So the `openOnScreen` immediate POST now matches what the global broadcaster (`output-broadcaster.tsx` L271 — already on `buildOutputPayload`) will send on the next tick. No more race window where `pushCurrentSlide`'s null clears the second screen before the broadcaster catches up.

The payload now also carries every field the renderer expects (mediaPaused, mediaCurrentTime, audio block, settings block, preheatMediaUrl, blanked, etc) — the hand-rolled subset was missing those, which is why the second screen would briefly drop audio/lose paused-state even when the slide field happened to match. Pixel parity is now by construction.

### GUARD-RAIL (every `/api/output` POST MUST go through `buildOutputPayload`)

Any new fetch to `/api/output` from anywhere in the app MUST call `buildOutputPayload(useAppStore.getState())` instead of constructing a payload literal. The composer enforces `liveSlide` precedence + media-video time/paused fields + audio block + all 40+ settings fields. Hand-rolled payloads will drift the moment a new field is added to the OpenAPI surface. Three remaining hand-rolled call sites identified by grep (logos-shell.tsx L5038 `goLogo`, easyworship-shell.tsx L1536 `sendToOutput`) intentionally NOT touched in this release — they're transient overlays (logo, manual slide push) that don't carry liveSlide direct refs, so the bug class doesn't apply. If a future change moves either to use `liveSlide` direct ref, they must be ported to `buildOutputPayload` in lockstep.

### GUARD-RAIL (architect review is load-bearing for `liveSlide` invariant changes)

v0.7.245 looked complete from a manual reading — the `openOnScreen` branch demonstrably preserves `liveSlide`. The residual `pushCurrentSlide` leak was caught only by code-review evaluation that traced the call graph one frame deeper. Any future change that touches the `liveSlide` invariant (store.ts L1367-1377 setters, output-payload.ts L28-30 precedence, or any callsite that reads `slides[liveSlideIndex]` without checking liveSlide first) MUST run `architect({responsibility:'evaluate_task', includeGitDiff:true})` against the diff before shipping. The bug class is "setter / payload-builder pair looks safe in isolation, drops liveSlide silently."

### PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal stays from v0.7.245)

"Second screen shows X, in-app Live Display pane shows Y" is ALWAYS a `liveSlide` vs `slides[liveSlideIndex]` divergence. First diagnostic from operator side: `console.log(useAppStore.getState().liveSlide, useAppStore.getState().liveSlideIndex)`. First diagnostic from broadcast side: `curl -N /api/output/congregation` from a second device; if the SSE event payload has `slide:null` but in-app pane shows media, you're hitting either the v0.7.245 openOnScreen path OR this v0.7.246 pushCurrentSlide path — depending on which fix is missing. Both must be present.

### Tests

- `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Manual reasoning trace (state: `liveSlide={type:'media',mediaKind:'video',mediaUrl:'…'}`, `liveSlideIndex=-1`, `isLive=true`, `outputEnabled=true`):
  - Pre-fix: `openOnScreen` → new branch noops (correct), then `pushCurrentSlide` runs old code → `cur = slides[-1] = undefined` → coerced to `null` → POST `{slide:null,…}`. Secondary screen clears.
  - Post-fix: `openOnScreen` → new branch noops (correct), then `pushCurrentSlide` calls `buildOutputPayload` → `baseCur = liveSlide` (the video) → POST `{slide:liveSlide,…}`. Secondary screen mirrors.
- Cold-start regression check: `liveSlide=null`, `liveSlideIndex=-1`, `slides=[verse,…]`. `openOnScreen` second branch fires → `setLiveSlideIndex(previewIdx)` + `setIsLive(true)`. `buildOutputPayload` → `baseCur = slides[previewIdx]` (verse). Behaviour unchanged.
- Already-staged regression check: `liveSlide=null`, `liveSlideIndex=2`, `isLive=false`. `openOnScreen` third branch fires → `setIsLive(true)`. `buildOutputPayload` → `baseCur = slides[2]`. Behaviour unchanged.

## v0.7.245 — Second-screen video mirror fix + Tier 1E paraphrases (+841 entries)

Operator escalation against v0.7.244 — bug confirmed in screenshot: "video is playing on Live Display, user clicks Output Display [to pick a second screen], it turns to a verse instead of the same video." Plus a 851-paraphrase catalog expansion (Tier 1E) appended to the voice-search corpus.

### A. `openOnScreen` no longer clobbers `liveSlide` when a video is on air

**Root cause.** `easyworship-shell.tsx` `openOnScreen` (L574-620) auto-promotes the operator's preview slide so the freshly-opened second screen never paints blank. Pre-v0.7.245 the auto-promote branch was unconditional on `liveSlideIndex < 0`:

```ts
if (before.liveSlideIndex < 0 && before.slides.length > 0) {
  const idx = Math.max(0, before.previewSlideIndex)
  before.setLiveSlideIndex(idx)   // ← bug
  before.setIsLive(true)
}
```

`setLiveSlideIndex(i)` in `store.ts` L1367-1377 explicitly sets `liveSlide: null` (v0.7.203 invariant — "operator pointed live at slides[] → release the auto-fire direct ref"). The output-payload composer at `output-payload.ts` L28-30 prefers `liveSlide` over `slides[liveSlideIndex]`, so wiping `liveSlide` made the broadcast fall through to `slides[previewIdx]` — a scheduled verse, not the video that was visibly playing on the operator's Live Display.

Trigger sequence: media item staged to Live via `setLiveAuto` (auto-fire) or the Go Live button on a media-video tile sets `liveSlide = { type:'media', mediaKind:'video', mediaUrl }` AND leaves `liveSlideIndex = -1` (auto-fire never claims a schedule slot). Operator clicks Output Display, picks a monitor → `openOnScreen` runs → auto-promote branch fires → `liveSlide` cleared → secondary screen + NDI + OBS Browser Source all re-render with `slides[previewIdx]` (a verse). The visible Live Display pane in the operator app keeps playing the video because it reads off the renderer state, not the broadcast payload — masking the bug from anyone testing single-monitor.

**Fix.** Add a `before.liveSlide` early branch BEFORE the auto-promote:

```ts
if (before.liveSlide) {
  if (!before.isLive) before.setIsLive(true)
} else if (before.liveSlideIndex < 0 && before.slides.length > 0) {
  const idx = Math.max(0, before.previewSlideIndex)
  before.setLiveSlideIndex(idx)
  before.setIsLive(true)
} else if (before.liveSlideIndex >= 0 && !before.isLive) {
  before.setIsLive(true)
}
```

When `liveSlide` is already driving the output (AI-pushed verse, auto-fired media video, manually-pinned media), DO NOT touch `slides[]` state. Promote `isLive` only if it isn't already on; leave the direct ref untouched. `pushCurrentSlide()` then broadcasts the actual playing video.

**GUARD-RAIL (`liveSlide` direct-ref is the source of truth — never clear it without operator intent).** `setLiveSlideIndex` / `setSlides` / `setIsLive(false)` ALL clear `liveSlide` deliberately (v0.7.203). Any new code path that calls these from a non-operator-initiated context (auto-fire effects, opening windows, hotkey side effects, AI handlers) MUST first check `liveSlide` and bail. The bug pattern is "setter looks safe in isolation, silently nukes ref when paired with output-payload's prefer-liveSlide composition."

**GUARD-RAIL (operator-detectable signal for this class of bug).** "Second screen / NDI / OBS shows something different from the in-app Live Display pane" is ALWAYS a `liveSlide` vs `slides[liveSlideIndex]` divergence — Live Display reads renderer state, the broadcast payload composes through `output-payload.ts`. First diagnostic is `console.log(useAppStore.getState().liveSlide, useAppStore.getState().liveSlideIndex)` from the operator app — if `liveSlide` is null and the in-app pane still shows media, the renderer is reading from a different code path than the payload.

**GUARD-RAIL (auto-promote branch is a fallback, not a precondition).** The original branch existed to handle "operator opens output window before staging any slide" — a cold-start UX nicety. It must remain conditional on the absence of any live source. Removing the entire auto-promote breaks cold-start; making it unconditional re-introduces this bug. The 3-branch shape (liveSlide → noop / liveSlideIndex<0 → promote-preview / liveSlideIndex≥0 → promote-isLive) is load-bearing.

**GUARD-RAIL (single-monitor testing misses this).** The bug only manifests when a real secondary screen, NDI receiver, or OBS Browser Source is consuming the broadcast payload. In-app Live Display reads renderer state directly so it always looks correct. Every "second screen behaves differently" report MUST be reproduced against either a connected display, an NDI receiver, or `curl -N /api/output/congregation` — not against the operator pane.

### B. Tier 1E voice-search catalog (+841 paraphrases)

Operator-supplied 851 starter paraphrases (Old + New Testament, format identical to Tier 1D from v0.7.242). Appended verbatim to `src/lib/ai/paraphrase-verses.ts` as `// ── v0.7.245 Tier 1E ──`. Catalog 1681 → 2522 entries.

Normalization at write time per v0.7.242 invariant: smart quotes → ASCII; `Psalm` → `Psalms`; `Song of Songs` → `Song of Solomon`; `I/II/III X` → `1/2/3 X`. Dedupe scope: `(reference, text-lower)` tuple within this batch only — 9 in-batch duplicates removed (846 → 837 single-verse). Four ranged references (`5:3-4`, `5:4-5`, `3:17-18`, `4:6-7`) added as their start verse to fit the single-verse `verseStart: number` schema, bringing total to 841.

**GUARD-RAIL (write-time normalization is load-bearing).** Runtime normalization on read would drift Map keys from `popular-verses.ts` silently. Every bulk-paraphrase append MUST run smart-quote → ASCII + book-name canonicalization at WRITE time. The Python parser in this changelog entry is the canonical pattern.

**GUARD-RAIL (Tier markers MUST stay distinct).** `// ── v0.7.<N> Tier 1<L> ──` delimits each batch so future agents can diff-detect drift, partial-revert a bad batch, and run per-tier QA. Never collapse two tiers into one comment block.

**GUARD-RAIL (ranged references use start-verse).** `Romans 5:4-5` → `verseStart: 4`. Single-verse schema is the contract; cosine match against the start verse routes the operator's spoken paraphrase to the correct chapter slot which is what they want. If a future schema gain adds `verseEnd?: number`, the four entries listed above are the seed.

### C. Ship-pipeline guard-rail (path-identity rule)

Caught mid-v0.7.244 ship: the bulk-drift PUT loop must put `artifacts/imported-app/<x>` at IDENTITY path `artifacts/imported-app/<x>` on GitHub, NOT strip the prefix down to `<x>`. The repo is a full monorepo with `pnpm-workspace.yaml` at root and the desktop app sub-tree at `artifacts/imported-app/`. Two follow-on bugs from the strip:

1. Root-level `package.json` got overwritten by the app's `package.json` (broke the workspace name/version contract).
2. Build produced an installer named for the OLD version because the version-bumped `package.json` was at the wrong path; the build resolved the un-bumped one.

**GUARD-RAIL (root `package.json` is curated, not auto-managed).** The workspace-root `package.json` (`name: "workspace", version: "0.0.0"`) MUST NOT be touched by the ship pipeline. It is the pnpm-workspace anchor; bumping its version or changing its name breaks `pnpm --filter` selectors and electron-builder's app discovery.

**GUARD-RAIL (PUT loop uses local relative path as remote path).** No prefix stripping, no path remapping. If the file lives at `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` locally, it PUTs to `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` remotely. The release-desktop.yml workflow `cd`s into that sub-tree on the runner, so the build sees the bumped version exactly because the path is identical on both sides.

### Tests

- `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean against `artifacts/imported-app/` (842 new const literals appended to the typed paraphrase array; output-display branch is straight reordering, no new types).
- Manual reasoning trace for the bug fix:
  - Pre-fix: `liveSlide={video}`, `liveSlideIndex=-1`, `outputEnabled=true`. `openOnScreen` → `setLiveSlideIndex(0)` (clears liveSlide) → `setIsLive(true)` → `pushCurrentSlide` → payload `slide=slides[0]` (verse). Second screen renders verse.
  - Post-fix: `liveSlide={video}`, `liveSlideIndex=-1`, `outputEnabled=true`. `openOnScreen` → first branch fires (`if (before.liveSlide)`) → `setIsLive(true)` only → `pushCurrentSlide` → payload `slide=liveSlide` (video). Second screen renders video.
  - Cold-start regression check: `liveSlide=null`, `liveSlideIndex=-1`, `slides=[verse, verse, ...]`. First branch skipped (no liveSlide), second branch fires (auto-promote preview). Behaviour unchanged.
  - Already-staged regression check: `liveSlide=null`, `liveSlideIndex=2`, `isLive=false`. First branch skipped, second branch skipped (liveSlideIndex≥0), third branch fires (`setIsLive(true)`). Behaviour unchanged.

## v0.7.244 — Sync (Path A), audio-dup fix, red Live meter, "Later" actually means later

Operator escalation, four issues against v0.7.243, addressed together so they ship in one binary:

### A — Sync: receiver / second screen behind operator's Live pane

**Symptom.** Operator reports video on second screen / OBS Browser source lags the operator's Live pane by a visible 100–300ms in steady state — visible enough to call out, not an acceptable broadcast offset.

**Root cause stack.** Even though every hop is "in tolerance" individually, they compound:
- `MediaVideoSurface` writeback threshold was 0.20s (set in v0.7.234).
- Chromium fires `timeupdate` at ~4Hz (browser-dictated).
- SSE pipe is throttle-gated.
- Receiver-side soft-trim (`playbackRate` 0.97/1.03/1.0) accumulates ~30ms/sec correction.

Sum: a visible steady-state offset that no single component "owns".

**Fix — Path A (operator-approved).** In `MediaVideoSurface`'s timeupdate effect (logos-shell.tsx L493+):
1. Drop the live-surface writeback threshold from `0.20s` → `0.05s`. Preview stays at 0.25s — it doesn't broadcast, so the existing 4Hz tick is plenty.
2. Bolt a 60Hz `requestAnimationFrame` clock loop alongside `timeupdate`, **live surface only**, gated by the same 0.05s threshold so we don't write more often than drift actually demands. The loop wakes every frame so a moving clock always lands inside one frame of the broadcast tick.
3. rAF cancels on `pause`, `ended`, `emptied`; re-arms on `play`, `playing`. Seeded if `!v.paused && !v.ended` on mount (src swap mid-playback case).

Net: every frame the operator's Live `<video>` advances by ~16ms, the gate fires after ~3 frames at the new 0.05s threshold (vs ~12 frames at the old 0.20s threshold), so the receiver gets a fresh target ~4x as often and the soft-trim envelope has 4x less drift to chase. Preview stays at the previous tick rate (no broadcasting → no benefit from faster writes, just store churn).

**GUARD-RAIL (rAF lives in the same effect as `timeupdate` and shares its cleanup).** Splitting them into two effects re-introduces an ordering bug where the rAF loop can outlive a src-swap by one frame and write stale `currentTime` into the new clip's clock.

**GUARD-RAIL (rAF is LIVE-only).** Preview's 0.25s threshold + 4Hz timeupdate is intentional: preview isn't broadcasting, so faster writes only add Zustand churn and downstream React render cost (the audio meter + scrubber both read this value). Don't widen rAF to preview without proving a UX gain.

**GUARD-RAIL (gate-then-write, NOT write-every-frame).** The `if (Math.abs(cur - stored) > writeThreshold)` check inside `onTimeUpdate` means rAF + timeupdate together write at most ~20Hz to the store (every 0.05s of clock drift). Removing the gate would write 60 times per second to a Zustand atom and re-render every consumer 60 times per second — that's a different regression class.

### B — Audio duplicates when video plays on the second screen

**Symptom.** Operator: "audio duplicates when video plays on the second screen". Two voices/copies of the same audio in the operator's room.

**Root cause.** Both audio sources are emitting simultaneously:
- The operator's in-app Live `<video>` (slide-renderer.tsx L141 / MediaVideoSurface) plays unmuted when `liveMonitorAudio === true`.
- The visible secondary screen / congregation TV (route.ts L1035 — `applyAudio`) plays unmuted at master volume (this IS the broadcast audio source).

`liveMonitorAudio` defaulted to `true` (store.ts L1666), so the operator's Live pane and the receiver both emitted audio out of the box.

**Fix.** Change `liveMonitorAudio` initial value `true → false` (store.ts L1681). The operator's Live pane is a MONITOR — silent until the operator explicitly puts on headphones and clicks the headphone toggle. The receiver remains the sole audio source by default.

**GUARD-RAIL (don't conflate `liveMonitorAudio` with `liveBroadcastAudio`).** `liveBroadcastAudio` (default `true`) is the BROADCAST speaker — controls receiver + NDI + the in-app live `<video>` `muted` attribute via MediaVideoSurface (per v0.7.216 follow-up #3). `liveMonitorAudio` (now default `false`) is the OPERATOR HEADPHONE monitor — controls the slide-renderer `<video>` `muted` attribute via the `audible` computation. Two independent toggles, two different mute paths.

**GUARD-RAIL (broadcast stays default ON, monitor stays default OFF).** A first-launch operator expects audio to leave the building (broadcast on) and expects their own room to be silent unless they ask for monitor audio (headphones off). Don't flip either default without a separate operator decision.

**GUARD-RAIL (persisted operators who already toggled monitor ON stay ON).** `liveMonitorAudio` is in the persisted Zustand slice — existing installs that explicitly set it `true` will keep their preference. The new default only affects fresh installs and operators who never touched the headphone toggle.

### C — Red vertical line on the Live Display

**Symptom.** Operator's screenshot arrow at the LEFT EDGE of the LIVE DISPLAY card stage. The strip read as alarming — they said "remove that red line".

**Root cause.** The Live Display card's right-rail audio meter (`AudioMeter` at logos-shell.tsx L2553) was rendered with `tone={liveBroadcastAudio ? 'red' : 'green'}`. With `liveBroadcastAudio` defaulting to `true` (the desired broadcast-on state), the meter rendered with the rose/red gradient (`from-rose-500 via-rose-400 to-amber-400`), which painted a static red strip whenever any audio was flowing. The narrow `w-2` strip on the LEFT edge of the stage container read as a vertical alarm bar.

**Fix.** Force `tone="green"` for the Live meter (same as Preview). Both surfaces now use the same green VU gradient. The broadcast on/off state is still communicated by the speaker-toggle BUTTON right next to the meter — that button still flips between rose-tinted (on) and grey (off) so the operator hasn't lost the visual cue, just the alarming meter colour.

**GUARD-RAIL (the meter is information, not an alarm).** Audio meters are status indicators (signal flowing / silent) — they should not double as state indicators (broadcast on / off). State belongs on a button with explicit affordance + click target; status belongs on a passive VU bar with a single non-alarming colour. Don't re-introduce a state-tone on the meter without a separate operator decision.

### D — "Later" button on the update dialog downloads anyway

**Symptom.** Operator clicks "Later" on the v0.7.x update popup, dialog closes, ~60s later the installer downloads anyway in the background.

**Root cause.** `electron/updater.ts L286` calls `scheduleAutoDownload()` from the `update-available` handler. That timer (60s grace) fires regardless of dialog dismissal — only an explicit IPC call to `updater:set-auto-download` (false) clears the timer (handler at updater.ts L535). The dialog's `handleDismiss` was writing the per-version `localStorage` dismissal flag but never reaching into the main process to disable the background timer. Operator saw their "Later" choice silently overridden 60s later.

**Fix.** `update-available-dialog.tsx` `handleDismiss` now calls `desktop.updater.setAutoDownload?.(false)` BEFORE writing the dismissal flag. The IPC handler clears `autoDownloadTimer` immediately (`updater.ts L535: if (!autoDownloadEnabled) clearAutoDownloadTimer()`). Guarded with try/catch and `?.` so a web build (no desktop bridge) never throws.

**GUARD-RAIL (opt-out is PER-SESSION, not permanent).** `autoDownloadEnabled` is a process-local `let` (updater.ts L121), reset to `true` on each app launch. The operator gets the background-prefetch benefit on subsequent launches — they only opt out for the CURRENT session when they explicitly click Later. Don't promote this to a persisted setting without a separate operator request — the current behaviour matches the operator's mental model ("I clicked Later, so don't download THIS time").

**GUARD-RAIL (dialog AND banner BOTH need to honour Later).** The `<UpdateBanner>` (update-banner.tsx) is the secondary surface for the same update — if a future change adds a Later button to the banner, that handler must ALSO call `setAutoDownload(false)`. Currently the banner only has Download/Install actions, so no parity bug yet.

### Files changed

- `artifacts/imported-app/src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` — fixes A + C
- `artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/store.ts` — fix B
- `artifacts/imported-app/src/components/update-available-dialog.tsx` — fix D
- `artifacts/imported-app/package.json` — version bump
- `artifacts/imported-app/CHANGELOG.md` — this entry
- `replit.md` — Recent (full detail) for v0.7.244, demote v0.7.243 to one-liner

### Tests

`pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. No new dependencies. No schema migrations. Ship via the canonical pipeline (bulk-drift PUT to `release/v0.7.244` from `release/v0.7.243` ancestor + workflow_dispatch on `release-desktop.yml`).
