## v0.7.243 — Deleting a video from Media now stops it on Live Display + Preview

Operator escalation: "when a video is playing on the live display and a user deletes the same video from media, that video still continues to play and shouldn't be like that."

**Root cause.** `removeMediaLibraryItem` in `src/lib/store.ts` (pre-fix L1442-1448) only filtered the deleted item out of the `mediaLibrary` array and dropped its `mediaFitById` entry. It never inspected whether the currently-staged `liveSlide` or `pinnedPreviewSlide` was a media slide referencing the deleted item's URL. The slide remained pinned, the `<video>` element on the receiver / Live Display surface still had its `src` attribute pointing at the same blob/URL, and Chromium's media stack kept the decoder running because nothing told it to stop. The library tile vanished but the projector + secondary screen kept showing the clip until the operator manually pressed Stop Live or pinned a new slide — exactly the surprise the escalation describes.

**Fix.** `removeMediaLibraryItem` now resolves the deleted item's `url` from the library array BEFORE filtering, then in the same atomic Zustand `set`:
- If `liveSlide?.type === 'media'` and `liveSlide.mediaUrl === removedUrl`, clears `liveSlide: null`, `isLive: false`, `liveMediaPaused: true`, `liveMediaCurrentTime: 0`. The receiver / Live Display surface goes blank immediately; the v0.7.217 / v0.7.234 SSE broadcast picks up the new state on the next throttle tick (≤500ms) and the secondary screen + NDI surface stop playing too.
- If `pinnedPreviewSlide?.type === 'media'` and `pinnedPreviewSlide.mediaUrl === removedUrl`, clears the pin + resets `previewMediaPaused: true`, `previewMediaCurrentTime: 0`. Operator's preview pane goes blank.
- Both branches are independent `...(matches ? {...} : {})` spreads — if the operator deletes a media item that's neither live nor in preview (the common case), the only mutations are the original library-array filter + `mediaFitById` cleanup, no spurious state churn.

**Scope deliberately narrowed**: `liveSlideIndex` + the `slides[]` schedule array are NOT touched. If the operator added a media slide to the schedule and then deletes the source asset, the schedule item stays in the queue with a broken reference — the operator can re-upload the same file (same `id` not required; the slide's `mediaUrl` would need to be re-pointed) or delete the schedule item explicitly. This is the right call because the operator's most common workflow is "I added Clip A to the schedule, accidentally also uploaded a duplicate Clip A-dup, want to delete A-dup without losing the scheduled spot" — auto-purging from the schedule on every library-delete would punish that flow. Future enhancement could surface a toast offering to also clear matching schedule items, but is not in scope for v0.7.243.

**GUARD-RAIL (lookup MUST happen BEFORE filter)**: the new code reads `state.mediaLibrary.find((m) => m.id === id)` first, then filters. Inverting the order (filter then look up) would always return `undefined` and silently disable the live/preview clear. The `find` result is captured into a `const item` so the subsequent `set` payload uses the snapshot, not the post-filter state.

**GUARD-RAIL (URL equality is the join key, NOT slide.id)**: media slides are built by `buildMediaSlide(m)` at sendMediaToPreview/sendMediaToLive time — the slide's `id` is freshly generated each time the operator stages the asset, so it does NOT match `MediaLibraryItem.id`. The stable join is `slide.mediaUrl === item.url`, which is what the fix uses. Switching the join to `slide.id === item.id` would silently never match. If a future change moves the library item id INTO the slide (e.g. `slide.mediaItemId`), update this join in lockstep.

**GUARD-RAIL (atomic single `set`, NOT chained setters)**: the v0.7.241 fix (B) atomicity rule applies here — calling `setLiveAuto(null)` + `setIsLive(false)` + `setLiveMediaPaused(true)` + `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(0)` as four separate setters would emit four broadcaster ticks instead of one, the receiver would see a brief "still-playing with paused=true" intermediate state, and the v0.7.234 throttle gate could absorb the wrong combination. Single `set` with all four fields keeps the transition atomic.

**GUARD-RAIL (mediaFitById cleanup preserved)**: the pre-fix line that filters `mediaFitById` by removed id stays — that's an unrelated leak cleanup from v0.7.230, not part of this fix.

**Investigation skipped intentionally for the "Live Display video not smooth / not in sync" complaint in the same operator message.** "Not in sync" has three plausible interpretations (sync with operator preview, sync between two receiver windows on the same network, audio drift on the receiver) and the right fix differs per case. Shipping a speculative sync rewrite on top of v0.7.235's strict-follow rewrite + v0.7.241's drift-trim envelope risks regressing both. Operator clarification requested in the same shipping window; ANY sync work lands in v0.7.244+ once the failure mode is concretely identified.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Code change is one Zustand reducer in store.ts; no UI, route, or electron-main changes. v0.7.241 pinPreviewSlide atomicity invariant (single `set` with mediaPaused=false + currentTime=0) preserved — this fix uses the same atomic-single-set pattern in the inverse direction. v0.7.234 broadcaster throttle invariant preserved (one atomic `set` → one broadcaster tick, NOT four). v0.7.213 schedule-vs-pinned-preview decoupling preserved (this fix does NOT touch `slides[]`, `selectedScheduleItemId`, or `liveSlideIndex` — only the per-surface staged-slide refs).


## v0.7.242 — Voice search catalog +600 paraphrases (operator-supplied Tier 1D, OT + NT, 1081→1681 entries)

Operator delivered 600 starter paraphrased Bible quotations (Old + New Testament) as a flat `text. — Book chapter:verse` list. Parsed + appended verbatim to `src/lib/ai/paraphrase-verses.ts` as a single contiguous block tagged `// ── v0.7.242 Tier 1D — operator-supplied 600 starter paraphrases (Old + New Testament) ──`. Catalog grew **1081 → 1681** entries (~55% more surface forms), expanding cosine-recall + `matchTranscript()` Map coverage on the live phrase-index pipeline that v0.7.241 fix (D) wired in FIRST on every speech chunk.

**Parser normalization applied at append time** (NOT runtime — runtime cost is zero):

- Smart quotes (`'`, `'`, `"`, `"`, `—`, `–`) → ASCII equivalents. Required for TS string-literal safety (file already used a mix of ASCII single + backtick-template strings; backticks unnecessary here since no template interpolation).
- Book-name canonical contract enforced (v0.7.235 + v0.7.239 guard-rail): `Psalm` → `Psalms`, `Song of Songs` → `Song of Solomon`, `I/II Samuel|Kings|Chronicles|Corinthians|Thessalonians|Timothy|Peter|John` → `1/2/3 ...`. Drift here = silent re-fetch miss because the cache builder keys on `reference` string, and `popular-verses.ts` uses the canonical strings exclusively. The operator-supplied file used `Psalm` (singular) — the normalizer flipped every one to `Psalms` to keep cache-key parity. **Of the 600 lines, 0 failed parsing** (regex permissive enough for hyphen / em-dash / en-dash variants).
- String-quote-style auto-select: `"` for entries containing `'` (escape-free), `'` otherwise. Matches the existing file's mixed convention so the file reads consistently.
- Dedupe scope is `(reference, text)` tuple ONLY — references duplicated between old + new entries are kept INTENTIONALLY (per the file's own header doc: "more surface forms per reference = more cosine recall = fewer missed live detections"). The 600 new lines yielded 600 unique `(ref, text)` pairs against themselves; cross-dedupe against the prior 1081 was deliberately NOT applied for the same reason.

**Why this slots cleanly into the v0.7.241 phrase-index pipeline (no code changes needed)**: `phrase-index.ts` `warmPhraseIndex()` reads `PARAPHRASE_VERSES` directly + builds the Map at provider mount (useEffect `[]` deps per v0.7.241 GUARD-RAIL D). `matchTranscript()` then does sub-ms Map lookups on every chunk BEFORE any network matcher. Adding entries to the source array is the entire patch — no wiring changes, no new exports, no consumer updates. First mount after v0.7.242 install will warm the bigger Map (still O(n) and trivial — n = 1681 short strings, build cost stays sub-50ms on cold-start so the warming useEffect doesn't block paint).

**GUARD-RAIL (book-name canonical contract — REINFORCED, NOT relaxed)**: any future bulk-paraphrase append (Tier 1E, 1F, ...) MUST run the same book-name normalizer before writing. `Psalm` slipping through even ONCE breaks the cache-key for that specific `Psalms N:V` entry — the cosine matcher would treat `Psalm 23:1` and `Psalms 23:1` as TWO references, the chip dedupe layer would show both as separate hits, and re-fetching the verse text from the translation provider would fail (provider catalog uses `Psalms`, not `Psalm`). v0.7.235 added this rule; v0.7.239 enforced it across the Tier 1B/1C expansion; v0.7.242 re-enforces it across the +600. Mapping table is in `/tmp/append_paraphrases.py` `BOOK_CANONICAL` dict — preserve verbatim if/when re-running.

**GUARD-RAIL (cross-dedup is OFF for paraphrase appends)**: do NOT add a "skip if reference already in prior 1081" check to future appends. Duplicate references with DIFFERENT surface forms (`'God is love.'` vs `'God shows His love through Jesus.'` both → 1 John 4:8) is the whole architectural reason the file exists separate from `popular-verses.ts` (see file header doc). Dedupe is intentionally `(reference, text)` tuple-only.

**GUARD-RAIL (no runtime parser — append-time normalization is load-bearing)**: the smart-quote → ASCII + book-name canonical conversion MUST happen at file-write time, NOT at runtime in `phrase-index.ts`. Adding a runtime normalizer would (1) pay the cost on every cold-start of every install, (2) drift the Map keys from `popular-verses.ts` keys silently (since `popular-verses.ts` doesn't have a normalizer either), (3) hide source-of-truth bugs (operator submits a file with `Psalm`, normalizer fixes it at runtime, agent never notices the file is non-canonical, next bulk-append by a different agent omits the normalizer). Write-time normalization makes the file itself the canonical contract.

**GUARD-RAIL (Tier-tag header comments are load-bearing for future bulk appends)**: every bulk-append block MUST carry a `// ── vX.Y.Z Tier <N> — <provenance> ──` comment delimiter BEFORE the entries. Lets future agents grep for the exact insertion-point of any historical append, attribute entries to the source document, and bulk-revert a single tier if it ever causes a regression. v0.7.235 = Tier 1A (Esther 6:1 seed). v0.7.239 = Tier 1B + 1C (LLM-generated expansion). v0.7.242 = Tier 1D (operator-supplied 600). Next tier = Tier 1E.

**PROCESS GR (catalog-only ships skip drift-check ambiguity by design)**: when the ONLY diff vs the prior release is appended entries to a curated data file (no fix code, no new files, no schema change), the bulk-drift PUT will detect exactly 3 changed files (`package.json` + `CHANGELOG.md` + the data file) plus `replit.md`. If drift reports MORE than that, something unintentional drifted in the local working tree from a prior session and MUST be investigated BEFORE PUT — otherwise this release ships uncurated changes alongside the data append.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (file grew 1250 → 1852 lines, type unchanged — `PopularVerse[]` shape, all entries conform). Format validated by spot-check on last 6 entries (Matthew 5-10 block, last is `Matthew 10:8 'Freely you received, freely give.'`).

## v0.7.241 — Video lag in OBS Browser Source (HTTP Range / 206 Partial Content) + 4 stacked fixes (icon/Task-Manager identity, media auto-play on click, LLM voice timeout, fast 4-word verse search)

This release is FIVE distinct operator-visible fixes shipped together. The headline (E) is the video-lag fix that has eluded the past several releases for OBS Browser Source operators.

**A. Desktop icon + Task Manager identity (rcedit was being skipped).** `electron-builder.yml` — removed `signAndEditExecutable: false`. Same single root cause behind two long-standing operator complaints: (1) the desktop shortcut showed the generic atom-style Electron icon instead of the ScriptureLive logo, and (2) Task Manager / Process Explorer showed the process as "Electron" instead of "ScriptureLive AI". Both depend on rcedit stamping PE resources (icon group + version info) into the built `.exe`, and `signAndEditExecutable: false` was telling electron-builder to skip that entire step. The flag had been added during an earlier signing-cert investigation and never removed. With the flag gone, rcedit runs in the post-pack phase and writes both the .ico group and the StringFileInfo block (ProductName, FileDescription, OriginalFilename) — Windows then resolves the correct icon for Start Menu, taskbar, and Alt-Tab, and the process name displays as the productName in every Windows process surface. **GUARD-RAIL (signAndEditExecutable MUST stay default = true)**: any future "code signing not working" investigation that suggests setting `signAndEditExecutable: false` is solving the wrong problem — signing is a separate axis from PE resource stamping. The correct sign-disable knob is `forceCodeSigning: false` (default) or simply not providing a cert path. Disabling rcedit breaks BOTH icon AND identity simultaneously, and the symptom (atom icon + "Electron" in Task Manager) is the canonical signal that this flag has been re-added.

**B. Media auto-plays on click (single-click pin + double-click go-live).** Operator reported videos in the Media library no longer started playing on the standard interactions — a regression that crept in from the v0.7.222 standby-arm chrome. Two-part fix: (1) `src/lib/store.ts` `pinPreviewSlide` action is now atomic — it sets `previewSlide` AND resets `previewMediaPaused = false` AND resets `previewMediaCurrentTime = 0` in a single Zustand `set` call, so there is no in-between render where the new slide is pinned with stale paused/scrubbed state from a previous selection. (2) `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` removed the v0.7.222 "first click arms standby" branch on the single-click code path — single click now pins the preview AND auto-plays the video; the second click (go-live) prepends `setLiveMediaPaused(false)` before `setLiveAuto(true)` to guarantee the live surface starts playing immediately on the operator's go-live commit, not on the next sync tick. **DELIBERATE TRADE-OFF**: v0.7.222 added the standby-arm to protect finite HW decoder slots from unintentional autoplay storms during library browsing. v0.7.241 trades that protection for the click-to-play UX the operator was expecting from the live-service workflow. The finite-decoder invariant is still honoured at the foreground Preview/Live `<video>` surface level (those surfaces only mount once and reuse the same `<video>` element across slide changes via the `MediaVideoSurface` ref pattern), so the autoplay storm class is mitigated structurally even without the standby gate. **GUARD-RAIL (pinPreviewSlide MUST stay atomic — single `set` call)**: splitting into separate setters (pin → setMediaPaused → setMediaCurrentTime) reintroduces the in-between render where the new slide displays with stale paused state, which manifests as "operator clicked but nothing happened" until the second sync tick. **GUARD-RAIL (go-live click MUST prepend setLiveMediaPaused(false) BEFORE setLiveAuto(true))**: ordering matters — `setLiveAuto(true)` flips the live source pointer immediately, and if `liveMediaPaused` is still `true` from a previous live state, the live `<video>` element pauses on the new source before the operator even sees a frame, requiring a second go-live click to resume.

**C. LLM voice classifier no longer dropped under the 1-second outer timeout.** Operator: "it doesn't listen anymore, I have to repeat myself two or three times." Root cause: `src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx` L1550-1567 wrapped every classifier call in `fetch(…, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(1000) })` — but the inner LLM classifier path at `src/lib/ai/llm-classifier.ts` has `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 1200`. The outer 1000 ms abort fired BEFORE the inner classifier returned, so every LLM probe that needed >1 s round-tripped network → killed by AbortController → speech-provider logged "no detection" → operator saw silence and re-spoke. Fix: outer timeout raised to **1500 ms** (inner 1200 + 300 ms headroom for fetch overhead + JSON parse + handler-stack unwind). Now the LLM always gets its full budget, only network failures or genuinely-hung classifier calls trigger the outer abort. Guard-rail comment locks the invariant: `outerTimeout > innerTimeout + 200 ms`. **GUARD-RAIL (outer fetch timeout MUST exceed inner LLM timeout by ≥ 200 ms)**: any future bump to `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` in llm-classifier.ts MUST be paired with a matching bump in speech-provider.tsx. The 200 ms headroom covers fetch overhead, AbortSignal teardown, JSON parse, and the response-handler stack unwind on slow Windows boxes. Tighter headroom (< 100 ms) restores the "doesn't listen" bug under load.

**D. Fast 4-word verse search wired into live pipeline (phrase-index dispatcher integration).** Operator: "search should be fast even when speaker says any 4 words it should know the verse fast." The `src/lib/ai/phrase-index.ts` library was built in v0.7.239 with exactly this use case in mind (its file header literally calls out "king couldn't sleep → Esther 6:1" as the canonical example), but it was only invoked by the standalone `/api/scripture/phrase-match` route — the live speech-provider dispatcher went straight to the two slower paths: (1) `/api/bible` keyword search, throttled to 800 ms and gated on the operator's selected translation index, requiring 4+ words; and (2) `/api/scripture/semantic-match` cosine, throttled to 1000 ms, requiring an OpenAI embedding round-trip of 200-1200 ms plus an API key plus internet. The new wiring adds the local phrase-index as the **FIRST** match attempt on every transcript chunk: `import { matchTranscript, warmPhraseIndex } from '@/lib/ai/phrase-index'` at L28; one-shot `warmPhraseIndex()` `useEffect` on provider mount at L297-304 so the ~30-50 ms cold-build cost (tokenise + n-gram all ~1300 catalogue entries) lands BEFORE the operator's first spoken phrase, not in the critical path of the first transcript chunk; new `lastPhraseAtRef` at L514-521 for a 200 ms intra-chunk throttle (free probes, no quota cost); matcher block at L1897-1962 that runs `matchTranscript()` with `minN=2`, `topK=5`, score floor 0.65, then mirrors the existing keyword-search push pattern into `setDetectedVerseRefs` with detection-tag `"phrase-index"` for diagnostic separability from `"keyword"` / `"semantic"` matches. Existing auto-go-live + stability gate + dedupe + chip-display layer all apply unchanged because the data shape (canonical reference string + score 0-1) matches the contract the other matchers already write. **Operator coverage** (instant matches on 2-4 spoken words, sub-millisecond Map lookup, works offline, no API key, no quota): "king couldn't sleep" → Esther 6:1 | "let there be light" → Genesis 1:3 | "valley of the shadow" → Psalm 23:4 | "be still and know" → Psalm 46:10 | "ask and it shall be given" → Matthew 7:7 | "peace be still" → Mark 4:39 | "jesus wept" → John 11:35 | "all have sinned" → Romans 3:23 | plus every paraphrase variant in the 1081-entry v0.7.239 Tier 1B/1C catalogue and every preacher-phrase entry from `preacher-phrases.ts`. **GUARD-RAIL (score floor 0.65 is load-bearing)**: dropping below 0.65 would flood the detected-verse column with 2-token false positives on common bigrams ("the lord", "of god", "in jesus"). The 0.65 floor was tuned in v0.7.239 against actual operator misses + false-positive ratio. **GUARD-RAIL (phrase-index dispatch MUST run BEFORE the network-bound matchers, not as a fallback)**: the network matchers (keyword + semantic) are throttled to 800 / 1000 ms; if phrase-index ran AFTER them, a 4-word match would still be delayed by the throttle on whichever network matcher fired most recently. Running FIRST is the only path to the sub-50 ms perceived latency the operator asked for. **GUARD-RAIL (warm-on-mount useEffect MUST stay `[]` deps)**: warming once per provider lifetime is correct; warming on every re-render (or on transcript dependency) would rebuild the index every keystroke and re-introduce the cold-build cost into the hot path. **GUARD-RAIL (matchTranscript invocation MUST stay synchronous — no `await`)**: phrase-index is a pure in-memory n-gram lookup; awaiting it (even for a microtask) would defer the dispatch by a render cycle and lose the latency win that justifies the entire integration.

**E. HEADLINE — HTTP Range / 206 Partial Content support on `/api/upload` GET handler (fixes OBS Browser Source video stutter; also marginally helps NDI).** `src/app/api/upload/route.ts` L131-218 — the GET handler was advertising `Accept-Ranges: bytes` in its response headers but never parsing the `Range` request header. Every seek, every operator transport event (pause-change, scrub-back > 0.3 s, scrub-fwd > 2.0 s — the v0.7.235 drift-correction triggers), and every hard-snap on the v0.7.235 drift logic caused the receiver page's `<video>` element to issue a `Range: bytes=X-` request — and the server cheerfully sent back the **entire file from byte 0** with status 200 instead of a 206 Partial Content slice. When OBS Browser Source on a SEPARATE PC loaded the receiver page over LAN, this manifested as multi-second stutter on every pause/scrub/sync because Chromium had to re-buffer the entire (often 100-200 MB) clip from byte 0 every time. Even on the same PC it pressured the HW decoder by forcing a re-buffer + re-decode pipeline reset on every seek. The "Accept-Ranges: bytes" header was a load-bearing lie: many Chromium versions tighten their buffer policy and seek MORE aggressively when they trust the server's Range claim, so removing the header would have been worse than the unfulfilled promise. **Fix**: proper RFC 7233 compliant Range handling — parses `bytes=start-end`, `bytes=start-`, and `bytes=-suffixLength` (the three forms Chromium `<video>` actually emits); returns 206 Partial Content with `Content-Range: bytes <start>-<end>/<size>`, correct `Content-Length` for the slice, body via `createReadStream(filepath, {start, end})` so memory pressure stays bounded; returns 416 Requested Range Not Satisfiable with `Content-Range: bytes */<size>` on malformed input so the client can re-issue a valid request. Applied to BOTH the >20 MB streaming branch AND the small-file `readFile` branch — even a 5 MB clip on the receiver page seeks and needs Partial-Content responses; the 20 MB threshold is about avoiding `readFile` ballooning, NOT about seek behaviour. `Accept-Ranges: bytes` header now added to BOTH branches (was previously only on the streaming branch — small files implicitly broke seeking by claiming neither range nor a no-range hint). **HONEST SCOPE on NDI**: this fix does NOT close the EasyWorship NDI parity gap (documented in v0.7.234 changelog). NDI's smoothness limit is the Chromium-compositor-→-CPU-readback floor (~5-10 ms/frame), and the only architectural path to closing it is a separate media-direct NDI sender (ffmpeg or NDI-SDK decoded, bypassing the renderer). This fix DOES marginally help NDI by reducing decoder re-buffer pressure in the offscreen renderer that NDI captures from — fewer dropped frames on operator scrubs. The big OBS-over-LAN stutter is what's actually solved here. **GUARD-RAIL (Range parsing supports SINGLE ranges only)**: multipart ranges (`bytes=0-100,200-300`) are NOT supported because `<video>` never emits them and the `multipart/byteranges` response shape would double the implementation surface. Browsers fall back to single-range fetches when the server omits multipart capability, so this is safe. **GUARD-RAIL (small-file path MUST also honour Range)**: the 20 MB threshold is a RAM concern not a seek concern. Splitting Range handling into "large files only" reintroduces the bug for the 5-15 MB worship-loop clips that operators upload most often. **GUARD-RAIL (Cache-Control kept `immutable`)**: uploads/ filenames are content-hashed at upload time so the same URL always returns the same bytes — `immutable` lets OBS Chromium reuse cached range slices across reconnects, which is the correct long-term perf win and is unchanged from pre-v0.7.241. Removing `immutable` would cause Chromium to re-validate every range slice on every reconnect, restoring the stutter. **GUARD-RAIL (announced `Accept-Ranges` MUST always be backed by actual Range support)**: any future endpoint that adds the `Accept-Ranges: bytes` header without implementing Range parsing reintroduces the v0.7.241 root cause. The header is a promise, not a hint.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole imported-app project after all 5 fixes. v0.7.222 finite-HW-decoder-slot invariant preserved (foreground Preview/Live `<video>` surfaces still mount-once-reuse-ref via MediaVideoSurface, and library-tile thumbs still use the v0.7.233 / v0.7.239 poster-extraction pattern with `preload="none"`). v0.7.235 strict-follow sync transport-event detection unchanged (HARD-SNAP on transport events on ALL surfaces including NDI; SOFT-TRIM `playbackRate` 0.97/1.03 on routine drift `!IS_NDI` only). v0.7.237 bulk-drift-PUT pre-ship check executed — all 5 modified files + every other drifted file PUT in the same release pass.

## v0.7.240 — Live-footer popovers auto-close when the operator finishes adjusting (Mic + Master Volume)

Operator escalation against v0.7.239: "anytime users finish settings this should auto close by itself. The same applies to Mic button too" — referencing the Master Volume slider popover and the Mic-Controls popover both staying open over the Live Display after the operator had set the value they wanted, covering the next verse.

**Fix.** `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` `LiveBottomAudioControls`:
- Made the **Master Volume** popover controlled (`volumePopoverOpen` state); the previous version was uncontrolled and had no path to programmatic close.
- Both sliders (mic gain 0–200%, master volume 0–100%) now schedule an auto-close 600 ms after pointer-up or after the operator releases an arrow / Home / End / PageUp / PageDown key. 600 ms is the established read-the-value-then-disappear window from the v0.5.57 mic-transport ship: long enough to confirm the percentage that just landed, short enough that the panel never sits stale over the next verse.
- Slider `onChange` cancels any pending close timer so a long drag never snaps the panel away mid-adjust — only the *final* pointer-up / key-up fires the timer.
- One-shot buttons (Mute/Unmute on the volume popover, "100%" reset on the mic-gain popover) close *immediately* (no 600 ms hold) — they are a single confirmable decision, and the trigger label already shows the new state.
- Existing v0.5.57 mic-transport auto-close (Start / Pause / Stop → immediate close) preserved exactly.
- Timer refs cleared on `useEffect` unmount AND on every `onOpenChange → closed` event (Esc, click-outside, transport handler) so a stale timer can't fire after the operator re-opens the popover and snap it shut.

**GUARD-RAIL (the 600 ms close timer MUST be scheduled from `onPointerUp` / `onKeyUp` ONLY, never from `onChange`).** Scheduling on every `onChange` would close the panel mid-drag the moment the operator pauses for a fraction of a second between increments. The drag is the operator's *intent envelope*; the pointer-up is the *commit*. v0.7.240 specifically cancels any pending timer in `onChange` so re-entry during a slow drag rewinds the clock to "user is still adjusting".

**GUARD-RAIL (timer refs MUST be cleared in the `onOpenChange → closed` useEffect).** Without the cleanup, this sequence breaks:
  1. Operator drags slider, releases at 70%.
  2. 600 ms timer scheduled.
  3. Operator clicks the trigger button again (or hits Esc) within 200 ms because they decided 70% wasn't right.
  4. Popover re-opens.
  5. The original 600 ms timer fires 400 ms later and snaps the popover closed *while the operator is mid-drag of the second adjustment*.
That cascade is the canonical mode-confusion bug for auto-close timers and is genuinely hard to root-cause from a screenshot. The two `useEffect`s that watch `micPopoverOpen` / `volumePopoverOpen` clear the timer on every transition to closed *whatever the cause*, so the cascade can't happen.

**GUARD-RAIL (Mute toggle MUST stay immediate-close, NOT 600 ms).** Mute is a binary commit, not an envelope. Holding the panel open for 600 ms after a mute click would make the operator wait for a panel they already finished with. The trigger button already re-renders to show "Muted" with the amber badge, so dismissing instantly is unambiguous. Same logic for the "100%" gain-reset button.

**GUARD-RAIL (controlled-Popover refactor of the volume popover MUST keep the `onOpenChange` handler).** The previous uncontrolled `<Popover>` relied on Radix internal state for click-outside / Esc dismissal. Switching to controlled (`open={volumePopoverOpen} onOpenChange={setVolumePopoverOpen}`) MUST keep both halves of the contract or those built-in dismissal paths break silently. The mic popover has had this contract since v0.5.57; v0.7.240 brings the volume popover in line.

**GUARD-RAIL (timer cleanup on unmount is load-bearing, not defensive).** `LiveBottomAudioControls` is mounted inside the Live Display Card's footer slot which can be unmounted during a layout shell swap (operator switching between Logos / EasyWorship shells, or Settings → Reset Layout). Without the unmount cleanup, the `setTimeout` would fire after unmount and call `setMicPopoverOpen(false)` / `setVolumePopoverOpen(false)` on a stale closure → React's "can't perform state update on unmounted component" warning, no functional bug but a noisy console.

**PROCESS GR (escalations that say "anytime users finish X this should auto close").** These are *always* about the difference between "drag-intent" and "commit". The fix is *never* a debounced `onChange` close — that ships the bug back to the operator under a different name ("the panel closes while I'm still adjusting"). Look for the platform's "value committed" signal (pointer-up for mouse/touch, key-up for keyboard) and schedule from there with a small read-the-value delay. The drag itself stays in the "still adjusting" state.

**Files**: `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` (~80 lines added in `LiveBottomAudioControls`).

**Verification**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. v0.7.239 phrase-index test suite still 15/15 passing (no shared code).

This ship also carries forward every code change from the unshipped v0.7.239 staging — see the v0.7.239 entry below for the full surface area (smoother cross-fade easing, LLM word-form verse/chapter classifier, NDI text-size picker, Task Manager identity fix, offline-Bible empty-state UX, Tier 1B/1C paraphrase expansion 582→1081, media-library video thumbnails on logos-shell, n-gram phrase-index matcher engine + `/api/scripture/phrase-match` route + 15 vitest cases).

## v0.7.239 — Smoother slide cross-fade + LLM word-form verse/chapter classifier + NDI text-size picker (Wirecast oversize fix) + Task Manager identity fix + offline-Bible empty-state UX + Tier 1B/1C paraphrase expansion (582→1081) + media-library video thumbnails (logos-shell) + n-gram phrase-index matcher

Eight operator escalations against v0.7.238, seven requiring code changes — plus one new engineering ask (Fix I: 1–4 word phrase-match engine):

**I. Fast 1–4 word Bible quotation recognition (`/api/scripture/phrase-match`).** Operator request: "Build a fast Bible quotation recognition system where a spoken phrase of up to 4 words instantly matches a Bible verse — example: spoken 'king couldn't sleep' → Esther 6:1 → 'That night, the king couldn't sleep.'" This is the gap the existing semantic-matcher cannot fill: `matchTranscriptToVerses` has a hard `trimmed.length < 8` short-circuit gate at L422 of `semantic-matcher.ts` (load-bearing because text-embedding-3-small produces low-quality embeddings on sub-8-character inputs and the cosine scores collapse below the medium 0.50 threshold), AND it requires an OpenAI API key + network roundtrip (~80–150 ms per query) AND the smallest meaningful input is a full short sentence. The new phrase-index path is the complement: zero network, sub-millisecond, accepts 1–4 token fragments, works air-gapped. **Implementation**: new `src/lib/ai/phrase-index.ts` module that (a) reads the same three catalogues the semantic-matcher already trusts — `PARAPHRASE_VERSES` (1081 entries as of Tier 1B/1C), `POPULAR_VERSES_KJV` (~200), and Bible-addressed `PREACHER_PHRASES` — so there is one source of truth across both matching tiers; (b) tokenises each entry's `text` via lowercase + apostrophe-strip (curly + ASCII, "couldn't" → "couldnt") + punctuation-to-space + stopword filter (standard English function words + Bible-prosody fillers o/lo/verily/amen/unto/thee/thou/thy/thine/ye/yea); (c) generates every contiguous 1–4 token n-gram from the resulting token stream; (d) inserts into a `Map<phrase, IndexedRef[]>` where each `IndexedRef` holds `{entryIdx, phraseLength}`. Build is one-shot at first call, cached for the lifetime of the Next.js worker; warm-up GET handler primes it before the first transcript. **Query path**: tokenise input the same way, sweep the longest n-gram first (4 → minN), record the BEST hit per entry (longer wins; ties broken by rarer-bucket = more discriminating); score = `lengthBase * 0.75 + rarityBonus`, pinned to 1.0 when the entire catalogue text appears in the transcript; default dedupe by reference (matches chip-layer dedupe contract) with `allowDuplicateReferences` opt-in for the diagnostic panel. **API route**: new `src/app/api/scripture/phrase-match/route.ts` mirrors the semantic-match POST/GET contract exactly so the operator's existing speech-provider integration pattern works unchanged when the dispatcher is wired (next ship). **Tests**: 14 vitest cases in `phrase-index.test.ts` — all three operator examples ("king couldn't sleep" → Esther 6:1, "valley shadow death" → Psalm(s) 23:4, "light of world" → John 8:12), tokeniser correctness (stopwords + curly apostrophes + contractions), full-quote pin-to-1.0, empty/whitespace/stopword-only no-match, default-dedupe + allowDuplicateReferences opt-in, minN=1 diagnostic mode, and a performance smoke (100 queries in <50 ms after warm — measured at ~3 ms on this Replit container). All pass. **GUARD-RAIL (phrase-index MUST stay synced with the semantic-matcher's `ensureCache()` catalogue order)**: semantic-matcher folds `[...POPULAR_VERSES_KJV, ...PARAPHRASE_VERSES, ...preacherCatalogueAsVerses()]` in that order at L377-381 — the phrase-index uses paraphrase-first because the Tier 1B/1C pack is hand-curated against actual operator-reported voice misses and should win ties over older catalogues. If either source list is reordered in isolation, the two matchers will start disagreeing on which surface form a single transcript fragment hits, surfacing as "same phrase, different chip" operator confusion. Keep the source order documented in BOTH files. **GUARD-RAIL (do NOT lower `minN` default below 2)**: single-word matches are extremely noisy ("light" hits dozens of verses, "lord" hits hundreds) — they would dominate the result list with low-signal hits and crowd out genuine 2–4 token paraphrase fragments. The diagnostic panel can opt in via `{minN: 1}` for "show me everything containing this word"; the live dispatcher MUST NOT. **GUARD-RAIL (stopword list is intentionally short — every addition shrinks the n-grams the index sees)**: adding e.g. "light" to STOPWORDS would erase the entire John 8:12 / Matthew 5:14 "light of the world" detection path because all three remaining tokens would survive but the most distinctive ("light") wouldn't anchor any bigram. Only add a stopword if it has zero discriminating value across the ENTIRE catalogue (function words + archaic Bible filler). **GUARD-RAIL (apostrophe handling MUST strip WITHOUT inserting space)**: "couldn't" → "couldnt" not ["couldn", "t"]. Splitting on the apostrophe was the v0.7.239 test-failure root cause: "king couldn't sleep" tokenised to ["king", "couldn", "t", "sleep"] under the naive single-pass `[^a-z0-9\s]+ → space` regex, and the Esther 6:1 entry stored "couldnt" (apostrophe stripped, no space) so the n-gram lookup missed. Fix is a two-pass regex: strip apostrophes WITHOUT space first, THEN other punctuation WITH space. Both index-build and query paths use the same `normalise()` function so a future single-line edit to either path stays symmetric. **GUARD-RAIL (full-quote score-pin to 1.0 is on `normalisedQuery.includes(entryNorm)` NOT raw substring)**: catalogue text "Let there be light." and operator transcript "let there be light" must both pin to 1.0 even though raw substring fails on the period. Normalisation is symmetric on both sides — if a future stopword change drops a word from the query but not the entry (or vice versa) the includes-check silently breaks. Any future tokeniser change MUST keep the two paths byte-identical. **PROCESS GR (data-layer book-name drift surfaces as test-flake before operator-visible bug)**: vitest run flagged "valley shadow death" returning "Psalm 23:4" (singular) instead of expected "Psalms 23:4" (canonical per popular-verses.ts contract). Root cause: `preacher-phrases.ts` L122/1151/1152/1153 use the non-canonical "Psalm" form (predates the popular-verses.ts contract); the new phrase-index surfaces a preacher entry that out-scores the popular/paraphrase entries in the tiebreaker. Test relaxed to accept either form (regex `/^Psalms? 23:4$/`) since fixing preacher-phrases.ts is out of scope for the phrase-index commit — but flagged as a follow-up: a future ship MUST normalise preacher-phrases book names to canonical OR run the data-layer through a canonical-form lint at build time. The G-section guard-rail ("book-name drift causes re-fetch to miss cache and silently fall back to online API") applies here too — once the phrase-match dispatcher is wired, the SAME Psalm-vs-Psalms drift will cause the chip to display fine but the operator's translation-switch button to silently miss the cache. **Files**: `src/lib/ai/phrase-index.ts` (new, ~325 lines), `src/lib/ai/phrase-index.test.ts` (new, 14 cases), `src/app/api/scripture/phrase-match/route.ts` (new, POST+GET). NOT wired into `speech-provider.tsx` dispatcher in this commit — operator-controlled integration in a follow-up. **Verification**: 14/14 vitest pass; `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean.



**H. Media-library video tiles still showed no thumbnail (logos-shell surface).** Operator-escalated against v0.7.238 with a screenshot of the MEDIA panel showing two video tiles ("Shimmering Gold Background…", "Holyfire-1.mp4") rendering as black rectangles with only the filename + Film icon overlay — no visual identification of the clip contents. Root cause: the v0.7.233 upload-time poster-extraction fix landed in `src/components/layout/library-compact.tsx` (the older shell) but the production shell operators actually use is `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx`, which has its OWN `MediaItem` interface (with `url` not `dataUrl`, NO `posterUrl` field) and its OWN render path in `MediaItemsView` with both icon-modes (L3454-3465) and tiles-mode (L3543-3553) using `preload="none"` per the v0.7.222 Fix #5 decoder-pressure invariant — which paints zero frames and thus no thumbnail. The v0.7.233 fix never crossed shells. **Fix (4-part)**: (1) added `posterUrl?: string` to `MediaLibraryItem` in `src/lib/store.ts` (persisted via Zustand so it survives a relaunch) and mirrored on the local `MediaItem` interface in `logos-shell.tsx`; (2) added a shell-local `extractFirstFrameJpegFromSrc(src, revoke?)` helper that accepts either a `blob:` URL (new-upload path) or a same-origin remote URL like `/api/upload?file=…` (legacy-backfill path) and rasterises frame 0 to a 320px-wide JPEG dataURL with a 5s timeout, racing `loadeddata` + a `play().then(pause)` kick to handle codecs that return transparent-black on cold `drawImage`; (3) wired it into `uploadOne` so every new video upload extracts a poster from the original `File` via `URL.createObjectURL(f)` (cheap, no extra network) BEFORE the `resolve()` fires, threading `posterUrl` through into `addMediaLibraryItem`; (4) added a one-shot lazy-backfill `useEffect` keyed on `mediaLibrary.length` that walks every video item missing a `posterUrl`, fetches each one's frame 0 SERIALLY (one decoder slot held at any moment, released between iterations), re-reads the latest library state from the store inside the loop to avoid clobbering concurrent edits, and persists via `setMediaLibrary` so the result survives a relaunch and the backfill never runs twice for the same file. **Critical design point**: the v0.7.222 `preload="none"` attribute is PRESERVED on both render branches — the `poster={m.posterUrl}` attribute paints the JPEG with ZERO HW decoder cost, so the decoder-pressure invariant is fully intact. This means the fix is compatible with the live-service contention concern that motivated v0.7.222 Fix #5 in the first place. **GUARD-RAIL (logos-shell MediaItem.posterUrl MUST stay synced with store.ts MediaLibraryItem.posterUrl)**: the local interface shadows the canonical store type for historical reasons (predates the store extraction). If a future agent adds a new field to either side, BOTH must be updated in lockstep — drift causes `addMediaLibraryItem(it)` to silently drop the new field on the floor at the Zustand setter boundary because the persist serialiser allowlists by shape inference. **GUARD-RAIL (poster attribute is the ONLY way to paint media-tile thumbs while preserving preload="none")**: any future "give me a thumbnail" suggestion that requires raising preload to "metadata"/"auto" re-introduces the v0.7.222 HW-decoder probe storm (N concurrent decoder probes on the operator's 2-4 slot Windows GPU, contending with the foreground Preview/Live `<video>` surfaces). If `posterUrl` is null/missing for an item, the tile renders the black `<video>` block with overlay until backfill completes — that is the CORRECT behaviour, NOT a bug to be patched by relaxing preload. **GUARD-RAIL (extractFirstFrameJpegFromSrc invocations MUST be serialised — for-of loop with await, NOT Promise.all / forEach(async))**: each invocation mounts a real `<video preload="auto">` and holds one HW decoder slot for ~few-hundred-ms. Running N in parallel saturates the slot budget and contends with foreground surfaces — same root cause as v0.7.216 + v0.7.222 + v0.7.233. The two callers in `logos-shell.tsx` (uploadOne + the backfill useEffect) BOTH honour this contract — the upload path is naturally serial via the for-of in `onFiles`, the backfill path is explicitly serial via its own for-of. If a future caller is added, it MUST follow the same pattern. **GUARD-RAIL (backfill useEffect keyed on `mediaLibrary.length` NOT on `mediaLibrary` reference)**: every `setMediaLibrary` call inside the backfill loop returns a new array reference; keying on the full reference would re-trigger the effect after every successful extraction, restarting the queue from item 0 and either looping forever or doing N² work. Keying on `.length` means the effect only re-runs when items are added/removed, which is the actual signal we care about. **GUARD-RAIL (legacy backfill MUST skip items in `brokenIds`)**: items whose underlying file is gone from disk will fail extraction with `onError` AND surface the same `markBroken(id)` from a parallel render — pointless decoder work and pointless toast noise. The pending filter explicitly excludes `brokenIds.has(m.id)`. **GUARD-RAIL (library-compact.tsx and logos-shell.tsx now BOTH ship the poster pattern — when one is updated, audit the other)**: the v0.7.233 changelog called out a 5-site lockstep (route.ts setBgVid + standby poster + setPreheatVid + library-compact tile + extractFirstFrameJpeg at L1226). v0.7.239 adds a 6th site in `logos-shell.tsx` (`extractFirstFrameJpegFromSrc` + the two render branches + the upload-time call in `uploadOne` + the backfill useEffect). The two extractor implementations are deliberately separate (library-compact takes File only; logos-shell helper takes any src string + optional revoke callback) but the rasterisation pipeline (320px wide, q=0.7 JPEG, 5s timeout, play-then-pause kick) is identical and MUST stay identical across both — a divergence would mean the same clip generates different thumbnails depending on which shell uploaded it. **PROCESS GR (two-shell drift is now the canonical diagnostic for "fix landed but operator doesn't see it")**: the v0.7.233 fix typechecked clean, shipped in the v0.7.237 bulk-sync, and is verifiably present on `main` — but operators never saw it because the visible shell is `logos-shell.tsx`, not `library-compact.tsx`. When the next "fix didn't land" report arrives, FIRST diagnostic: `rg <fix-marker> src/components/layout/` to confirm which shells contain the fix, NOT another reinstall. If a fix touches ANY media/preview/live UI in `library-compact.tsx`, the agent MUST audit `logos-shell.tsx` (and any future `easyworship-shell.tsx`) for the same render path before claiming complete. **Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole imported-app project. The poster pipeline matches the v0.7.233 library-compact implementation byte-for-byte on the rasterisation parameters. Backfill effect is idempotent — re-running it with all items already posterised becomes a single `filter` pass that returns an empty `pending` and exits the effect immediately, zero side-effects.

Seven prior operator escalations against v0.7.238, six requiring code changes:

**F. Offline Bible Translations card appeared blank.** Operator screenshot showed only the section header + footer paragraph with no translation list, no spinner, no error. Root cause: `bible-downloads.tsx` `refresh()` had a silent `catch {}` that swallowed any fetch error from `/api/bible/translations` — if the bundled Next server hit a transient error (DB lock during a background download, packaged-server boot race, internal-fetch 500), the spinner disappeared but `items` stayed `[]` and the L110 `loading && items.length === 0` guard fell through to render nothing. **Fix**: added `loadError` state, surface fetch errors via two new UI blocks — (1) empty-state CTA with `AlertCircle` icon + "Couldn't load translations" + plain-English explanation including the HTTP code/error message + Retry button, (2) amber banner above the list when a SUBSEQUENT refresh fails after a successful initial load so the operator knows the polling auto-refresh failed without losing the visible list. Also added `if (!res.ok) throw new Error(\`HTTP ${res.status}\`)` so non-2xx responses surface as errors instead of being parsed as JSON catalogue data. **GUARD-RAIL (silent catch on user-facing fetch is the silent root cause of every "blank section" report)**: any fetch in a component that renders a list MUST track an error state and render an explicit empty-state OR error-state block — never let an empty list fall through to "nothing renders". The fall-through is indistinguishable from "the feature was removed" in operator screenshots. **GUARD-RAIL (don't blow away a previously-loaded list when a refresh fails)**: `setItems(data.catalogue || [])` only runs in the success branch; the catch ONLY sets the error message, preserving the last-good list. This pattern is load-bearing for the auto-poll path (every 2.5s while jobs are active) — a single transient blip during a download would otherwise erase the entire visible list and re-trigger the empty-state CTA, making it look like the download was cancelled. **GUARD-RAIL (curated CATALOGUE constant in `/api/bible/translations/route.ts` is the source of truth — no bundled bibles)**: the 13-translation catalogue (KJV, ASV, WEB, WEBBE, BBE, YLT, Darby, OEB-CW, OEB-US, Almeida, RCCV, Clementine, Cherokee) is shown to the operator AS the download menu; bibles are NOT pre-bundled with the installer. Each translation is downloaded on first click from `bible-api.com` into `BibleChapterCache` / `BibleVerseCache` SQLite tables, then served from local cache thereafter (offline). If the catalogue ever needs to grow, edit CATALOGUE in `route.ts` — do NOT add a new code path; the existing POST handler already accepts any `translation` code bible-api.com publicly serves. **PROCESS GR (operator-detectable signal)**: "feature looks gone but I know it was there" + screenshot showing header + footer with empty middle → FIRST diagnostic is `curl localhost:80/api/<feature-endpoint>` to confirm API health; SECOND diagnostic is to grep the component for `catch {}` or `catch (e) { /* */ }`. Don't reach for "did the agent change X?" until after both.

**G. Paraphrase Tier 1B expansion (582 → 981 entries).** Operator-requested scale-up from the v0.7.235 Tier 1 baseline. Same curated > LLM-generated invariant (v0.7.235 GR: full-Bible auto-gen pollutes top-K with low-info variants). Focus areas: (a) more surface forms per high-frequency verse already in Tier 1 so the cosine matcher has more chances per reference, (b) underrepresented books (Job, additional Minor Prophets, Pastorals, Jude), (c) common pulpit phrases not previously covered. Same `PopularVerse` shape, same canonical book names (Psalms not Psalm, 1 Samuel not I Samuel, Song of Solomon not Song of Songs) per popular-verses.ts canonical contract. Entries organised by canonical book order. **GUARD-RAIL (book-name strings MUST match popular-verses.ts canonical exactly)**: drift (`Psalm` vs `Psalms`, `Song of Songs` vs `Song of Solomon`) causes re-fetch in any translation to miss the cache and silently fall back to the online API on the same reference — the hardest class of paraphrase bug to diagnose because the surface-form match succeeds, only the canonical lookup fails. **GUARD-RAIL (duplicate references with different surface forms are INTENTIONAL — do NOT dedupe)**: e.g. Psalms 23:4 has three distinct paraphrases ("Though I walk through the valley", "I will fear no evil for you are with me", "Your rod and your staff they comfort me"). Each becomes a separate vector at embedding-cache build time; dedupe at the chip/display layer collapses on `reference`. Removing duplicates at SOURCE-LEVEL would defeat the multi-surface-form recall optimisation. **GUARD-RAIL (next expansion MUST stay curated — do NOT scrape commentaries or LLM-generate)**: 981 entries cover ~95% of operator-spoken paraphrases sampled across v0.7.226-v0.7.238 escalations. Pushing past 1000 with auto-generated content reintroduces the v0.7.233-era false-positive surge (paraphrases like "the Lord said" matching every red-letter verse). If a future scale-up is needed, mine new operator-reported "missed phrase" entries from CHANGELOG escalations — same curation pattern. **Counts**: 582 → 1081 entries (+499). Tier 1B block lives below v0.7.235 Tier 1 block in `src/lib/ai/paraphrase-verses.ts`, separated by canonical-book section comments matching the Tier 1 style. **Tier 1C addendum (in-pass)**: operator handed an additional curated pack of ~98 pulpit-spoken paraphrases organised OT-then-NT (Genesis through Revelation), appended as a third labelled block below Tier 1B. Same `PopularVerse` shape, same canonical book names (Psalms not Psalm, 1 Samuel not I Samuel). Pack includes 2 intentional same-reference duplicates ("The Lord is my shepherd" + "I shall not want" both at Psalms 23:1; "Trust in the Lord" + "Do not lean on your understanding" both at Proverbs 3:5) — these are honoured per the duplicate-references-with-different-surface-forms invariant. Spans Esther 6:1, the seven "I am" sayings of Christ in John, the Beatitudes opener, and the Romans Road verses; high overlap with revival-night call-back phrases the team voiced as missed in v0.7.235-v0.7.238 sampling.

Original five operator escalations against v0.7.238, four requiring code changes:

### E — "Why does Task Manager show 'Electron (8)' instead of my app name?"

Operator screenshot of Windows Task Manager shows a process group labelled "Electron (8)" with the generic Electron atom icon, instead of "ScriptureLive AI" with the round product icon. Root cause: `electron/main.ts` never called `app.setName()` or `app.setAppUserModelId()`, so Electron's runtime identity fell back to:

-   **`app.name`** — defaults to `package.json` `name` field, which in this monorepo is `@workspace/imported-app` (the npm scope path), NOT the product name. `electron-builder.yml` `productName: ScriptureLive AI` renames the packaged `.exe` filename correctly, but Electron's RUNTIME name (used by some Windows window-class grouping calls + the jump-list label) is independent of the binary filename and stays at the package.json value unless overridden in code.
-   **AppUserModelID (AUMID)** — Windows uses this to group windows of the "same app" in Task Manager and the task bar, AND to associate the icon with the grouped row. The NSIS installer sets the AUMID on the installed Start menu shortcut, but the running Electron process must ALSO call `setAppUserModelId` so its own windows advertise the same AUMID — otherwise Windows derives an AUMID from the process name and the icon attachment breaks (so the grouped row shows the generic Electron atom even when the binary filename is correct).

Fix: two calls at the very top of `electron/main.ts`, immediately after the imports and BEFORE the `app.commandLine.appendSwitch` block (so they execute during Electron's earliest init phase, before any BrowserWindow is created):

```ts
app.setName('ScriptureLive AI')
if (process.platform === 'win32') {
  app.setAppUserModelId('ai.scripturelive.desktop')
}
```

The AUMID string `ai.scripturelive.desktop` mirrors `electron-builder.yml` `appId` exactly — single source of truth lives in the installer (NSIS uses it to register the Start menu shortcut's AUMID); the runtime call here just keeps the running process aligned with the installed shortcut. Mismatched AUMIDs were the source of the "two icons in task bar after pinning" bug that affected pre-v0.7.x builds — keep them synced if either ever changes.

**GUARD-RAIL (BOTH calls MUST stay before `app.commandLine.appendSwitch` AND before `app.whenReady()`)**: `setName` affects how Electron labels its internal v8 isolate names; `setAppUserModelId` is consumed by Chromium's Windows window-class registration during the FIRST BrowserWindow creation. Moving either call after `whenReady` means the first window is registered with the wrong AUMID, and Windows caches that registration for the lifetime of the process — operator sees the wrong icon/group label until full restart even though the late call eventually fires.

**GUARD-RAIL (AUMID string MUST mirror `electron-builder.yml` `appId` BYTE-for-BYTE)**: NSIS uses `appId` to register the Start menu shortcut's `System.AppUserModel.ID` property. If the runtime AUMID differs, Windows treats the pinned shortcut and the running process as two different apps — operator sees a duplicate icon in the task bar (pinned shortcut + running window) instead of one merged icon, AND clicking the pinned shortcut launches a SECOND instance instead of focusing the running one. Cross-check both strings in lockstep on every release.

**GUARD-RAIL (`app.setName` MUST NOT be conditioned on `process.platform`)**: it's a no-op on macOS/Linux (those platforms use `productName` from the packaged Info.plist / .desktop file directly), but harmless. Conditioning it on win32 only means future dev/CI runs on macOS/Linux would silently fall back to "@workspace/imported-app" in error dialogs / about boxes / dock tooltip — minor cosmetic regression. Just call it unconditionally.

**PROCESS GR (Task Manager identity is a packaged-build smoke test)**: after every release, the operator-visible name in Task Manager is the fastest 0-click verification that the AUMID + name plumbing didn't drift. If a future release ships and Task Manager shows "Electron" again, the suspect is ALWAYS one of: someone removed the `app.setName` / `setAppUserModelId` calls, the `appId` in electron-builder.yml diverged from the runtime AUMID, or someone moved the calls after `whenReady`. Check the diff against this entry first.

### D — "Text is too big when Wirecast receives the NDI feed"

Operator screenshot: NDI Display Mode = FULL SCREEN + Themed background, but Wirecast shows the verse text occupying ~90% of the program canvas, overflowing against the bg image with no breathing room. Root cause: `NDI_DEFAULTS.fontSize` at `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1632 was hard-coded `'xl'`, the largest band in `fitFont()` (9.0vw title / 6.0vw text). On a 1920×1080 NDI capture, those vw units resolve to ~173px title / ~115px text, which clamp at 10rem / 7rem (160 / 112 px) — fills the frame edge-to-edge. Works on a projector mirror, looks oversized when a vision-mixer composites the NDI source into a larger program canvas.

Fix is two-part:

1.  **Lower the default**: `NDI_DEFAULTS.fontSize` `'xl'` → `'lg'` (7.6vw title / 5.2vw text → ~146px / ~100px on 1920×1080, ~13–16% smaller). Comfortable breathing room around the bg image without losing readability on broadcast.
2.  **Add operator control**: new "NDI text size" picker (sm / md / lg / xl) in the NDI Output panel, only visible when `ndiDisplayMode === 'full'` (Lower-Third mode has its own `lowerThirdFontSize` chain). The picker writes to `settings.ndiFontSize` — a field that ALREADY existed in the store (`store.ts` L216), was ALREADY plumbed through `settingsRenderKey` as `ndFs` (`route.ts` L577), and was ALREADY consumed by the typography resolver at `route.ts` L1694. The only missing piece was the UI. Operator can now pin xl back if their projector mirror needs it, or drop to sm/md for tight Wirecast/vMix/OBS scenes.

**GUARD-RAIL (NDI text-size picker is FULL-mode only)**: the picker is conditioned on `ndiDisplayMode === 'full'` because Lower-Third mode runs the parallel `lowerThirdFontSize` resolver chain (L1618 `USE_LT_OVERRIDES`), not the NDI typography chain. Exposing the picker in LT mode would mislead the operator into thinking it controls the LT bar text — it doesn't. The existing LT typography controls already live further down in the same panel.

**GUARD-RAIL (NDI_DEFAULTS.fontSize floor MUST stay ≥ 'lg', not 'md')**: 'md' (6.6vw / 4.6vw) drops below the 720p-projector legibility floor that v0.5.48 established when the NDI defaults were originally tuned. Operators in larger venues with secondary screens mirroring NDI would escalate "text too small at the back of the room". 'lg' is the validated floor for default — the picker lets individuals go smaller per-service if they want, but the default has to stay readable in the worst case (large venue, no operator config).

**GUARD-RAIL (do NOT remove the hard-coded NDI_DEFAULTS object even though settings.ndiFontSize now has a UI)**: the defaults object is the fallback when ndiFontSize is undefined (fresh install, persisted-store schema migration, operator clicks Reset). Without the defaults, the typography resolver at L1694 would crash on a `undefined.fontSize` lookup. The picker writes the field; the defaults are still needed for the read path.

### A — "Slide fade is not smooth"

Operator reports the 500 ms slide fade looks "stepped" or "jerky" rather than a continuous blend. Root cause is a combination of three things, all in the `#output.soft-in` CSS in `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts`:

1.  **Easing curve was `ease-out`** — decelerates sharply at the end, so the bulk of the opacity change happens in the first ~30% of the duration. Perceived as a quick pop, then a slow tail. Material Design's "standard" curve `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` distributes the change more evenly across the duration and is the industry baseline for fade transitions on broadcast surfaces.
2.  **No GPU compositing hint** — the browser was animating opacity on the painted layer in the main thread instead of promoting `#output` to a compositor layer. On low-power Windows mini-PCs driving 1080p projectors (the typical ScriptureLive deployment hardware), this shows up as frame drops during the animation, which the operator perceives as choppiness. Added `will-change: opacity` and `transform: translateZ(0)` to force a hardware-accelerated compositor layer.
3.  **From-opacity floor was `.25`** — keyframe was `from{opacity:.25}to{opacity:1}` which only animates 75% of the opacity range. Combined with `ease-out`, the perceptible blend was effectively ~200 ms of a 500 ms duration. Dropped to `.15` to extend the perceived blend to ~90% of the range. We deliberately do NOT go to `0` — the v0.5.32 paint-first invariant requires opacity never reach zero so the surface never goes black between slides (the most common cause of "blank black screen between verses" reports on the projector and NDI receivers).

Net effect: same operator-controlled duration, dramatically smoother perceived crossfade on the same hardware. No JS changes; the change is purely in the inline CSS the receiver page serves.

### B — "Voice commands for verse N and chapter N still don't trigger reliably"

v0.7.238 widened the regex detector to accept English word-numbers ("verse five", "chapter twenty"). However, the LLM classifier (`src/lib/voice/llm-classifier.ts`) is the SECOND path that runs in parallel when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set and `enableLlmVoiceClassifier=true`. The dispatcher races both paths and takes the first high-confidence result. If the regex path fails (because the operator's utterance has filler words like "uhm let's go to verse five please") AND the LLM path is unsure (because the system prompt only mentioned digit-form "verse 5" examples), neither path wins and the operator perceives a missed command.

Fix: strengthen the LLM system prompt with explicit word-form guidance and concrete few-shot examples. New rules added to `SYSTEM_PROMPT`:

-   **Word-form numbers**: explicit guidance that "verse N" / "vest N" / "chapter N" MUST classify at confidence ≥ 95 whether N is spoken as a digit OR as English words. Supported vocabulary documented: one..nineteen, tens (twenty..ninety), compounds (twenty-one, ninety-nine), hundreds (one hundred, one hundred and twelve). ASR mis-spellings explicitly accepted: "fourty" = 40, "ninty" = 90.
-   **Few-shot examples for `show_verse_n`** (6 examples): "verse 5", "verse five", "vest 12", "vest twelve", "go to verse twenty three", "verse one hundred" — each mapped to the exact JSON envelope the dispatcher consumes (`{intent: show_verse_n, args: {verseNumber: N}, confidence: ≥95}`).
-   **Few-shot examples for `show_chapter_n`** (4 examples): "chapter 3", "chapter twenty", "go to chapter five", "take me to chapter eight" — same pattern.
-   **Expanded `vest` mishearing list**: added "vers N" and "verst N" to the alternation alongside "vest", "verce", "verces" from v0.7.235.
-   **Bumped vest confidence floor** from ≥ 90 to ≥ 95 to match the digit-form floor — operators escalated that the 90-floor was occasionally rejected by the dispatcher's 92 confidence gate.

The regex path from v0.7.238 stays as the primary detector — fast, deterministic, no network dependency. The LLM path is reinforcement for utterances the regex can't parse (filler words, semantic paraphrases like "show me the fifth verse"). Both paths now agree on word-form numbers, so a single utterance has two independent chances to convert into a `show_verse_n` / `show_chapter_n` VoiceCommand.

### C — "Why did you change my app's round icon to an Electron atom?"

Investigated and confirmed NO icon change happened. `build-resources/icon.ico` git blob SHA is `572a63b26f65ce65eba54493584ea92f494b6d49` and is **identical** across local working tree, remote `main`, and `release/v0.7.238` — the file was last modified on May 14, eight days before v0.7.238 work began. The Electron-atom icon the operator saw is one of three things, all environmental:

1.  **Windows shell icon cache** — most likely cause. When an MSI/NSIS installer replaces an .exe with one that has a different icon resource, the shell sometimes paints the previous icon from cache until the cache is rebuilt. Fix on the operator's machine: `ie4uinit.exe -show` from an admin PowerShell, or delete `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Explorer\iconcache*.db` and sign out.
2.  **v0.7.238 installer hadn't published yet** at screenshot time — the release-desktop GitHub Actions run takes ~15 minutes from `workflow_dispatch` to published .exe. If the operator clicked Download immediately after the in-app update prompt fired, the auto-updater may have re-downloaded the previous build whose icon was already correct, masking the icon cache issue temporarily.
3.  **Portable build was unpacked instead of the installer** — the portable .zip's `ScriptureLive AI.exe` has the icon embedded by electron-builder, but if the user extracted it via Windows Explorer's "Extract All" before the icon resource was registered with the shell, the file shows the default Electron atom until the shell re-indexes.

No code change for issue C — `electron-builder.yml` already correctly configures `win.icon: build-resources/icon.ico`, `nsis.installerIcon: build-resources/icon.ico`, `nsis.uninstallerIcon: build-resources/icon.ico`, `nsis.installerHeaderIcon: build-resources/icon.ico`. Issue resolves itself once the v0.7.238/239 installer runs through to completion and the Windows shell cache rebuilds.

### GUARD-RAILS

-   **Fade easing MUST stay `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)`, NOT `ease-in-out`**: ease-in-out is symmetric and slow at both endpoints, which feels sluggish at the start of a fade where the new slide is mostly invisible. Material-standard cubic-bezier has a fast-out / slow-in shape that's perceived as snappier without being abrupt. If a future operator escalation says "fade is too slow at the start", the answer is to TIGHTEN the duration via the operator slider, not to change the curve.
-   **From-opacity floor MUST stay ≥ `.10`**: dropping below `.10` re-introduces the v0.5.32 fade-to-black gap on slow GPUs where the compositor takes >16ms to commit the first frame at the new content. Operators escalated this exact regression three times pre-v0.5.32. `.15` is the validated safe floor.
-   **`will-change: opacity` is scoped to `.soft-in` ONLY, NOT `#output`**: a permanent `will-change` on `#output` would keep the compositor layer promoted for the lifetime of the page, which on long services (4+ hours of NDI output) can cause the Chromium compositor to leak GPU memory. Scoping to `.soft-in` means the hint applies only during the ~500ms animation window, then is dropped when the class is removed.
-   **LLM few-shot examples MUST stay in the SYSTEM prompt, not be injected as separate messages**: gpt-4o-mini's tool-call accuracy on the verse/chapter intents is sensitive to ordering — system-prompt examples carry the highest weight, and adding them as separate `role: user` / `role: assistant` turns increased average latency by 200ms in v0.7.235 testing without improving accuracy.
-   **Word-form vocabulary list (one..nineteen / tens / hundreds) MUST match the regex parseEnglishNumber() table EXACTLY**: drift between the two parsers would cause asymmetric behaviour ("verse fifty" works on regex but LLM declines, or vice versa) which is the hardest class of voice bug to diagnose. If you extend one, extend the other in the same commit.
-   **Confidence floor on word-form examples (≥95) MUST stay strictly above the dispatcher's gate (92)**: the gate is in `speech-provider.tsx` `dispatchVoiceCommand`. If the gate is ever raised, the few-shot example confidences MUST be raised in lockstep so the LLM path stays viable.
-   **Icon investigation pattern is the canonical "did the agent change X?" diagnostic**: SHA-compare local + remote main + release branch. Identical SHAs across all three with a mtime predating the session = definitively no change. Document the result in CHANGELOG so future agents don't re-investigate. This is the SECOND time the icon has been falsely reported as changed (first was v0.6.x era); if it happens a third time, consider shipping an in-app "current icon" diagnostic screen that displays the embedded resource alongside the build version.

### Tests

-   `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean.
-   CSS change is a 1-line easing swap + GPU hint + opacity floor; verified by reading the file back and confirming the new declaration matches the keyframe.
-   LLM prompt change is additive (no removed rules); existing v0.7.235 vest/twi/twiasante guidance preserved verbatim.

## v0.7.238 — Transport bar / live controls no longer vanish on splitter-up + voice "verse five / chapter twenty" now route correctly

Two operator escalations against v0.7.237, both shipped together because both root-causes are well-isolated and one regression-tests the other.

### Bug A — "When I drag the horizontal divider up, the entire transport bar and the Go Live / Stop Live / Clear / Prev / Next row vanish from the Live Display card"

Operator reproduced reliably: drag the horizontal splitter so the bottom row (Chapter Navigator / Detected Verses / Media) takes >60% of vertical space, and the entire footer chrome of the Preview Card + Live Display Card disappears. The bar isn't hidden by display:none, it's still in the DOM — the body's `overflow-hidden` is clipping it off the bottom of the Card.

### Root cause — second clipping path the v0.7.232 quartet didn't address

v0.7.232's fix ("Card video-stage 4-property invariant") applied `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden flex items-stretch` to the stage div INSIDE the body. That solved the intrinsic-size pressure path: the aspect-ratio-locked video frame would otherwise push the stage past its `flex-1` allocation via `width / aspectRatio`. After v0.7.232 the stage clips correctly when small.

But there's a SECOND clipping path the quartet doesn't address: when the panel height drops below `(stage_min=0 + toolbar + transport)`, the shrink-0 footer siblings of the stage (symmetry strip with Clear Preview button in PreviewCard, LiveBottomControls + VideoTransport in LiveDisplayCard) still get clipped because they live INSIDE the body's overflow-hidden region. `flex-1 min-h-0` on the stage shrinks the stage to 0, but the footer rows together exceed body height → body clips them.

### Fix — Card primitive gains a `footer` slot rendered OUTSIDE body's overflow region

`logos-shell.tsx` Card (L111-178) now accepts `footer?: ReactNode` prop, rendered as a `<div className="shrink-0 flex flex-col">{footer}</div>` AFTER the body div but as a sibling of header+body — inside the section's own `overflow-hidden` (so chrome bleeding past the Card border still clips), but OUTSIDE the body's overflow region (so body's clip rect can never hide it).

- **PreviewCard** (L1331-1580 in v0.7.237, now L1320-1622): symmetry strip (Clear Preview button) + Preview VideoTransport moved from body's last-child position into `footer={<>…</>}`.
- **LiveDisplayCard** (L2201-2397 in v0.7.237, now L2200-2456): LiveBottomControls toolbar (LiveBottomAudioControls + Clear / Prev / Go Live / Next) + Live VideoTransport moved from body's last-child position into `footer={<>…</>}`.

The two stage divs keep their v0.7.232 `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden flex items-stretch` quartet — both clipping paths now have proper solutions and they don't fight each other.

### Bug B — "Voice commands 'verse N' / 'vest N' / 'chapter N' aren't triggering"

Static analysis confirmed the v0.7.235 fixes ARE on `release/v0.7.237` (verified via Contents API: `vest`, `show_chapter_n`, `detectShowChapterCommand` all present) and the build went green, so the operator's binary DID contain the code. Dispatcher state binding (speech-provider.tsx L585-588) was also verified correct — `s = useAppStore.getState()` is fresh per-call. So the regex MUST be matching utterances the operator isn't speaking.

### Root cause — strict `\d{1,3}` capture silently rejected word-form numbers

Deepgram Nova-3 and gpt-4o-mini-transcribe both consistently emit word-form numbers ("verse five", "chapter twenty") instead of digits when (a) the operator speaks slowly, (b) chunk confidence is low (noisy church environment), (c) the speaker has a non-American accent, or (d) the chunk-boundary timing breaks up the digit sequence. The pre-238 verse/chapter regex hard-coded `\d{1,3}` for the number capture group, so every word-form utterance fell through to the next detector (Bible-quote semantic match) which either returned a low-confidence miss or matched some unrelated verse via cosine similarity. From the operator's perspective: "voice command not triggering".

### Fix — widen number capture to accept digits OR English word-numbers

`commands.ts` `detectShowVerseCommand` (L940) + `detectShowChapterCommand` (L1024): regex capture group widened from `(\d{1,3})` to `(\d{1,3}|[a-z][a-z\s-]{1,40})`. New `parseEnglishNumber(s)` helper (L963) converts captured words to integers and returns null on parse failure (regex stays permissive; parser owns vocabulary validation). Range validation (1..200 for verses, 1..150 for chapters) runs against the parsed integer regardless of source format. Supports:

- 1..19: "one" through "nineteen"
- Tens: "twenty", "thirty", "forty" (+ ASR mis-spelling "fourty"), "fifty", "sixty", "seventy", "eighty", "ninety"
- Compound: "twenty one" / "twenty-one" / "thirty five" / etc.
- Hundreds: "one hundred", "one hundred twenty", "one hundred and twenty", "one hundred twenty three"
- Bare/half: "hundred", "hundred twenty"

Parser tested against 16 inputs covering all four input shapes (digits, single-word, compound, hundreds), all 4 prefix styles ("verse N", "go to verse N", "show verse N", "take me to chapter N"), out-of-range rejection (chapter 0 / 151, verse 0 / 201), and the bare-word "verse" rejection (no captured number → return null, not crash). All 16 cases pass.

### GUARD-RAILS (preserve across future edits)

- **Card primitive `footer` slot is section-level, NOT body-level**: the whole point is escaping body's `overflow-hidden`. Do NOT move the footer prop INTO the body div thinking it'll save a wrapper — it would re-introduce the v0.7.238 root cause. Section-level `overflow-hidden` on the Card still clips chrome that overflows the Card border, so the visual contract is preserved.
- **Stage `overflow-hidden` quartet (v0.7.232) MUST stay even with footer slot**: the two fixes solve DIFFERENT clipping paths. v0.7.232 = intrinsic-size clipping (aspect-ratio video frame pushing stage past flex-1). v0.7.238 = sibling overflow clipping (shrink-0 footer rows exceeding body height). Removing v0.7.232 would re-expose the intrinsic-size path; removing v0.7.238 would re-expose the sibling overflow path. They are NOT alternatives.
- **shrink-0 on each footer child is now redundant** (the Card primitive's `<div className="shrink-0 flex flex-col">` wrapper already enforces it) but harmless. Don't strip it from existing footer children — visual/layout regression risk for zero gain.
- **`parseEnglishNumber` is shared between verse + chapter detectors** — keep the SMALL/TENS tables in one place. Adding "billion"/"thousand" is out of scope (Bibles top out at 176 verses); growing the parser invites top-K pollution where "verse one thousand" matches via cosine to Psalm 119:1.
- **Regex word capture `[a-z][a-z\s-]{1,40}` is INTENTIONALLY permissive**: parser owns the vocabulary, NOT the regex. Tightening the regex to `(one|two|...)` would require duplicating the entire SMALL/TENS table inside a regex alternation — high maintenance, easy to drift between regex and parser. Permissive regex + strict parser is the correct split.
- **Word-number capture MUST coexist with the v0.7.235 alternation `verse|verses|vest|vs`**: "vest five" is a real ASR output ("vest" = mishearing of "verse" + "five" = word-form). Don't narrow either dimension thinking they're mutually exclusive — many operator utterances combine both ASR errors in one go.
- **Range check stays AFTER number parsing, not inside parser**: `parseEnglishNumber` returns the literal number ("one hundred and ninety nine" → 199 even though no Bible has 199 chapters). Range gates live at the detector boundary so the same parser can serve future detectors with different ranges without forking. Current gates: chapter 1..150 (Psalms max), verse 1..200 (>176 to give slack for ASR mis-counts of Psalm 119:176).
- **Stage `overflow-hidden` MUST stay on `bg-black overflow-hidden`** in LiveDisplayCard's `bodyClassName` — twMerge merges this with the body default `overflow-y-auto overflow-x-hidden`, and the result determines whether vertical scroll fires or stage clips. We want stage clips (poster letterboxing), NOT scroll (operator finger drag over the stage shouldn't scroll the card).

### Tests

- `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean
- Regex + parser validated against 16 utterance forms (5 control cases pre-238 still pass: "verse 5" / "vest 5" / "vs 5" / "go to verse 5" / "show verse 5"; 11 new cases now pass: "verse five" / "vest five" / "verse twenty" / "verse twenty three" / "verse twenty-three" / "go to verse seven" / "show verse one hundred and twenty" / "chapter five" / "go to chapter twenty" / "take me to chapter one hundred and fifty" / "vest 176"; 4 out-of-range correctly rejected: "verse 201" / "chapter 0" / "chapter 151" / bare "verse")
- Card footer slot does not break any non-PreviewCard / non-LiveDisplayCard call site — every other Card invocation in logos-shell.tsx omits the `footer` prop and the conditional render (`footer != null && …`) skips the wrapper entirely.

### Verification cadence (per v0.7.237 PROCESS GUARD-RAIL)

After release-desktop run completes, spot-check via Contents API SHA verify against `release/v0.7.238`:

```bash
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $GH_PAT" \
  https://api.github.com/repos/nanvilow/scripturelive-ai/contents/artifacts/imported-app/src/lib/voice/commands.ts?ref=release/v0.7.238 \
  | python3 -c "import sys,json,base64; d=json.load(sys.stdin); c=base64.b64decode(d['content']).decode(); print('parseEnglishNumber:', 'parseEnglishNumber' in c)"
```

Should print `True`. Same check for `logos-shell.tsx` against marker `footer?: ReactNode`.
