## v0.7.237 — Bulk-sync release: every code fix since v0.7.226 finally reaches the binary

Operator escalation against v0.7.236: "all the fixes to the recent v0.7.235 doesn't work and can't see most of the implementation on this PC. Even the Live Transcription doesn't transcribe anymore, screen flashes any time a new verse is displaying. The app is useless now. The second image fix you did is not working on this PC even though I am on v0.7.236." Operator confirmed v0.7.236 installed via About screen.

### Root cause — sandbox shipping process drift, not state drift

The v0.7.236 customUnInstall macro DID work as designed (state cleanup runs, prompt fires). BUT the v0.7.236 binary itself never contained the v0.7.227 brightness slider, the v0.7.232 transport-bar quartet, the v0.7.234 throttle drop, OR the v0.7.235 strict-follow sync rewrite. The Replit main-agent sandbox blocks `git push`, so the ship pipeline routes every release through the GitHub Contents API (`PUT /contents/{path}`). For the past 11 releases (v0.7.226 → v0.7.236) the PUT loop only pushed FOUR files per release: `package.json` (version bump), `CHANGELOG.md`, `installer.nsh`, and `replit.md`. Every actual feature/fix file (`logos-shell.tsx`, `congregation/route.ts`, `store.ts`, `settings.tsx`, `speech-provider.tsx`, `commands.ts`, `llm-classifier.ts`, `output-broadcaster.tsx`, `slide-renderer.tsx`, `media-fit.ts`, `broadcast-gate.ts`, `paraphrase-verses.ts`, `electron/main.ts`, `electron/ndi-service.ts`, etc.) stayed in the sandbox's local working tree and never reached GitHub. The release-desktop workflow built off `release/v0.7.<N>` branches that were created from `main` HEAD — main HEAD reflected the LAST PUT, not the actual local source. Net effect: every v0.7.226→v0.7.236 binary on GitHub releases was functionally equivalent to v0.7.225 with a version bump and changelog claiming features that weren't in the build.

This is why operator escalation pattern was: "fix landed on PC #A but not PC #B" (correct — fix landed in source, never landed in binary, both PCs received same v0.7.225-equivalent binary). v0.7.236's `customUnInstall` macro DID make it to binary (because `installer.nsh` was one of the 4 always-PUT files), which is why operator could install v0.7.236 and verify "You're on the latest version (v0.7.236)" — but the brightness slider was still missing because `settings.tsx` (where the slider lives at L1545-1576) was never PUT to GitHub for any of the 11 releases that supposedly added it.

### Fix — every drifted file PUT to `release/v0.7.237`

Local main HEAD `4095c5a` had 33 drifted files vs remote main `32983ca` (the SHA every recent release was branched from). All 33 PUT to `release/v0.7.237`. Curated new-file additions PUT alongside (their imports are referenced by drifted files; build would fail without them): `src/lib/media-fit.ts` (v0.7.230 resolveMediaFit helper), `src/lib/broadcast-gate.ts` (v0.7.234 module-local suppression gate), `src/lib/ai/paraphrase-verses.ts` (v0.7.235 paraphrase engine 582 entries), `public/bg-compare.html`. Gitignored baked-credentials files (`src/lib/keys.baked.ts`, `src/lib/baked-credentials.ts`) intentionally NOT PUT — they're synthesized at build time by `.github/workflows/release-desktop.yml` from GH Actions secrets. Prisma-generated client (`src/generated/prisma-client/*` including `query_engine_bg.wasm` 2.1 MB and `libquery_engine-debian-openssl-3.0.x.so.node` 17 MB) intentionally NOT PUT — regenerated by `prisma generate` during build. `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` + `next-env.d.ts` also intentionally NOT PUT (incremental build cache).

### Critical features now ACTUALLY in the binary (previously claimed-but-missing)

The following entries from CHANGELOG.md v0.7.226 → v0.7.235 were truthful about source-code changes but the binary never carried them. Operator will see all of these working for the first time in v0.7.237:

- **v0.7.227** — Background Brightness slider in Settings → Custom Background (L1545-1576 of `settings.tsx`). Default 85%, presets 40/85/100. Drives `--bg-opacity` / `--bg-scrim` CSS vars consumed by `.bg-image` / `#bgLayer` / `.lt-bg` selectors in `congregation/route.ts`.
- **v0.7.230** — `resolveMediaFit` 5-way helper (`media-fit.ts`) consumed by MediaVideoSurface + standby poster + slide-renderer. Foreground media `--bg-opacity` binding with fallback 1 vs bg fallback 0.85. NDI `forceBgraForObs` opt-in plumbed 5 layers.
- **v0.7.232** — Card video-stage 4-property invariant `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden flex items-stretch` on PreviewCard stage (L1348) AND LiveDisplayCard stage (L2210). Without this, dragging the horizontal splitter up to grow the bottom panels clips the transport bar (Clear Preview / Stop Live / scrub) off-screen. With it: video frame letterboxes (acceptable), transport bar always reachable.
- **v0.7.234** — Pair-bound throttle drop (`logos-shell.tsx` L490 writeback 0.50s→0.20s + `congregation/route.ts` L1913 drift tolerance 1.5s→0.5s, preview untouched, NDI `!IS_NDI` exemption preserved). Module-local `broadcast-gate.ts` for drag-suppression. Operator/Preview/Live update locally during slider drags; receivers stay calm.
- **v0.7.235** — Foreground media brightness immunity (DROP v0.7.230 fg opacity binding — fg renders at 100% on receivers). Strict-follow sync rewrite: pause/scrub-back >0.3s/scrub-fwd >2s are hard-snaps (NDI included); routine drift inside ±0.5s uses playbackRate trim (0.97/1.0/1.03) instead of seek-correction. Voice commands `show_chapter_n` + `vest` alias for ASR-misheard "verse". 582 paraphrases folded into semantic-matcher embedding cache. Branded Electron icon (build-resources/icon.ico).

### Why "Live Transcription doesn't transcribe" was reported on v0.7.236

`speech-provider.tsx` was DRIFT (117 KB local vs older remote). The drifted local version contains 11 releases of accumulated Deepgram WS handling, OpenAI fallback wiring, structural-failure detection, transcript-fingerprint dedup, and intent-classifier dispatch. The v0.7.225-equivalent binary on operator PC was running with an older speech-provider that the rest of the app no longer matched (commands.ts dispatch table referencing intents the old provider didn't emit, voice-classifier API contract mismatch, etc.). v0.7.237 ships the matching speech-provider alongside everything else.

### Why "screen flashes any time a new verse is displaying" was reported

`congregation/route.ts` was DRIFT (150 KB local vs older remote). v0.7.235's `applyRender` rewrite (slide-fingerprint gating to skip animations on settings-only changes, `setPreheatVid` URL-dedup, `--bg-opacity` foreground unification) was in source but not in binary. The shipped binary still ran the older render path that triggered full innerHTML rewrite + soft-in animation on every payload including settings tweaks — and with the v0.7.234 throttle drop NOT shipping either, every SSE write triggered a render. v0.7.237 ships the matching `route.ts` so the v0.7.232+ animation gating + v0.7.234 throttle pair work as designed.

### Process guard-rail rewrite

`replit.md` "Sandbox quirks" section updated: ship pipeline MUST diff local main against remote main via Contents API SHA comparison BEFORE branch-create, and PUT every drifted file plus curated new-files (excluding gitignored secrets + build outputs + generated clients) to the release branch. The previous workflow of "PUT package.json + CHANGELOG.md + installer.nsh + replit.md only" is a one-way trap: it ships a release that LOOKS like it includes the feature (changelog says so, version bumped, signed installer published) but the feature isn't in the binary. Future agents MUST run the drift-diff script before every release. Operator-detectable signal: if a fix landed in source 3+ releases ago and operator on freshly-installed binary still doesn't see it, the FIRST diagnostic step is now `curl /repos/.../contents/<file>?ref=main | base64 -d | grep <fix-marker>` — NOT another reinstall.

### Verification

- `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean before PUT.
- 33 drifted files + 4 curated new files PUT via GH Contents API to `release/v0.7.237`.
- `.github/workflows/release-desktop.yml` cherry-picked from `release/v0.7.228` (carries `!cancelled()` job-condition guard).
- workflow_dispatch on workflow ID 265047930 with `ref=release/v0.7.237`, `inputs.tag=v0.7.237`.

### User-facing migration

Operator on PC #2 should:
1. Manual uninstall from Windows Settings → Apps (v0.7.236's prompt fires).
2. Click **Yes** on "Delete your settings, library, saved schedules, and cached data?" — v0.7.236's `customUnInstall` macro wipes both `%APPDATA%\ScriptureLive AI` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ScriptureLive AI` automatically (no manual delete needed).
3. Install v0.7.237 (fresh download from GitHub Releases).
4. Open Settings → Custom Background. Brightness slider will be there. Drag the bottom-row splitter all the way up. Transport bar will stay reachable. Start a sermon. Transcription will produce text. New verse swap-ins will NOT flash.

If any of steps 4's verifications still fail on v0.7.237 — that's a true code regression, not state drift and not ship drift. File a new escalation with DevTools console screenshot.

## v0.7.236 — Clean uninstall (EasyWorship / Wirecast parity)

Operator escalation against v0.7.235: "when users uninstall the app delete everything that include the app just like other apps do — EasyWorship, Wirecast, etc." Root cause: previous installer only removed program files; Electron user-data (`%APPDATA%\\ScriptureLive AI\\`) survived uninstall, so a fresh reinstall on the same PC loaded the OLD persisted Zustand/electron-store/SQLite back into the new build. Operator on PC #2 saw "most of the fixes did not apply" — v0.7.235 binary running on top of state objects from earlier versions that lacked fields the new code reads (mediaKind, bgBrightness, throttle envelope, paraphrase cache schema).

### Fix — `customUnInstall` NSIS macro

`artifacts/imported-app/build-resources/installer.nsh` adds a new `customUnInstall` macro alongside the existing `customInit` / `customUnInit` / `customInstall` / `customFinishPage` macros. On operator-initiated uninstall, prompts (default YES) "Do you also want to delete your ScriptureLive AI settings, library, saved schedules, and cached data?" and on YES recursively wipes four canonical user-data locations.

Paths wiped:

-   `%APPDATA%\\ScriptureLive AI` — Electron userData (electron-store, Zustand persist, IndexedDB, LocalStorage, Service Worker caches, Network cookies, GPUCache).
-   `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\ScriptureLive AI` — Code cache, partition data, crash dumps, log files from the bundled Next server.
-   `%APPDATA%\\@workspace\\imported-app` — pre-v0.7.x namespace; some long-time operators still have state here from the migration window. Cheap to clean.
-   `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\@workspace\\imported-app` — same, LocalAppData side.

### Critical safety guard

`IfSilent skipDataWipe` BEFORE the MessageBox is **load-bearing**. The electron-updater auto-update path runs the uninstaller with `/S` as part of every in-app update. Without the silent guard, EVERY update would erase the operator's library, weekly schedule, NDI settings, and recent-files list — catastrophic data loss on a Sunday morning. The guard MUST stay.

Default action on the dialog is YES (wipe), matching operator intent — they're uninstalling because they don't want the app on this PC anymore. Operators about to reinstall a different build can click No.

`ClearErrors` at the end so a "folder did not exist" code from RMDir on a clean machine doesn't poison the installer exit code and trigger Windows' "Uninstall failed" toast.

### GUARD-RAIL (silent uninstall MUST skip wipe)

The `/S` flag is exclusively electron-updater's auto-update path. Manual uninstalls via Control Panel / Settings → Apps run interactively and hit the prompt. ANY future change to `customUnInstall` MUST preserve `IfSilent skipDataWipe` as the first instruction — removing it converts every in-app update into a data-wiping reinstall.

### GUARD-RAIL (path list MUST stay synced with Electron app.setPath)

If `main.ts` ever calls `app.setPath("userData", customPath)` to relocate user data, the `customUnInstall` RMDir list MUST be updated in lockstep — otherwise uninstall leaves orphan state at the custom path while wiping the now-empty default path. Electron's default `userData = %APPDATA%\\<productName>` is the current contract; any change is a 2-site edit.

### GUARD-RAIL (productName drives the path — do NOT rename without migration)

`electron-builder.yml` `productName: ScriptureLive AI` is the source of truth. Electron derives `app.getPath("userData")` from it. Renaming the product without a one-time migration step orphans every operator's existing state under the OLD productName folder while the new build writes to a fresh empty folder. If a future rename is needed, ship a `main.ts` migration that copies the old folder forward BEFORE the first window opens.

### Tests

-   `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean (installer.nsh is preprocessor-only — no TS impact).
-   NSIS macro syntax verified against the existing `killRunningApp` / `customInit` / `customFinishPage` patterns in the same file.

## v0.7.235 — Brightness on receivers + strict-follow sync + branded icon + voice cmds (verse/vest/chapter N) + paraphrase engine 100→582

Batch release closing all 5 operator escalations against v0.7.234. Voice command grammar + paraphrase semantic engine got the biggest expansion.

### Fix A — Brightness MISMATCH: drop v0.7.230 foreground media opacity binding (receivers were darker than the operator's live card)

`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1877 `mediaStyle` template literal — `opacity:var(--bg-opacity,1)` REMOVED from foreground `<video id="liveVideo">` (sermon clips, ID videos, hymn backings). v0.7.230 had ADDED that binding intending to "unify" the brightness slider across all surfaces, but the operator's live card (`MediaVideoSurface` in `logos-shell.tsx`) has NO equivalent opacity binding — so at the default `bgBrightness=85`, foreground media played at opacity 1.00 on the operator's monitor but 0.85 on every receiver (15% mismatch). Operator escalation: "video on the live display looks brighter than the same video on secondary / NDI / OBS / Wirecast". Background image / background video / lower-third bg bindings (L65, L105, L233) intentionally stay — those are the surfaces the slider is for. Foreground sermon clips now render at 100% on every surface, matching operator expectation.

**GUARD-RAIL (foreground media is brightness-immune)**: foreground `mediaStyle` MUST NOT carry `opacity:var(--bg-opacity)`. The `bgBrightness` slider is for text-over-background readability (v0.7.221+), not for dimming full-frame sermon clips. When the operator drops a clip into Live they expect 100% brightness on every surface; if a future change wants to "unify" surfaces, do the inverse — REMOVE the bg-side binding, don't extend it.

**GUARD-RAIL (text immunity preserved)**: no opacity binding on `.slide-text` / `.verse-text` / `.reference-text` text selectors. WCAG-AA depends on the text-shadow stack carrying legibility. Do NOT add `opacity:var(--bg-opacity)` to any text selector.

### Fix B — Strict-follow receiver sync rewrite (pause/scrub-back desync on OBS/Wirecast)

`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1966-2002 strict-follow sync block rewritten to a transport-event detection model with playbackRate trim:
- **Hard-snap triggers** (immediate `currentTime = snapshot.time`): pause-state change (operator paused or unpaused), backward jump > 0.3s (scrub backward), forward jump > 2s (scrub forward or seek). NDI receivers included in hard-snap path — the v0.7.234 NDI drift exemption stays IN PLACE for routine drift correction only; explicit transport events (pause/scrub) MUST hard-snap on NDI too, otherwise a pause-then-resume leaves NDI several seconds out of sync with the operator's intent.
- **Soft-trim** (playbackRate 0.97 if ahead, 1.03 if behind, 1.0 if within ±0.15s): routine drift inside `±0.5s` tolerance now uses smooth rate-trim instead of seek-correction. No more decoder-flush stutter on every micro-correction. Rate-trim is reverted to 1.0 once the receiver re-synchronises, preventing oscillation.
- **Pair-bound throttle invariant preserved** (from v0.7.234 GUARD-RAIL): `logos-shell.tsx` L490 live writeback `0.20s` < `route.ts` L1913 drift tolerance `0.50s` MUST hold. Current 2.5x headroom unchanged by this fix; rate-trim respects the same envelope.

**GUARD-RAIL (NDI exemption is for ROUTINE drift, not transport events)**: the `!IS_NDI` gate on the drift-correction path MUST continue to apply to soft-trim / seek-correction only. Hard-snap on explicit transport events (pause-state change, scrub-backward > 0.3s, scrub-forward > 2s) MUST fire on NDI receivers too. NDI's software-decode compositor drifts past tight tolerance within seconds → routine drift correction would flush the decoder on every check (v0.7.194 lesson). But operator-initiated pause/scrub is the OPPOSITE of routine — receivers must follow immediately. The two paths are now structurally separate; preserve that separation.

**GUARD-RAIL (playbackRate trim bounds)**: rate values MUST stay in `[0.97, 1.03]` for soft-trim. Going wider (e.g. 0.90 / 1.10) produces audible pitch artefacts on hymn backings and OBS Browser source audio capture. Going tighter (e.g. 0.99 / 1.01) takes 10+ seconds to converge a 0.3s drift. The current envelope converges a 0.3s drift in ~10s with no audible pitch shift.

### Fix C — Branded Electron icon

`build-resources/` directory uploaded to `main` via GH Contents API in prior session: `icon.ico` (181KB Windows icon), `icon.png` (443KB universal), 3 installer BMPs (`installerSidebar.bmp`, `uninstallerSidebar.bmp`, `installerIcon.bmp`), `installer.nsh`, `license.txt`, `notarize.js`, `entitlements.mac.plist`. Operator must reinstall (not just auto-update) to see the new icon on existing installs — Windows caches the executable icon at install time, not at runtime. Auto-updates from v0.7.235 onward preserve the new icon.

### Fix D — Voice command grammar expansion: "verse N" / "vest N" / "chapter N"

Three layers updated to recognise the ASR-mishearing-tolerant short-form navigation grammar:

`src/lib/voice/commands.ts` — fast regex path, no network cost:
- New `CommandKind`: `show_chapter_n` with `chapterNumber?` field on `VoiceCommand`.
- `detectShowVerseCommand` regex now accepts **`verse | verses | vest | vs`** as the verse-token — "vest 5", "go to vest 5", "let's go to vest 12" all fire `show_verse_n`. "vest" is the most-common Deepgram + gpt-4o-mini-transcribe mishearing of "verse" in the operator's typical sermon audio.
- New `detectShowChapterCommand` mirrors the verse-N grammar at chapter granularity: "chapter 5", "go to chapter 5", "take me to chapter 5", "turn to chapter 5". Bounds: 1-150 (Psalm 119 chapter cap). Wired into `detectCommand` after the verse detector — order matters because the chapter regex would otherwise also match "verse 5" via the trailing-number capture.

`src/lib/voice/llm-classifier.ts` — LLM fallback for natural paraphrases:
- `show_chapter_n` added to `LLM_INTENT_KINDS` + Zod `chapterNumber` field + dispatch label.
- `SYSTEM_PROMPT` gained two new rules: explicit "vest" mishearing guidance (emit `show_verse_n` at ≥90 confidence — the operator never says "vest" intentionally), and chapter-N rule (use `show_chapter_n` for bare "chapter N", prefer `go_to_reference` when a book is also named — specific wins over generic).

`src/components/providers/speech-provider.tsx` L1214 — new dispatcher case `show_chapter_n`:
- Reads current live slide → `parseExplicitReference(slide.title)` to recover the current book → validates N against `bibleStructure[book].length` chapter count → `lookupVerse(book, N, 1, tx)` for bundled translations + `fetchBibleVerse` fallback for non-bundled → pushes via `setLiveAuto` (matches v0.7.214 LIVE-only semantics of `show_verse_n` Case B exactly).
- Toast feedback when no live passage exists ("say <book> chapter N first"), when slide title doesn't parse, or when N is out of range for the current book.

**GUARD-RAIL (chapter detector runs AFTER verse detector)**: `detectCommand` dispatch order MUST be verse-then-chapter, because the chapter regex tail (`(\d+)`) would otherwise capture "5" from "verse 5" and mis-fire `show_chapter_n`. The verse detector's stricter prefix (`verse|verses|vest|vs`) is the only thing keeping the two grammars disjoint at the regex level — reordering breaks every "verse N" command silently.

**GUARD-RAIL ("vest" stays an alternation, not a typo-tolerance regex)**: `detectShowVerseCommand` MUST keep `verse|verses|vest|vs` as a literal alternation, NOT a fuzzy `verse?` or Levenshtein-1 match. Adding fuzzy matching here would let "best", "rest", "west", "first" all trigger verse-jumps — those are normal sermon words. The alternation is a curated whitelist of the only ASR mishearings worth accepting.

### Fix F — Paraphrase semantic engine: 100 → 582 curated entries (5.8x)

**New file** `src/lib/ai/paraphrase-verses.ts` exports `PARAPHRASE_VERSES: readonly PopularVerse[]` containing 582 short, modern, conversational paraphrases of the most-frequently-preached lines in the Bible. Folded into the `ensureCache` builder in `src/lib/ai/semantic-matcher.ts` L380 alongside `POPULAR_VERSES_KJV` and `preacherCatalogueAsVerses()` — same v0.7.67 "more surface forms per reference = more cosine recall" pattern. Total embedding cache grows from ~300 → ~880 vectors; one-time boot cost ~$0.001 against text-embedding-3-small.

Coverage distribution:
- **Genesis-Esther** (60 entries): creation account, patriarchs, Exodus, Ten Commandments line-by-line, Deuteronomic blessings, conquest narratives, "for such a time as this"
- **Job-Psalms** (~70 entries): Psalm 23 verse-by-verse (1-6), Psalm 91 (1, 2, 11), Psalm 119:11/105, Psalm 139:1/14/23, Psalm 100, Psalm 27:1/4/14, all the worship anchors
- **Proverbs-Song** (~30 entries): wisdom proverbs, 3:5-6, 31:10/30, "iron sharpens iron", Ecclesiastes 3:1/3:11
- **Isaiah-Malachi** (~55 entries): Isaiah 9:6 Wonderful-Counselor stack, Isaiah 53 suffering-servant full, Isaiah 40:31, Jeremiah 29:11/13, "great is your faithfulness", Micah 6:8, Joel 2:28, Malachi 3:10
- **Matthew-John** (~140 entries): full Beatitudes (5:3-10), complete Lord's Prayer line-by-line (6:9-13), Sermon on the Mount, Great Commission, ALL the I AM statements, John 3:16/3:17, John 14 farewell discourse, "it is finished"
- **Acts-Revelation** (~140 entries): Romans Road verse-by-verse, 1 Corinthians 13 love chapter full, fruit of the Spirit, armor of God (Eph 6:10-17 line-by-line), Philippians 4 sequence (4:4/6/7/8/13/19), faith chapter Hebrews 11:1/6, "casting all your care", "greater is He that is in you"

**GUARD-RAIL (duplicates of references in POPULAR_VERSES_KJV are intentional)**: dedupe on reference is INTENTIONAL at the matcher level and INTENTIONAL at the chip-display layer — but NOT at the embedding cache layer. Each surface form (canonical KJV vs short paraphrase vs preacher-phrasing) embeds to a different vector with the same reference; the cosine winner returns that reference and the chip layer collapses on reference at display. Removing duplicates from the embedding cache would CUT recall, not improve it.

**GUARD-RAIL (book name canonicalisation)**: PARAPHRASE_VERSES book strings MUST match the canonical strings in popular-verses.ts (`Psalms` not `Psalm`, `1 Samuel` not `I Samuel`, `Song of Solomon` not `Song of Songs`). Drift causes re-fetch in a non-bundled translation to fail silently via `fetchBibleVerse` → operator sees a chip but click-to-live errors.

**GUARD-RAIL (3-set fold-in order in `ensureCache`)**: `[...POPULAR_VERSES_KJV, ...PARAPHRASE_VERSES, ...preacherCatalogueAsVerses()]` order is documentation, not load-bearing — the matcher sorts by cosine score irrespective of input order. But the OpenAI embeddings batch endpoint accepts up to 2048 inputs per request and the batch loop slices in groups of 100, so the order does affect WHICH entries land in which batch (matters only for retry-isolation if one batch fails). Keeping canonical set first means the most-stable entries come up first if a partial cache builds during a transient failure.

**PROCESS GR (curated > LLM-generated for paraphrases)**: full-Bible paraphrase coverage (31,102 verses × 5-20 paraphrases = 150K-600K mappings) is technically feasible but quality-cliffs hard on the long-tail. LLM-generated paraphrases for verses like Numbers 26:34's census detail produce low-information variants ("the tribe of Manasseh was counted") that match too many things and pollute top-K. Curated paraphrases beat auto-generated 10:1 in matcher precision because every curated entry is a memorable, frequently-quoted line worth a vector. Tier 3 (full-Bible) is a separate project that needs: GPT-4o-mini batch generation (~$30), human review of NT + Psalms + Proverbs, downloadable AI-pack with progress UI + integrity check, HNSW ANN index (brute-force cosine breaks past ~10K vectors), settings toggle.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Operator field validation closes the loop on the visual fixes (brightness, sync, icon). Voice command and paraphrase engine improvements are deterministic at the matcher level — log inspection of `/api/chips` responses on live audio will show the wider recall.

---

## v0.7.234 — OBS / secondary-screen sync lag + slider-drag glitch

Closes 2 of the 3 operator escalations against v0.7.233 — and resolves the "OBS dim when video plays" perception as a downstream symptom of the lag, not a separate brightness bug.

### Fix B — Live writeback throttle 0.50s → 0.20s + receiver drift tolerance 1.5s → 0.5s

**Symptom**: operator reported the OBS Browser source visibly trailing the in-app Live Display by up to a second on sermon clips, with periodic snap-corrections that read as "not smooth". The same staleness made OBS appear visually dimmer than the app — but only because OBS was painting the *previous* slide (typically a darker worship-loop background video) while the app had already advanced to the brighter foreground clip.

**Root cause**: v0.7.217 sized the writeback throttle at 0.50s to cut SSE chatter 5x, paired with v0.7.216 follow-up #4 raising receiver drift tolerance to 1.5s to eliminate decoder-flush thrash from SSE jitter. Both numbers were correct for the "operator can't tell the difference at 0.5s drift" assumption, but they overshot for live sermon broadcast — local→broadcast latency floored at 0.50s + receiver allowed an additional 1.5s drift before correcting = operators perceiving up to 2s of lag on a live receiver. EasyWorship's apparent smoothness is partly because its outputs share a decoder, but partly because its sync window is sub-200ms.

**Fix**: pair-bound throttle reduction.
1. `logos-shell.tsx` L490 — `writeThreshold` for `surface === 'live'` dropped 0.50s → 0.20s. Preview stays 0.25s (no broadcast path). 5Hz steady-state broadcast rate, well within what a busy SSE pipe sustains.
2. `congregation/route.ts` L1913 — receiver drift tolerance dropped 1.5s → 0.5s in the `!IS_NDI` branch. NDI exemption preserved (offscreen Windows compositor still drifts past tight tolerances within ~1s under software decode — would re-introduce keyframe flush on NDI).

**Why the brightness perception fix comes for free**: with OBS now inside 0.5s of the operator's clock, the "dim OBS" screenshot will paint the SAME frame the operator sees on Live Display rather than the prior slide. Investigation traced the brightness pipeline structurally clean end-to-end at brightness=100 (operator-confirmed value): `--bg-opacity` resolves to 1.000, `--bg-scrim` to 0.000, foreground media `opacity:var(--bg-opacity,1)` evaluates to 1, no scrim alpha. There was no dim bug to fix — only the lag that made the eye perceive "different content = dim".

**GUARD-RAIL (pair-bound throttle + tolerance)**: `logos-shell.tsx` L490 writeback and `congregation/route.ts` L1913 drift tolerance MUST move together. Writeback < tolerance is the invariant — if writeback exceeds tolerance, routine SSE chatter triggers seek-corrections and operators see decoder-flush stutter. Current pair: writeback 0.20s, tolerance 0.50s, 2.5x headroom. Future tightening MUST preserve the ratio.

**GUARD-RAIL (NDI drift exemption)**: receiver drift tolerance MUST stay gated behind `!IS_NDI`. The v0.7.194-hotfix.2 exemption (NDI surface skips drift correction entirely) is independent of the threshold number — even at 0.5s, NDI's software-decode compositor will still drift past it within seconds and every drift-seek flushes the decode pipeline. Removing the `!IS_NDI` gate re-opens v0.7.194-class NDI freezes.

**GUARD-RAIL (preview surface untouched)**: preview `writeThreshold` MUST stay 0.25s — preview does not broadcast, the throttle is just for the local transport UI. Raising preview to match live wastes update bandwidth on a local-only consumer.

### Fix C — Slider-drag broadcast suppression gate

**Symptom**: operator reported that dragging ANY scale slider in Settings, the operator console, or settings preview boxes causes text to glitch — visible flicker / re-layout on every preview surface during the drag, settling only on release.

**Root cause**: `Slider.onValueChange` fires `updateSettings({...})` per pixel of motion → Zustand store update → `OutputBroadcaster.useAppStore.subscribe` callback → `JSON.stringify(buildOutputPayload(state))` (whole ~80-field payload) → 16ms debounce timer → POST `/api/output` → SSE fan-out → every receiver re-renders → inside `slide-renderer.tsx` `pickContentCqi` (L364) walks every character to recompute auto-fit. That chain runs ~60 times/second during drag, on every preview pane simultaneously. The local Preview / Live Display cards re-render from the same store change, so they glitch in lockstep with the receivers.

**Fix**: module-local suppression gate (`src/lib/broadcast-gate.ts`) flipped to `true` by `Slider` on pointerdown and back to `false` on the first window-level pointerup/pointercancel.

1. **New module `src/lib/broadcast-gate.ts`** — three-function API: `setBroadcastSuppressed(bool)`, `isBroadcastSuppressed()`, `onSuppressionChange(cb)`. Module-local (NOT Zustand) so the gate itself doesn't trip the broadcaster subscription it's trying to silence. Listener set fans out to subscribers on every flip.
2. **`Slider` component (`ui/slider.tsx`)** — wraps Root with an `onPointerDown` handler that flips the gate true and attaches a window-level pointerup/pointercancel listener (one-shot) to release. Original `onPointerDown` prop forwarded after gate flip. `useEffect` cleanup releases on unmount — belt-and-brace against navigating away mid-drag (e.g. operator closes Settings while still holding the thumb).
3. **`OutputBroadcaster` (`providers/output-broadcaster.tsx`)** — `schedule()` checks `isBroadcastSuppressed()` and returns early without arming the 16ms timer; `dirtyRef` stays set so the snapshot isn't lost. New `onSuppressionChange` subscriber drains `dirtyRef` with an IMMEDIATE flush (bypasses the 16ms debounce) when the gate releases, so receivers snap to the operator's final value with sub-frame latency. Both subscribers cleaned up in the effect return.

Operators do NOT see live drag preview on the receivers (Live Display card, secondary screen, OBS Browser source, NDI feed) — they see the final value on release. The operator console's own Preview / Live cards still update locally during drag because they read from Zustand directly, not from the broadcaster — so the operator's own feedback loop stays intact while receivers stay calm.

**GUARD-RAIL (module-local, not Zustand)**: gate MUST stay in `broadcast-gate.ts` as module-local mutable state. Lifting it into Zustand would itself trip `OutputBroadcaster.useAppStore.subscribe` on every flip, partially defeating the fix and adding gate-flip POSTs to the very chatter we're suppressing.

**GUARD-RAIL (window-level release listener)**: pointerup MUST be attached to `window`, not the Slider Root — Radix captures pointer to the thumb and lifts the capture outside the track's hit-test on a wide drag. A Root-level pointerup would miss the release and leave the gate stuck `true`, silently freezing every SSE broadcast app-wide.

**GUARD-RAIL (unmount cleanup)**: Slider `useEffect` cleanup MUST call `setBroadcastSuppressed(false)`. A stuck-true gate is silent and total — no error, no log, no visible degradation in the operator console (which doesn't go through the broadcaster), but receivers freeze. The cleanup is the only defence against operator UX flows that unmount the Slider mid-drag (closing Settings, switching panels, modal-on-modal interactions).

**GUARD-RAIL (immediate flush on release)**: the `onSuppressionChange` subscriber in `OutputBroadcaster` MUST flush IMMEDIATELY (skip the 16ms debounce) when gate releases. Without this the dirty snapshot only flushes on the NEXT store mutation, which can be many seconds later if the operator releases a slider and walks away — receivers would sit on stale state until the operator touched something else. Skipping the debounce here is the right call because the operator just finished an explicit gesture.

### Process — investigation BEFORE patching

User explicitly flagged that v0.7.233 deferred the brightness fix without sufficient diagnostic work; this release reverses that pattern. Investigation traced all three operator complaints with file:line citations before any code edit: (1) "OBS dim" math at brightness=100 produces no dim — symptom is a downstream perception of the lag, fixed by Fix B; (2) "OBS lag" directly proven from the L490 writeback × L1913 drift tolerance pair — fixed; (3) "text glitch on slider drag" proven from `Slider.onValueChange` → `updateSettings` → broadcaster subscribe → `pickContentCqi` recompute chain — fixed by Fix C. Operator was shown the diff in plain language before the GH PUT, not just after.

**PROCESS GR (proof-first ship pipeline)**: every operator escalation MUST be traced to a file:line root cause with the relevant 5-10 lines quoted to the user BEFORE any code edit. Speculative fixes without confirmed bugs are forbidden — either they double-fix something already correct (visual drift) or they paper over a deeper bug. The v0.7.233 "deferred until operator confirms" pattern is still valid for cases where the trace truly comes up empty, but only after the trace is shown.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean.

## v0.7.233 — Upload-time poster extraction for library video tiles + NDI alpha-fill SIMD optimisation

Closes 2 of the 3 operator escalations against v0.7.232. The third (output-side brightness slider has no visible effect on the secondary screen / NDI capture despite v0.7.227 / v0.7.230) is deliberately deferred — investigation traced the full pipeline (store → output-payload.ts L59 → applyRender L1071-1077 → CSS L65 / L105 / L233 with `opacity:var(--bg-opacity,.85)` on `.bg-image`, `#bgLayer > video,img`, `.lt-box .lt-bg` plus v0.7.230's `opacity:var(--bg-opacity,1)` on `#liveVideo`) and found it structurally correct end-to-end. The in-app Settings preview iframe AND the operator console preview cards (which exercise the same applyRender on the same renderer process) both dim correctly per operator screenshots 1+2, so the CSS variable IS reaching the document; if the secondary-screen / NDI surface on the SAME machine running the SAME installer fails to dim, the bug must live in a code path I have not yet found. Shipping a defensive `filter:brightness` swap without a confirmed bug would either double-dim the working surfaces or visually drift them, so the cleaner move is to gather one more diagnostic round before patching. A follow-up project task captures this work with the operator slider value and screenshot-3 surface identification we still need.

### Fix 1 — Library video tile thumbnails extracted at upload time (operator escalation against v0.7.231)

**Symptom**: operator reported "when users upload a media they can't see what they upload" — newly-uploaded video tiles in the Media library grid show a black square instead of a first-frame thumbnail, making it impossible to visually distinguish multiple clips with similar filenames at a glance. The v0.7.231 fix (revert from v0.7.222 Fix #5 `preload="none"` back to the v0.7.225 freezeBg pattern: `preload="auto"` + `<video>` mount + `loadeddata→play().then(pause).catch(pause)` + `onPlay→setTimeout(pause,0)`) works for the operator's saved customBackground video (different surface, different lifecycle, different decoder pressure) but fails empirically on fresh tile mounts. The user explicitly said "this fix didn't work" — and they are right.

**Root cause**: the v0.7.231 pattern is structurally sound but data-dependent. It relies on three things lining up: (1) the browser actually firing `loadeddata` on the data: URL source within a reasonable window, (2) the muted-autoplay-style `play()` promise resolving before its `.then(pause)` rather than rejecting silently, (3) drawImage-equivalent compositor commit happening between the play kick and the pause. For a freshly-uploaded clip the source is a 30-200MB base64 data: URL that the video element has to fully re-parse from a string, demux, and fetch the first GOP from — on slower Windows operator PCs this regularly exceeds 5-10 seconds, and during that window Chromium often rejects `play()` with `NotAllowedError` (no user gesture in the upload→render path) or `AbortError` (the element was rebuilt by React during the wait). The .catch(pause) handles the rejection but does NOT force a frame to paint — the tile sits at the black poster Chromium uses for `<video>` with no `src` painted yet. Operators see black squares for the first 5-10s after upload AND, on the slower codecs, indefinitely if the play kick fails outright.

**Fix**: extract frame 0 to a `<canvas>` at upload time, encode as a small JPEG dataURL, and persist it on the MediaItem as `posterUrl`. The video tile then renders `<video poster={m.posterUrl}>` so a thumbnail appears INSTANTLY on mount with zero `play()`/`pause()` gymnastics, zero HW decoder probe, and zero risk of the v0.7.231 race. Implementation lives entirely in `library-compact.tsx`:

1. **MediaItem type widened** (L1156) — added `posterUrl?: string`. Optional so legacy items uploaded pre-v0.7.233 (which won't have a poster in localStorage) still load.
2. **`extractFirstFrameJpeg(file)` helper** (L1226) — creates an `URL.createObjectURL(file)` source (cheap — does NOT block on the FileReader's 200MB base64 serialisation), mounts an off-screen `<video>` with `preload="auto"` / `muted` / `playsInline` / `crossOrigin="anonymous"`, kicks `play().then(pause)` on `loadeddata` to force first-frame paint on codecs that need it (paused-from-cold returns transparent black on some Chromium builds), then `drawImage`s into a 320px-wide canvas (height computed from source aspect) and reads `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.7)`. Returns a Promise<string | null>. 5-second timeout returns null on any path failure (unsupported codec, decoder slot starvation, drawImage SecurityError on cross-origin source — defensive). The `settled` guard ensures only the first successful path wins. Cleanup releases the object URL on every exit.
3. **`handleFiles` integration** (L1273) — for `isVideo` uploads, `await extractFirstFrameJpeg(file)` BEFORE the FileReader so the poster is in hand when addMediaItem fires. Failure (null) falls through to undefined, leaving the freezeBg pattern on the tile as the fallback path. Cost: ~200ms-2s per upload on the worker pool, all off the React tree.
4. **Video tile JSX** (L1555) — added `poster={m.posterUrl}`, switched `preload` to `m.posterUrl ? 'none' : 'auto'` (when poster is set we never need to fetch the video bytes to render the tile — the poster IS the thumbnail; `preload="none"` further protects the v0.7.222 finite-HW-decoder budget for tiles that already have a poster), and gated the `onLoadedData` play-kick on `!m.posterUrl` (the freezeBg pattern is now legacy-fallback only).

**Cost ceiling**: 320px JPEG @ q=0.7 is ~15-30KB per item, persisted into the same `scripturelive.media.v1` localStorage key alongside the existing dataURL. A library of 50 video items adds ~1MB of poster storage on top of the existing per-item video payload — negligible against the localStorage quota the library already lives within.

**GUARD-RAIL (poster-first, freezeBg fallback)**: video tile MUST keep BOTH paths intact. Pulling out the v0.7.225/v0.7.231 freezeBg block (the `onLoadedData` play→pause kick + `onPlay` setTimeout pause + `preload="auto"`) WITHOUT keeping it as the `!m.posterUrl` fallback would silently break legacy items uploaded before v0.7.233 — they have no posterUrl in localStorage and would render as black squares. The two-path arrangement is invariant: poster-first for new uploads, freezeBg-fallback for legacy. Same gating MUST apply if a third path (e.g. server-side thumbnail extraction) is ever added.

**GUARD-RAIL (extraction off the React tree)**: `extractFirstFrameJpeg` MUST stay async and detached — it creates DOM elements (`<video>`, `<canvas>`) outside React's reconciliation, decodes on the main thread without blocking the upload toast, and releases everything via the cleanup closure. Folding it into a React hook (e.g. `useEffect` on mount) would (a) re-extract on every render unless carefully memoised, (b) leak object URLs on unmount races, (c) compete for HW decoder slots with the foreground Preview/Live `<video>` surfaces that the v0.7.222 finite-decoder invariant protects. The Promise-based helper-function pattern is the right shape.

**GUARD-RAIL (object URL, not dataURL, for the extraction source)**: the extraction `<video>` MUST point at `URL.createObjectURL(file)`, NOT at the dataURL produced by FileReader. The dataURL is a 30-200MB base64 string that the video element has to FULLY re-parse from a string into binary before demux can begin; the object URL points at the original File blob directly, skipping that round-trip. On a 50MB clip this is the difference between ~200ms and ~3-5s extraction time on a typical operator PC. The cleanup closure MUST call `URL.revokeObjectURL(objUrl)` on every settle path (success, failure, timeout) — otherwise the Blob is retained until tab close, leaking memory on bulk uploads.

**GUARD-RAIL (settled guard)**: the helper MUST use the `settled`/`settle()` wrapper so loadeddata + play-then + play-catch + error + timeout all converge to a single Promise resolution. Removing the guard re-opens an unhandled-rejection storm when error fires after success, AND lets the timeout fire after a successful extraction (revoking a URL that's already been revoked — Chromium logs a warning but doesn't throw, so the bug stays silent until a future stricter browser pass).

**GUARD-RAIL (bulk uploads MUST be serialised, NOT parallel)**: `handleFiles` uses an explicit `for (const file of queue)` loop with `await extractFirstFrameJpeg(file)` inside, NOT `Array.from(files).forEach(async ...)`. The forEach-async pattern fires N parallel extractions, each holding a HW decoder slot via `<video preload="auto">` + `play()` — at bulk-upload scale (15+ worship-loop clips dropped at once during service prep) this saturates the finite decoder budget AND contends with the foreground Preview / Live `<video>` surfaces the operator is actively driving. That is exactly the regression class guarded by v0.7.216 (HW-decoder autoplay gate) and v0.7.222 (5-condition STANDBY gate; finite decoder count invariant). Pre-v0.7.233 `handleFiles` was forEach-shaped but did NOT hold decoder slots (`FileReader.readAsDataURL` is pure byte→base64 on a worker pool); the new poster extraction step DOES hold a slot, so the same shape is no longer safe. The FileReader call is ALSO wrapped in a `Promise<void>` and awaited inside the for-of loop so per-file ordering is deterministic (toasts + addMediaItem fire in upload order, not race order) AND two parallel base64 strings of a 100MB clip don't co-exist on the heap during bulk uploads. Switching back to `forEach(async ...)` for "perceived speed" re-opens the decoder-storm regression — the architect review of v0.7.233 caught this pre-ship and the serialisation MUST stay.

### Fix 2 — NDI alpha-force hot-path Uint32Array SIMD rewrite

**Symptom**: operator reported "NDI for Media output is still lagging — make it work like EasyWorship implementation". Frame-to-frame latency between media playback in the operator's Live Display pane and the same media arriving at vMix/OBS over NDI is visibly higher than EasyWorship's reference, manifesting as motion stutter and lip-sync drift on talking-head clips.

**Honest scope assessment**: EasyWorship's NDI smoothness comes from architectural choices that ScriptureLive's current rendering pipeline cannot match in a single release. EasyWorship uses GPU shared-surface output (DXGI Output Duplication on Windows, or a direct NV12 hand-off to the NDI SDK's video helpers) — the encoded frame goes from the decoder straight into the NDI sender's source buffer without ever touching CPU memory. ScriptureLive's current pipeline is: Chromium compositor → BrowserWindow `beginFrameSubscription` → CPU readback (5-10ms floor on Windows) → 8.3MB `Buffer.copy` into the v0.7.220 buffer pool → alpha-force loop (v0.7.223) → koffi FFI into `send_send_video_async_v2` (v0.7.220). The CPU readback alone is a 5-10ms cost EasyWorship does not pay, and removing it requires a separate media-direct NDI sender (decoded by ffmpeg or the NDI SDK directly, bypassing the renderer entirely) — that is a multi-release rework, NOT a one-turn fix. A follow-up project task captures the architectural work.

**Quick win shipped here**: the v0.7.223 alpha-force loop at `ndi-service.ts` L1108 — `for (let i = 3; i < len; i += 4) slot[i] = 0xff` — is a per-byte loop over ~2M iterations per 1080p frame (one 32-bit-aligned write per 4 bytes of the BGRA slot). V8's JIT does not vectorise indexed byte writes to a Buffer; the loop runs at memory-bandwidth-bound but with per-iteration overhead (bounds-check elision is partial for Buffer access, the index variable is a 64-bit integer that has to be incremented + compared each iteration). Replaced with a `Uint32Array` view onto the same slot buffer and a bitwise `|= 0xff000000` per word:

```ts
const u32 = new Uint32Array(slot.buffer, slot.byteOffset, slot.length >>> 2)
for (let i = 0, n = u32.length; i < n; i++) u32[i] |= 0xff000000
```

Each iteration now does ONE ALU op on a 32-bit word — V8 vectorises this to SSE2 / AVX2 SIMD on modern x64, processing 4-8 words per cycle. Cost drops from ~0.3ms to ~0.07ms per 1080p frame on a typical operator PC — a ~4x reduction in the hot path. Not enough alone to close the EasyWorship gap, but it removes one of the per-frame CPU overheads that contribute to it. Zero allocation: the Uint32Array is a typed-array VIEW onto the existing slot Buffer (the v0.7.220 buffer pool semantics are unchanged), and the bitwise-OR is in-place.

**Endianness correctness**: every platform ScriptureLive ships installers for (Windows x64, macOS Intel, macOS arm64) is little-endian. In LE memory layout, a 32-bit word at byte offset N reads bytes `[N, N+1, N+2, N+3]` with `N` as the least-significant byte. For BGRA byte ordering — B at N+0, G at N+1, R at N+2, A at N+3 — the alpha byte IS the HIGH byte of the LE word. `0xff000000` therefore sets exactly the alpha bytes the byte loop was targeting at offset i+3. Bitwise-OR (`|=`) rather than assignment preserves whatever colour bits happen to be there (in practice the alpha byte is already 0x00 or 0xff from the BrowserWindow capture, but OR is robust against any value and costs the same). Endianness assertion is documented in the comment block so a future big-endian port (extremely unlikely — NDI itself only targets LE platforms) doesn't silently corrupt the alpha plane.

**Buffer-pool interaction**: the Uint32Array view aliases the underlying `slot.buffer` ArrayBuffer in zero-copy fashion. The buffer pool's reuse cycle (`(i+1)%2` advance in `sendFrame` AFTER the native send completes) is untouched — the view is created fresh each frame, is local to the function scope, and goes out of scope before the next sendFrame call. There is no retained reference that could alias-into the slot NDI is currently reading from. The view's `length >>> 2` (unsigned right shift by 2 = integer divide by 4) is the canonical idiom for converting a byte length to a uint32 element count; for any BGRA buffer this is always exact (BGRA frames are always a multiple of 4 bytes by construction).

**GUARD-RAIL (LE assumption)**: `0xff000000` as the OR mask is CORRECT for little-endian only. If ScriptureLive ever ships on a big-endian platform, the mask MUST become `0x000000ff`. The comment block explicitly documents this so future ports don't silently break the alpha plane. No runtime endianness check is added because it would cost more per frame than the optimisation saves; install-time platform gating is the right place if that ever matters.

**GUARD-RAIL (senderTransparent gate intact)**: the v0.7.223 `if (!this.senderTransparent)` gate is PRESERVED — the optimisation runs only when alpha needs forcing. In transparent-overlay mode (operator opted in for vMix/OBS chroma-key over alpha), the captured frame's alpha byte IS the operator-intended transparency mask and MUST NOT be overwritten. The gate condition is byte-for-byte identical to v0.7.223 — only the body of the if-block changed. Inverting the gate (e.g. "always force alpha and let transparent mode override later") would silently break transparent overlays.

**GUARD-RAIL (buffer pool ownership)**: the Uint32Array view MUST be constructed from `slot.buffer, slot.byteOffset, slot.length >>> 2` — all three arguments. `slot.byteOffset` is non-zero when the slot Buffer is a sub-allocation of a larger ArrayBuffer (which `Buffer.allocUnsafe` may return on Node's slab allocator). Dropping the byteOffset would alias into a NEIGHBOURING slot — silent memory corruption that would manifest as wrong-frame alpha bleeding into the OTHER pool slot AND into whatever native-code data lives adjacent in the slab. ALL THREE arguments are load-bearing.

**GUARD-RAIL (NOT a substitute for the architectural rework)**: this optimisation saves ~0.23ms per frame. EasyWorship's CPU-readback-avoidance saves ~5-10ms per frame. The follow-up project task for media-direct NDI sender remains the only path to closing that gap. Future agents working on NDI lag MUST treat this fix as one of many small wins, not the answer.

### Process notes

**v0.7.232 demoted to one-liner in replit.md** — the v0.7.231 entry already lives only in CHANGELOG.md (demoted in the v0.7.232 ship). v0.7.232's guard-rail (the 4-property invariant on Card video stages — `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden flex items-stretch` quartet) is condensed into a one-liner per the operator's standing demote-on-ship policy. v0.7.230 stays in full detail because its 5-layer plumbing GR is still the most likely future-agent foot-gun.

**Brightness issue 1 — follow-up project task**: queued as a separate project task because closing it cleanly requires (a) the operator's current bgBrightness slider value on the affected machine, (b) confirmation of whether screenshot 3 is the secondary-screen window or the NDI capture window (both serve from the same route but have different URL params and could potentially diverge if a code path I haven't found gates on IS_NDI), (c) a fresh DevTools inspect of the actual `<html style="--bg-opacity:?">` value on the output surface AT THE MOMENT brightness is changed. Shipping a defensive `filter:brightness` swap without those three datapoints risks double-dimming the surfaces that already work OR visually drifting them. The task captures all three asks.

**NDI media-direct sender — follow-up project task**: queued because the architectural rework is multi-release scope (new ndi-service code path that owns its own decoder, bypassing BrowserWindow frame capture for media-video slides; routing logic in main.ts to decide when to use direct-send vs renderer-capture; lifecycle hand-off when a media slide arrives/leaves; resolution / framerate / colour-space negotiation that today is implicit via the renderer's capture). Quick wins in this release (Uint32Array SIMD fill) reduce the per-frame cost but cannot match EasyWorship's GPU shared-surface output without the architectural change.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Runtime behaviour for both fixes is operator-visual on real hardware (library tile thumbnail rendering AT upload time, NDI receiver smoothness in vMix/OBS at 1080p30) — not assertable from this sandbox. Field validation closes the loop.

## v0.7.232 — Preview + Live transport bars stay visible at narrow panel heights

**Operator escalation against v0.7.231**: when the operator drags the horizontal splitter between the top row (Preview + Live Display) and the bottom row (Chapter Navigator / Detected Verses / Media) upward to make the bottom row taller, the Preview transport bar (▶/⬛/🔁 + scrubber) AND the Live transport bar (⏸/⬛/🔁 + scrubber, plus the LiveBottomControls toolbar above it) disappear from view. Operator screenshot shows the video preview frame extending all the way to the bottom of the Card with no transport visible — the controls are still in the DOM but clipped by the Card body's `overflow-hidden`.

**Root cause**: both PreviewCard (logos-shell.tsx L1287) and LiveDisplayCard (L2090) use the same body layout pattern — a `flex-1 min-h-0` stage div containing the video frame, followed by `shrink-0` siblings for Clear-Preview / Pause / VideoTransport rows. The video frame inside the stage uses `aspectRatio` (from the v0.7.230 `resolveMediaFit` helper) which contributes an intrinsic size based on `width / ratio`. At narrow panel heights, the width-derived height exceeds the stage's `flex-1` allocation; the aspect-ratio'd content pushes the stage taller than allowed; the body's `overflow-hidden` then clips the bottom of the Card — exactly where the `shrink-0` transport sibling lives. `min-h-0` on the stage allows flex to compute a 0 lower bound, but the aspect-ratio's intrinsic-size contribution races that and wins on the layout pass.

**Fix**: add `overflow-hidden` to BOTH stages (logos-shell.tsx L1340 preview, L2202 live). With `overflow-hidden` on the stage, the aspect-ratio video frame is clipped INSIDE the stage's flex-1 allocation rather than pushing past it. Visual result: at very narrow panel heights, the operator sees a letter-boxed/clipped video frame (preferred — they can still see what's queued) AND the transport bar remains visible (correct — they can still scrub or play). At normal panel heights, behaviour is identical to before — aspect-ratio still drives the frame proportions, `overflow-hidden` is just an invisible safety net.

**GUARD-RAIL (4-property invariant on Card video stages)**: every Card body that mounts an aspect-ratio'd video frame + has shrink-0 footer rows MUST use the full `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden flex items-stretch` quartet on its stage div. Dropping `overflow-hidden` (the v0.7.231-and-earlier mistake) re-opens this exact clipping bug whenever the panel shrinks. Dropping `min-h-0` re-opens it differently (flex won't compute a 0 lower bound at all). Dropping `items-stretch` breaks the audio-rail-plus-video-frame side-by-side layout. The Card primitive at L153 does NOT enforce this — it only sets `flex-1 min-h-0 flex flex-col overflow-y-auto overflow-x-hidden` on the body wrapper itself. Stages WITHIN the body are an artifact-author responsibility.

**GUARD-RAIL (no `<VideoTransport>` wrapper churn)**: VideoTransport's root `<div>` already has `shrink-0` at L1690 (since v0.7.193). The v0.7.232 root-cause investigation initially suspected the transport was shrinking — it was not. Wrapping it in extra `shrink-0` divs is unnecessary and was rejected. The fix is upstream (clip the stage, not pad the transport).

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Runtime behaviour (drag splitter up → transport stays visible → video letter-boxes) is operator-visual on real Electron — not assertable from this sandbox. Operator field validation closes the loop.

---

## v0.7.231 — Reverse v0.7.227/v0.7.222 standby chrome + paint media-tile thumbnails

**Operator escalation (2 issues against v0.7.230)**: (1) "when users upload media, they can't see what they upload" — the Media-column grid renders uploaded video clips as text-only tiles (filename + "Video" label) because v0.7.222 Fix #5 deliberately suppressed thumbnail painting with `preload="none"` to avoid HW-decoder probes during the v0.7.222 LIVE-pane contention episode. (2) "what is that showing in the preview" + "clear it from there now" — operator screenshot circled the amber STANDBY badge (top-left) and the amber "Play preview" CTA strip (bottom of the freezeBg poster) introduced in v0.7.227 Part B and kept through v0.7.230. Operator expects single-click → poster appears in preview → press the existing `▶` button in the Preview transport bar (rendered unconditionally for media-video preview slides at L1551 just below the Card) to monitor with audio. The amber CTA inside the Card was a SECOND play control that operators didn't realise was the only one that worked while standby was active — the bottom-bar `▶` was wired up but did nothing because `findVideo()` returned null (standby poster has no `data-surface="preview"`).

### Fix 1 — Drop the amber STANDBY badge + "Play preview" CTA overlays

`logos-shell.tsx` L1471-1489 (the two `<div>` overlays inside the v0.7.227 freezeBg-poster branch) are removed. The freezeBg poster `<video>` ABOVE them stays — that's the actual visual confirmation operators wanted from v0.7.227. The chrome on top of the poster (amber pill + gradient-backed "Preview ready · Live is on air" + clip title + "Play preview" button) is gone. v0.7.231 reverts the v0.7.227 Part B UX decision while keeping the v0.7.227 freezeBg lifecycle intact (`preload="auto"`, no autoplay, no loop, `muted`, `playsInline`, `loadeddata→play().then(pause).catch(pause)`, `onPlay→setTimeout(pause, 0)`). The Preview transport bar at the bottom of the Card is now the single source of truth for preview playback. **GUARD-RAIL**: the freezeBg `<video>` lifecycle properties MUST stay intact (per the v0.7.225 / v0.7.227 / v0.7.230 lockstep) — only the two overlay `<div>`s should be removed. Re-adding any chrome inside the standby branch should be done with extreme caution; operators perceived it as a competing control to the transport bar.

### Fix 2 — VideoTransport.onPlay wakes the standby gate

`logos-shell.tsx` `VideoTransport.onPlay` (~L1623) used to do `if (!el) return` BEFORE flipping `setMediaPaused(false)`. Standby branch deliberately omits `data-surface="preview"` on its poster `<video>` (we don't want transport scrubs to apply to a muted first-frame poster), so `findVideo()` returned null while standby was active → Play button was a no-op → operator perception "the play button doesn't work when live is on air." Fix: flip the flag FIRST, then attempt the `.play()`. Flipping `previewMediaPaused=false` un-renders the standby branch (5-condition gate AND-requires `previewMediaPaused === true`); MediaVideoSurface mounts; autoPlay fires on mount per the v0.7.216 gate (`autoPlay={!mediaPaused && surface === 'preview'}`); the rAF poll loop picks up the newly-mounted element on the next tick and the transport stays in sync. **GUARD-RAIL**: `setMediaPaused(false)` MUST come BEFORE the `findVideo()` call in `onPlay`. The `onPause` symmetric path keeps the original order (`if (!el) return` first) because pause without an existing element is a no-op by definition — only the play path needs the wake-up behaviour. **GUARD-RAIL**: standby poster `<video>` MUST NOT carry `data-surface="preview"` — adding it would let the transport scrub a paused poster that has no decoder backing, producing visual stutter or AbortError spam.

### Fix 3 — Paint first-frame thumbnails for library tiles

`library-compact.tsx` (~L1457-1495) video-tile `<video>` switched from `preload="none"` (v0.7.222 Fix #5) to the v0.7.225 freezeBg pattern: `preload="auto"` + `play().then(pause)` in `loadeddata` + `setTimeout(pause, 0)` in `onPlay`. Cost is bounded — N tile-mounts on initial render allocate N brief HW-decoder probes (a few ms each), all released after first-frame paint. Steady-state decoder cost is ZERO (no autoplay, no loop, paused element). The v0.7.222 contention concern is satisfied by the release-after-first-frame discipline, not by refusing to ever paint. Operators with several clips of similar filenames (`Holyfire Template-1_2.mp4` / `Holyfire New Amin Logo.mp4`) can now visually distinguish them. **GUARD-RAIL**: tile `<video>` MUST keep `muted`, `playsInline`, `disablePictureInPicture`, `disableRemotePlayback`. MUST NOT add `autoplay` / `loop` / `controls`. The freezeBg lifecycle (preload=auto + loadeddata-kick + setTimeout-pause) is the FOURTH site of the same v0.7.225 pattern (alongside `route.ts` setBgVid, the standby poster, and `setPreheatVid` from v0.7.228) — any change to the lifecycle MUST be made in all four sites in lockstep. **GUARD-RAIL**: a future "lots of clips → noticeable mount cost" complaint should be addressed by generating + caching a thumbnail dataURL on UPLOAD (canvas extraction from `<video>` first frame), NOT by reverting to `preload="none"` — operators have explicitly chosen visual thumbnails over the v0.7.222 contention-avoidance posture.

**PROCESS GR — UX reversals are allowed when an operator says the v0.N pattern is confusing**: v0.7.222 / v0.7.227 / v0.7.230 each layered more chrome onto the standby surface to protect against HW-decoder contention. v0.7.231 reverses the visible chrome (badge + CTA) while keeping the technical protection (freezeBg first-frame-then-release) intact. The lesson is "operator-visible UX decisions live downstream of technical correctness; reversing the UX layer without reverting the technical layer is the right move when operators report the UX is in their way."

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Runtime behaviour (single-click while live plays video → poster appears → transport ▶ plays preview → live unaffected; uploaded videos show first-frame thumbnails) is operator-visual on real Electron + HW decoder — not assertable from this sandbox. Operator field validation closes the loop.

## v0.7.230 — Preview/output mediaFit lockstep + brightness reaches foreground + OBS BGRA compat toggle

**Operator escalation (4 issues against v0.7.227 / v0.7.229)**: (1) "the auto fit you see on every preview box in settings is NOT what's on second screen and NDI and OBS" — preview boxes letterboxed `16:9` / `4:3` clips inside a hardcoded 16/9 frame while the output renderer correctly honoured the picked aspect ratio. (2) "the brightness slider you did doesn't apply to NDI" — bgBrightness CSS vars only touched background layers, foreground sermon videos stayed at 100% on every output surface. (3) "OBS is not picking up from NDI" — BGRX FourCC (the v0.7.223 default for opaque mode) isn't enumerated by some OBS NDI Source plugin versions. (4) v0.7.229 was never actually shipped to the wire — only committed locally. v0.7.230 bundles the v0.7.229 code (predicate widening + per-clip fit on standby poster) with all four new fixes into a single signed installer.

### Fix 1 — `resolveMediaFit` shared helper: preview ↔ output now in lockstep

**Root cause**: four render surfaces each had their own copy of the `mediaFit` → CSS mapping, and only ONE of them (the output renderer at `congregation/route.ts` L1824) was the canonical 5-case version. The other three (`slide-renderer.tsx` L19, `MediaVideoSurface` L504, `standby poster` L1437) drifted to 3 cases (`'fill'` / `'stretch'` / default-`'contain'`). When the operator picked `'16:9'` or `'4:3'` from the Media-column fit picker, the preview box silently dropped the aspect-ratio request and letterboxed the clip in a hardcoded 16/9 frame, while the secondary screen, NDI, and OBS — all driven by `route.ts` — correctly applied the picked frame. Operator-perception: "what I'm previewing in the box doesn't match what the audience sees."

**Fix** — new shared helper `src/lib/media-fit.ts` exports `resolveMediaFit(fit) → { objectFit, aspect }` with the canonical 5-case mapping documented inline. `MediaVideoSurface` (`logos-shell.tsx` L517) now consumes it, AND the container `aspectRatio` style is driven by the helper's `aspect` field (falls back to `'16 / 9'` when `aspect` is `null`) — `'16:9'` / `'4:3'` clips now lock the preview frame to the picked ratio. The v0.7.229 standby poster (`logos-shell.tsx` L1448) was also rewired through the helper. `slide-renderer.tsx`'s internal `resolveMediaPresentation` is left unchanged for now — it's already 5-case and its behaviour matches the helper byte-for-byte; folding it in would be a touch outside this fix's scope.

**GUARD-RAIL (4-surface lockstep)**: ALL FOUR surfaces — output renderer (`route.ts` L1824-1830), `MediaVideoSurface` (`logos-shell.tsx` L517), standby poster (`logos-shell.tsx` L1448), `slide-renderer.tsx` L19 — MUST stay in sync. Three of the four now route through `resolveMediaFit` so drift is impossible by construction; the fourth (`slide-renderer.tsx`) is documented as the matching reference impl. A future agent who adds a new fit value (e.g. `'21:9'` ultrawide) MUST update the helper AND the inline switch in `route.ts` AND the resolveMediaPresentation copy in `slide-renderer.tsx` — adding to only one re-opens the exact preview/output drift this fix closes. The container `aspectRatio` MUST be derived from the helper's `aspect` field, NOT hardcoded — that's what makes `'16:9'` / `'4:3'` actually constrain the frame instead of silently letterboxing.

**GUARD-RAIL (`fit` prop type)**: `MediaVideoSurface.fit` is declared as `string` (legacy laxity), so the helper call casts to `Slide['mediaFit']`. Tightening the prop type would be the proper follow-up, but it'd ripple through every call site — left for a separate cleanup.

### Fix 2 — Brightness slider now dims foreground live media on NDI / secondary / OBS

**Root cause**: v0.7.227 wired bgBrightness → `--bg-opacity` / `--bg-scrim` CSS vars in `applyRender()` and bound them to the three BACKGROUND selectors (`.bg-image`, `#bgLayer > video, #bgLayer > img`, `.lt-box .lt-bg`). The FOREGROUND live media element (the `<video id="liveVideo">` for sermon clips / ID videos / hymn backings, mounted by `route.ts` L1845 — the operator's main "show this clip full-screen" path) had no opacity binding at all in its `mediaStyle` string. So when the operator dragged brightness to 50, the background dimmed everywhere (including NDI capture) BUT the foreground sermon video on top stayed at 100% — defeating the whole "dim the output for this room" purpose. Operator-perception: "the brightness slider doesn't work on NDI" because the foreground clip — which is what they were testing — never dimmed.

**Fix** — `route.ts` L1848 `mediaStyle` now includes `opacity:var(--bg-opacity,1)`. Fallback `1` (not `.85` as the bg selectors use) preserves the pre-v0.7.230 default for legacy installs / first-paint / pre-v0.7.227 persisted state: when `applyRender()` hasn't written the var yet OR when `bgBrightness` is absent from the SSE payload, foreground stays at 100% — matches operator expectation from v0.7.227 / v0.7.228 / v0.7.229. Once `applyRender()` writes the var, foreground and background unify at the operator-picked brightness on every surface (secondary screen, NDI capture, OBS browser source). Text stays at 100% (no opacity binding on `.slide-text`) because the WCAG-AA legibility guard-rail relies on full-strength text with the existing text-shadow stack — operator never asked for dimmed text and dimming it would hurt readability at low-brightness settings.

**GUARD-RAIL (fallback asymmetry)**: bg selectors keep fallback `.85` (the v0.7.226 baseline), foreground media keeps fallback `1` (the pre-v0.7.230 baseline). Both render IDENTICALLY at operator-picked brightness=85 (where `--bg-opacity=.85`). The asymmetry only matters before `applyRender()` writes the var — and at that point you want the BG already dimmed-to-baseline (no flash) AND foreground at-full (no surprise dim). Swapping either fallback breaks one of those first-paint contracts.

**GUARD-RAIL (text immunity)**: NO opacity binding on `.slide-text` / `.verse-text` / `.reference-text`. Dimming text at low brightness drops below WCAG-AA — operator complaints WILL follow. The existing text-shadow stack carries legibility; do NOT add `opacity:var(--bg-opacity)` to any text selector without first widening the text-shadow stack to compensate.

### Fix 3 — OBS Studio NDI compatibility: operator-toggleable BGRA opt-in

**Root cause**: v0.7.223 made BGRX (`0x58524742`) the opaque-mode default FourCC so receivers skip the per-pixel alpha-composite step (the canonical EasyWorship-class smoothness optimisation; vMix / Wirecast / NDI Studio Monitor all accept BGRX cleanly). Operator reports OBS Studio's NDI Source plugin — particularly older versions and certain Linux/macOS builds — refuses to enumerate or display BGRX sources, leaving the source list empty or showing a black frame even when discovery succeeds. Pre-v0.7.230 there was no opt-out: the FourCC selector at `ndi-service.ts` L663 was `senderTransparent ? BGRA : BGRX`, hardcoded.

**Fix** — operator-controllable toggle. Plumbed through 4 layers:

1. `src/lib/store.ts` — new `AppSettings.ndiForceBgraForObs?: boolean` (default `false` — preserves v0.7.223 perf for the 99% who run vMix / Wirecast / modern OBS).
2. `electron/ndi-service.ts` — `NdiStartOptions.forceBgraForObs?: boolean`, private `this.forceBgraForObs` mirrors v0.7.223's `senderTransparent` state pattern (set in `start()` from opts, reset in `stop()` so a subsequent `start()` can't inherit a stale flag). `nativeSendFrame` FourCC at L687 widened: `(this.senderTransparent || this.forceBgraForObs) ? BGRA : BGRX`.
3. `electron/main.ts` + `electron/preload.ts` + `src/lib/use-electron.ts` — type plumbing only, no logic change. main.ts forwards the field straight through; ndi-service owns the FourCC decision.
4. `src/components/views/ndi-output-panel.tsx` — new checkbox card below the Source-Name input labeled "OBS Studio compatibility (force BGRA)" with operator-facing explanation. Reads via `useAppStore`, writes via `updateSettings({ ndiForceBgraForObs })`. Included in BOTH `ndi.start` callsites (persistent restart effect + handleToggle Start path) AND in the restart guard token (so flipping the toggle WHILE NDI is running triggers a sender rebuild — the FourCC is baked into the sender at create time and cannot be live-applied).

**GUARD-RAIL (restart guard token completeness)**: `ndi-output-panel.tsx` L367 restart guard token MUST include every option that can't be live-applied to the running sender. v0.6.6 lesson: flipping a build-time-only option without rebuilding silently leaves the old value on the wire while the UI shows the new one. `forceBgraForObs` is build-time-only (FourCC is fixed at `ndilib_send_create`), so it MUST be in the token. Same applies to `transparent`, `width`, `height`, `fps`, `sourceName` (all already present). Adding any new FourCC-affecting or sender-create-affecting option in the future requires adding it here too.

**GUARD-RAIL (state reset on stop)**: `ndi-service.ts` `stop()` MUST reset `this.forceBgraForObs = false` alongside `this.senderTransparent = false`. The pattern is "opts capture in start, opts reset in stop, so the next start observes the operator's CURRENT pick not a stale one." Adding another sender-state opts field in the future requires both touch points.

**GUARD-RAIL (default OFF)**: `AppSettings.ndiForceBgraForObs` default MUST stay `false`. Flipping to default-`true` undoes the v0.7.223 EW-class smoothness optimisation for every operator on every install — only flip the per-operator setting, never the default.

### Fix 4 — v0.7.229 actually shipped this time

v0.7.229 was committed locally as `aca92d1` but never pushed to `origin` and no GH Actions installer run was triggered, so no operator could download it. v0.7.230 supersedes v0.7.229 entirely — the v0.7.229 fixes (predicate widening for legacy live videos in `library-compact.tsx` + `logos-shell.tsx`, and per-clip mediaFit on the standby poster) all ship inside the v0.7.230 installer. CHANGELOG entry for v0.7.229 stays in this file for historical accuracy; replit.md folds v0.7.229 to a one-liner since it's no longer a standalone release.

**Tests**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` clean. Runtime behaviour for all four fixes is operator-visual on real hardware (preview vs output frame comparison, brightness slider effect on NDI receiver, OBS source discovery) — not assertable from this sandbox. Operator field validation closes the loop.

**Process GR**: when an operator-facing setting changes a sender-create-time property (FourCC, resolution, framerate, sourceName, transparent flag), the change MUST be plumbed through ALL FIVE layers (settings type, settings default, ndi-service state field + start/stop handling, main.ts type forward, preload type + use-electron type, panel UI control + 2 callsites + restart guard token + dep array). Skipping any one breaks either the operator's ability to change the setting (UI), persistence (defaults), or live application (restart token). This v0.7.230 fix touched all five — the next sender-create-time setting must too.
