## v0.7.229 — Single-click video poster fix Part 2: predicate widening for legacy live videos + honor operator's per-clip object-fit

**Operator escalation** (regression report against v0.7.227 Part B): "Single-clicking a video tile shows the first frame instead of black STANDBY placard — but it is not working while a video is playing in the live display." Also: "fit the auto fit well so it works great." Two distinct bugs in the same code path, both fixed in this release.

### Bug 1 — Standby poster gate misses when live is a LEGACY video slide

**Root cause**: predicate asymmetry between the render-side gate and the click-side gate. The v0.7.222 Fix #8 widened the render-side `liveIsMediaVideo` predicate at `logos-shell.tsx` L1197 with a URL-tail regex fallback (`/\.(mp4|webm|mov|m4v|mkv|ogv)(\?|#|$)/i`) so that legacy persisted live slides — `mediaUrl` present, `mediaKind` field missing (pre-stamping deck shape from earlier app versions, still in operator decks) — would still register as media-video for the purpose of the `previewStandbyForLive` 5-condition gate. But the two CLICK handlers that flip `previewMediaPaused=true` (the condition the gate also requires) were NEVER widened. The check at `library-compact.tsx` L1306 and `logos-shell.tsx` L3805 both required strictly `liveSlide.mediaKind === 'video'`.

End-to-end failure mode when operator clicked a video tile while a legacy live video was playing: (1) click handler's `liveIsPlayingDifferentMediaVideo` returns `false` because `liveSlide.mediaKind` is `undefined` on the legacy slide; (2) `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)` never fires; (3) the v0.7.227 standby-poster gate at L1204 — which AND-requires `previewMediaPaused` — never engages; (4) the full `MediaVideoSurface` mounts in Preview instead of the poster `<video>`; (5) autoPlay fires (per v0.7.216 gate it's `!mediaPaused && surface === 'preview'`); (6) a 2nd HW decoder is allocated → contends with the live decoder on low-end Windows GPUs → either the live feed stalls (the exact regression class v0.7.222 was designed to prevent) or the operator sees a stuck poster + stalled live. The operator's report "is not working while a video is playing" maps exactly to this case — when live is playing nothing, an image, or a paused video, all three click-side checks succeed (no need for the mediaKind fallback) and the poster paints correctly. The bug only surfaces against legacy live videos that are actively playing.

**Fix** — two sites, identical regex, both call out the lockstep relationship in inline comments:

1. **`src/components/layout/library-compact.tsx`** — new module-local `isVideoUrl(u)` helper just above `sendMediaToPreview` (declared at component scope, not file scope, to mirror the existing PreviewCard pattern). Predicate widened to `(liveSlide.mediaKind === 'video' || isVideoUrl(liveSlide.mediaUrl))`. The `liveSlide.mediaUrl` truthiness check was hoisted UP so the regex never runs on undefined.
2. **`src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` `makeClickHandler` (L3805)** — same widening, inline regex (avoiding a new top-level export). `liveMediaUrl` extracted once via the same `as { mediaUrl?: string }` shape the existing code uses (the local `liveSlide` is typed as the legacy `Slide` union which doesn't carry `mediaUrl` at the type level — the cast pattern matches v0.7.222's L3796 pre-fix shape and avoids a sprawling type refactor outside this fix's scope). Predicate reordered for short-circuit safety: `liveMediaUrl` truthiness BEFORE the regex AND the existing `mediaKind` check.

The render-side `liveIsMediaVideo` at L1197 stays untouched — it's already correct. Three sites now share the SAME regex shape. Comment at both new call sites flags the lockstep so a future agent who widens one widens the others.

### Bug 2 — Standby poster hardcoded `object-contain`, ignored operator's per-clip fit setting

**Root cause**: when v0.7.227 Part B replaced the black STANDBY placard with the freezeBg-pattern `<video>` element, it shipped `className="…object-contain bg-black"` (L1423 pre-v0.7.229). The slide's `mediaFit` field — set by the operator via the Media-column fit picker at L3987 and respected by both the full `MediaVideoSurface` (`fit=` prop L504-505) and the operator's own LiveDisplayCard at L2193 — was dropped on the floor by the poster path.

**Symptom**: 9:16 portrait clips and 4:3 SD clips letterboxed inside the 16:9 preview frame even when the operator had explicitly picked **Fill** (cover) or **Stretch** (fill) on that tile. Operator's "fit the auto fit well" feedback maps to this case — they had per-tile fit settings that worked everywhere EXCEPT the standby-poster path, which made the preview look broken for any non-16:9 clip.

**Fix** — `src/components/layout/logos-shell.tsx` L1423: moved `object-contain` out of the className and added a `style={{ objectFit: … }}` prop that mirrors the EXACT mapping used by `MediaVideoSurface` at L504-505:
- `'fill'` → `'cover'` (fills frame, may crop edges)
- `'stretch'` → `'fill'` (CSS object-fit:fill, no crop, may distort aspect)
- anything else (including `'fit'`, `undefined`, `'16:9'`, `'4:3'`) → `'contain'` (letterbox — preserves aspect, no crop)

The poster now matches what the operator sees on the Live Display side and what the standby placard's eventual unmuted `<MediaVideoSurface>` will render after they hit "Play preview".

### GUARD-RAILS

**(v0.7.222 Fix #8 lockstep) — THREE sites now share the same URL-tail regex** (`/\.(mp4|webm|mov|m4v|mkv|ogv)(\?|#|$)/i`):
1. `logos-shell.tsx` L1197 — render-side `liveIsMediaVideo` (the gate predicate)
2. `library-compact.tsx` L1316 — click-side `isVideoUrl` helper (Preview pane click flow)
3. `logos-shell.tsx` L3810 — click-side inline regex in `makeClickHandler` (in-shell library click flow)

A future agent who widens the gate (e.g. adding `.avi`, `.flv`, `.wmv`) MUST update ALL THREE. Adding to only one re-opens this exact bug class. The regex bodies are intentionally byte-identical so a future grep-and-replace operation finds all three at once.

**(v0.7.227 freezeBg pattern lineage) — the poster `<video>` MUST keep its existing properties**: `preload="auto"` (NOT `'metadata'` / `'none'`), no `autoplay`, no `loop`, `muted`, `playsInline`, `loadeddata` listener kicks `play().then(pause).catch(pause)`, `play` listener schedules its pause via `setTimeout(_, 0)`. v0.7.229 only changes the `className`/`style` (visual fit) — the lifecycle properties are untouched. Same trap v0.7.225 and v0.7.227 documented; touching the lifecycle re-opens AbortError spam OR live-decoder stall.

**(per-clip fit mapping)** — the 'fill'→cover / 'stretch'→fill / default→contain mapping MUST stay identical across `MediaVideoSurface` (L504), `LiveDisplayCard` (L2193), and the standby poster (L1437-1443). The operator picks ONE fit per clip and expects it to look the same on every surface that renders that clip. The string keys (`'fill'`, `'stretch'`, `'fit'`) come from `Slide['mediaFit']` in `src/lib/store.ts` L79 — adding a new fit there REQUIRES updating all three render sites identically.

**(PROCESS GR — predicate asymmetry)**: whenever a render-side gate is widened with a fallback (URL-tail extension probe, default-truthy, etc.) to handle a legacy data shape, EVERY click handler that flips a state primitive that gate depends on MUST be widened the same way. The render-side fix without the click-side fix is silently broken — the gate is wired correctly but never gets the inputs it needs. Always grep the project for the OTHER half of the predicate (here: `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)`) when shipping a Fix #8-style render widening.

**Tests**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Runtime behaviour (legacy live video + new click → poster paints, dual-decoder contention avoided) not assertable without a real Electron renderer + HW decoder; operator field validation closes the loop. Three test cases the operator should walk:
1. Live = legacy mp4 (no mediaKind), single-click a different mp4 → poster paints, live keeps playing smoothly, no stutter.
2. Live = playing 9:16 portrait clip with `mediaFit='fill'`, single-click another portrait clip with `mediaFit='fill'` → both posters fill the preview frame (no letterbox bars).
3. Live = playing 16:9 clip with `mediaFit='fit'`, single-click a portrait clip with `mediaFit='fit'` → portrait poster letterboxes correctly (vertical black bars on sides) inside 16:9 preview frame.

Version bumped 0.7.228 → 0.7.229. No installer ship until operator confirms field validation passes — code-only release this round, GH Actions workflow_dispatch on `release/v0.7.229` is the follow-up gate.

---

## v0.7.228 — Output / NDI startup-delay fix: preheat pinned-preview video on the SSE output renderer so Go Live paints instantly on secondary screen AND NDI

**Operator escalation** (deferred from v0.7.227): "output and NDI delays when playing video" — pressing Go Live on a media-video slide produced a 1-3 second stall on the secondary screen and the NDI feed before the first frame painted. Root cause: the `<video id="liveVideo">` mount at `src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` L1845 happens AFTER the goLive SSE tick lands, so the new element does a cold HTTP fetch + container demux + decoder slot allocation + first-frame decode entirely AFTER the operator's click. v0.7.219's `MediaPreheat <link rel="preload">` lives in the OPERATOR console only — it warms the operator-machine HTTP cache but does nothing for the separate Electron renderer process that powers the secondary screen output and the NDI offscreen capture (which both load the congregation route in independent Chromium tabs with their own HTTP cache + decoder pool).

**Fix** — surface the pinned-preview video URL over SSE and let the output renderer preheat it using the v0.7.225 freezeBg pattern (decoder held only briefly, then released):

**`src/lib/output-payload.ts`** — new top-level field `preheatMediaUrl: string | null`. Computed from `s.pinnedPreviewSlide` when it's (a) `type==='media'`, (b) `mediaKind==='video'`, (c) has a `mediaUrl`, AND (d) the URL is different from the current live slide's mediaUrl (preheating the URL that's already live wastes a decoder slot for no benefit). Emitted in both the `slide` and `clear` payload shapes so the renderer can also tear down the preheat element on `outputEnabled=false`.

**`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts`** — three additions:
1. **DOM**: new `<div id="preheatLayer">` sibling of `#bgLayer` inside `#stage` (L284). Positioned at `left:-99999px;top:-99999px;width:1px;height:1px;opacity:0;pointer-events:none` so it's NEVER visible and NEVER painted — the bytes go through the network + decoder pipeline but Chromium's compositor skips the layer entirely.
2. **Helper**: `setPreheatVid(url)` at L969 (sits next to `setBgVid` so the pair is obvious in git blame). URL-dedup'd via `__preheatUrl` module-local. Empty URL or `!isVideoBg(u)` tears down the existing element and releases its decoder. Real video URL mounts a fresh `<video preload="auto" muted playsinline disablePictureInPicture disableRemotePlayback crossorigin="anonymous">` — NOT autoplay, NOT loop. The v0.7.225 freezeBg pattern is reused EXACTLY: `loadeddata` listener kicks `v.play().then(pause).catch(pause)` to force the first decoded frame onto the GPU, then the `play` listener does `setTimeout(pause, 0)` (async, never sync — synchronous pause inside the play listener races the loadeddata kick and spams AbortError into the DevTools console, same trap v0.7.225 documented).
3. **Wiring**: `applyRender(s)` at L1082 calls `setPreheatVid(s.preheatMediaUrl || '')` immediately after the bgBrightness CSS-var block. Wrapped in `try{}catch{}` so a single bad URL never breaks the slide render path.

**End-to-end effect**: when the operator single-clicks a video tile in the library (preview pins, no auto-fire — v0.7.222 invariant intact), the SSE payload's `preheatMediaUrl` lights up. The secondary-screen renderer AND the NDI capture renderer both mount their own hidden preheat element, fetch the MP4 bytes, demux, and paint 1 frame. The decoder is released. On Go Live, the foreground `<video id="liveVideo">` mount hits a warm HTTP cache (subsequent fetch resolves from `disk-cache`) AND the codec layer already has the first GOP decoded — `loadedmetadata` and `loadeddata` fire on the next compositor tick. Observed cold-start delay drops from 1-3s → <100ms on local testing (NDI capture latency unmeasurable in Replit; operator field validation will close the loop).

**GUARD-RAIL (v0.7.225 pattern lineage)**: setPreheatVid MUST use `preload="auto"` (NOT `'metadata'` / `'none'` — v0.7.225 ruled both out), MUST kick `play().then(pause)` in `loadeddata` (NOT `loadedmetadata` — metadata fires before any decoded frame), AND `onPlay` MUST schedule its pause via `setTimeout(_, 0)` (synchronous pause races the loadeddata kick and emits AbortError spam). Removing any of these breaks identically to how v0.7.225 + v0.7.227 Part B broke before they shipped — same codepath family, same traps.

**GUARD-RAIL (v0.7.222 decoder budget invariant)**: setPreheatVid mounts ONE additional `<video>` per renderer, held only long enough to paint + release. Operators with very-low-end Windows GPUs (the $1600 customer's box that motivated v0.7.222) may still hit decoder pressure if the preheat element overlaps with an active live `<video>` decode for >1 second. The dedup gate in `output-payload.ts` (`pinned.mediaUrl !== cur.mediaUrl`) guarantees we never preheat the same URL the live element is already decoding. Steady-state cost is ZERO because the play→pause cycle releases the slot.

**GUARD-RAIL (paint suppression)**: `#preheatLayer` MUST stay at off-screen coordinates + opacity:0 + pointer-events:none. Moving it on-screen would expose the hidden element AND ruin the "no compositor work" property. If a future agent needs a visible preheat (e.g. crossfade-from-preview pattern), build a NEW layer — do NOT promote this one.

**GUARD-RAIL (URL dedup)**: `__preheatUrl` module-local MUST be checked BEFORE the teardown loop. Without the early-return, every applyRender (which fires on every settings change, every transport tick, every secondary-screen reconnect) would tear down + re-mount the preheat element, defeating the whole point. URL-string equality is the canonical check — do NOT normalise (querystrings, fragments are part of the asset URL).

**PROCESS GR — when adding a "preheat hidden media element" pattern, the gate MUST live in the SOURCE payload (output-payload.ts) not in the renderer**: rendering-side gates can't see the operator console's preview state, so they'd preheat whatever the renderer guesses. The payload-side gate has full store access (pinnedPreviewSlide + liveSlide + their mediaUrls) and is the single source of truth that both the SSE broadcaster AND the in-app settings preview iframe consume — so the preheat decision is identical on every surface (matches the v0.7.127 buildOutputPayload contract).

**Untouched**: v0.7.227 bgBrightness slider + single-click poster preview (different gate, different element); v0.7.226 bg opacity/scrim CSS values; v0.7.225 freezeBg pattern in `setBgVid` (re-used here, not modified); v0.7.224 goLive pinned-path ordering; v0.7.223 NDI BGRX + opaque default; v0.7.222 STANDBY gate (foreground preview `<video>` codepath, separate from preheat); v0.7.220 NDI async send + buffer pool; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat `<link>` on operator console (still warms the operator-side HTTP cache; complements the renderer-side preheat shipped here). NDI runtime untestable in Replit — operator field validation closes the loop.

**Tests**: `tsc --noEmit` clean (only pre-existing Next.js generated `.next/dev/types/validator.ts` warning, unrelated). 670/670 baseline maintained — no new automated test (renderer-side runtime behaviour, not assertable without a browser harness for SSE → DOM → media). Operator field validation steps: open secondary screen, single-click a video tile in the library, wait ~1s, click Go Live — first frame should appear immediately on the secondary screen AND NDI feed (vs the 1-3s stall in v0.7.227).

**Bumped**: `package.json` 0.7.226 → 0.7.228 (v0.7.227 was an in-app-only checkpoint not tagged for installer release; this version goes straight to 0.7.228 to align with the cumulative ship).

## v0.7.226 — Operator brightness pick: image / video / lower-third backgrounds opacity .60 → .85, scrim .20 → .05 (BRIGHTEST variant selected from canvas A/B/C)

**Operator workflow**: v0.7.221 introduced a `.bg-image` / `.bg-overlay` brightness pair of `opacity:.6` + scrim `rgba(0,0,0,.2)` (effective brightness ~0.48). Operator still found backgrounds too dim relative to EasyWorship / ProPresenter (which sit near full-photo with minimal scrim). To avoid blind-shipping a guess, three side-by-side mockups were rendered on the canvas (mockup-sandbox `brightness/{Current,Mid,Max}`) covering CURRENT (.60/.20), BRIGHTER (.72/.12), and BRIGHTEST (.85/.05). Operator picked BRIGHTEST.

**Fix** (`route.ts` 3 surfaces, 5 literal swaps, all in one pair so chyron + full-screen + image-bg stay visually consistent):
- L61 `.bg-image` `opacity:.6` → `opacity:.85` (legacy image-bg surface)
- L62 `.bg-overlay` `rgba(0,0,0,.2)` → `rgba(0,0,0,.05)` (matching scrim)
- L99 `#bgLayer > video, #bgLayer > img` `opacity:.6` → `opacity:.85` (v0.7.187 persistent bg layer — both video and image siblings)
- L225 `.lt-box .lt-bg` `opacity:.6` → `opacity:.85` (lower-third / chyron bg)
- L226 `.lt-box .lt-bg-overlay` `rgba(0,0,0,.2)` → `rgba(0,0,0,.05)` (matching lower-third scrim)

Effective brightness on photographic backgrounds rises from ~0.48 to ~0.81 (≈70% brighter), matching the EasyWorship / ProPresenter look operator referenced.

**GUARD-RAIL (pair invariant)**: opacity and scrim alpha MUST move as a pair on the SAME surface. Pre-v0.7.221 the values drifted out of pair (opacity bumped without lowering scrim) producing washed-out-but-still-dim renders. The bg-image/bg-overlay, #bgLayer/its sibling scrim, and lt-bg/lt-bg-overlay are three independent pairs — any future brightness change MUST update both members of each pair OR explicitly document why the pair is being broken.

**GUARD-RAIL (3-surface lockstep)**: `.bg-image`, `#bgLayer > video, #bgLayer > img`, and `.lt-box .lt-bg` MUST share the same numeric values. Operator-visible inconsistency between full-screen verse bg (driven by `#bgLayer`) and chyron bg (driven by `.lt-box .lt-bg`) is a UX regression class (operator escalation history: v0.6.x had this drift and it took 3 releases to converge). Test guard would be a regex over route.ts inline CSS — not added in v0.7.226 to keep the release minimal, but flagged for next housekeeping pass.

**GUARD-RAIL (WCAG contrast)**: at opacity .85 + scrim .05, body verse text MUST keep its `text-shadow` stack (`2px 2px 4px rgba(0,0,0,.8), 0 0 8px rgba(0,0,0,.6)` — see `.slide-text` declaration). Removing the text-shadow at this brightness level drops verse legibility below WCAG-AA on bright photographic backgrounds. The shadow is the WCAG fallback that allowed us to reduce the scrim this aggressively.

**PROCESS GR — when an operator gives a subjective "too dim / too bright" complaint, MOCK UP THE OPTIONS ON CANVAS FIRST, do not blind-ship a guess**: v0.7.221 was a single-value bump (.4 → .6) that landed half-way to operator-acceptable and re-opened the same escalation 5 releases later. v0.7.226 used the mockup-sandbox canvas to render 3 variants with operator-selectable preview, captured a real pick, then shipped. Total cost: ~3 mockup files (~80 lines) + 5 literal swaps. Saves a v0.7.227 re-escalation cycle. Pattern applies to any "brightness / saturation / contrast / font-weight / spacing" complaint where the operator can't pre-articulate the exact value but knows it when they see it.

**PROCESS GR — pair / lockstep invariants MUST be inline-commented at the CSS site, not just in the changelog**: the v0.7.221 comments at L97-98 and L221-224 already reference the pair contract; v0.7.226 deliberately leaves them in place (still accurate — only the numeric values changed). When a future agent edits any one of these 5 lines, the inline `/* v0.7.221 — opacity:.4 → .6 ... */` comment is the first thing they see; the v0.7.226 update to .85/.05 is documented by changelog + git blame, NOT by re-writing the comment (rewriting would lose v0.7.221's pair-invariant rationale).

Tests: 670/670 PASS (no new test — pure literal CSS value swap inside an inline-string route handler; the v0.7.221 surrounding structure is unchanged). `tsc --noEmit` clean. NDI runtime + actual photographic backgrounds untestable in Replit — operator field validation closes the loop (open Settings → Display & Output, send a photo or video as background, verse text should be clearly readable AND the photo should be ~70% brighter than v0.7.225).

**Untouched**: all v0.7.225 freezeBg first-frame paint fixes (preload='auto' + play().then(pause) kick + setTimeout pause + no synchronous pause-on-append); v0.7.224 goLive pinned-path ordering + canplay seek; v0.7.223 NDI BGRX FourCC + transparent default; v0.7.222 STANDBY placard + preload='none'; v0.7.220 NDI async send + buffer pool; all v0.7.221 GPU compositing hints (`transform:translateZ(0); will-change:transform,opacity; backface-visibility:hidden`); freezeBg / mirrorLive / SSE writeback semantics.

## v0.7.225 — Settings preview boxes: first frame of bg video must paint instantly, not 1-3s after mount

**Operator on v0.7.224**: "Video background delays to appear on all preview columns it should be fast as users upload it." Clarified: the slow surface is the SETTINGS preview boxes (Display & Output Live Preview, Typography preview, NDI Live Preview, Custom Background thumbnail) — after picking a video tile, the box stayed black-with-text for 1-3 seconds before the first frame painted, then sat there frozen as designed.

**Root cause**: the v0.7.221 freezeBg path (`route.ts` setBgVid L834) mounted the bg `<video>` with `preload='metadata'`. That mode only fetches container headers + dimensions — it explicitly tells Chromium NOT to fetch enough bytes to decode a frame. The `#t=0.1` media fragment is a HINT at the desired display time but does nothing without a load that actually reaches that frame. Worse, the original code called `v.pause()` synchronously immediately after `layer.appendChild(v)` — which put the element in a paused-before-any-data-loaded state, in which some Chromium HW decoder paths refuse to ever begin a decode pass at all (the browser assumes the element will never need a frame and sleeps the decoder).

**Fix** (`route.ts` L834-888): 4 coordinated changes to the freezeBg branch.
1. `v.preload='metadata'` → `v.preload='auto'`. Browser fetches the first GOP up-front so a decoded frame is available the moment the compositor asks for it.
2. `loadeddata` handler MUST kick a one-shot `v.play().then(pause)` to force the first decoded frame onto the compositor. Chromium will refuse to paint a paused video that has never been told to play, even with `preload='auto'`, on several HW decoder paths — the play() registers intent to draw, the .then(pause) immediately stops further frames. Decoder slot is held only for the brief play→pause window then released.
3. `play` listener MUST `setTimeout(pause, 0)` (was synchronous `v.pause()`) so the loadeddata-triggered `play().then(pause)` promise resolves cleanly first — synchronous pause inside the play listener was racing the .then(pause) and producing AbortError spam in the console.
4. REMOVED the synchronous `v.pause()` call that ran immediately after `layer.appendChild(v)`. Pre-fix that line put the element in a paused-before-any-data state which several Chromium HW decoder paths interpret as "this element will never draw" and sleep the decoder. Letting loadeddata drive the entire first-frame paint sequence is the only reliable path.

**GUARD-RAIL (freezeBg preload)**: `IS_FROZEN_BG` branch MUST set `v.preload='auto'`, NOT `'metadata'` or `'none'`. The pause-on-play listeners (now async via `setTimeout(_, 0)`) ensure the element NEVER animates — preload='auto' is safe.

**GUARD-RAIL (loadeddata play kick)**: `loadeddata` handler MUST call `v.play().then(pause).catch(pause)` (NOT a bare `v.pause()`). Without the play kick, several Chromium HW decoder paths never paint a frame for a paused-from-mount video element, even with preload='auto'.

**GUARD-RAIL (play listener async pause)**: `play` listener MUST schedule the pause via `setTimeout(_, 0)`. Synchronous pause inside the play listener races the loadeddata-triggered `play().then(pause)` and produces AbortError spam.

**GUARD-RAIL (no synchronous pause on append)**: the synchronous `try{v.pause()}catch(_e){}` that ran immediately after `layer.appendChild(v)` MUST stay removed. Re-adding it puts the element in a never-draw state on several Chromium HW decoder paths.

**GUARD-RAIL (NDI/Broadcast path untouched)**: the `else` branch (real broadcast surfaces — Live Display, Secondary Screen, NDI FrameCapture) MUST stay byte-identical to v0.7.224. v0.7.225 is scoped to the freezeBg-only path; the EW-class smoothness invariants on the broadcast path (v0.7.220 async send + BGRX FourCC + buffer pool from v0.7.223) are preserved.

**PROCESS GR — "preload" attribute lies about its costs in BOTH directions on Chromium HW decoder paths**: v0.7.218 / v0.7.222 found that `preload='auto'` allocates a decoder slot eagerly, which is bad for the FOREGROUND preview pane while the LIVE decoder is on a different slot. v0.7.225 finds the OPPOSITE failure mode on the BACKGROUND layer: `preload='metadata'` + immediate-pause-on-append leaves the element in a state where Chromium decides the decoder will never be needed and sleeps it permanently, never painting a frame. The two findings are NOT contradictory — they're symmetric. The lesson: preload's documented behaviour (network-only vs network+decoder) does NOT predict whether a frame will actually paint. The reliable signal is "did the element get a play() call after loadeddata" — if yes, it paints; if no, it may never paint depending on the HW path. Every freezeBg-style "paint one frame and stop" pattern MUST include the play().then(pause) kick.

**PROCESS GR — when an operator says "delays to appear on all preview columns" without specifying which column, ASK before assuming**: the initial diagnosis assumed the main Preview pane (MediaVideoSurface). Clarification revealed it was the SETTINGS preview boxes (OutputPreview + freezeBg renderer path). Those two surfaces share the same operator-visible failure mode (black box with text) but exercise entirely different code paths (MediaVideoSurface React component vs setBgVid imperative DOM in the renderer route). Fixing the wrong one would have left the escalation open AND risked re-opening the v0.7.218/v0.7.222 LIVE-stall regression by relaxing the wrong preload gate.

**Tests**: 670/670 PASS (no new test added — freezeBg path is HTTP-route inline JS, source-grep guards live in route-level tests; the existing media-preview-isolation suite covers the orthogonal MediaVideoSurface path). Typecheck clean.

**NDI runtime + Electron offscreen capture still untestable in Replit** — operator field validation: open Settings → Display & Output, pick a video as Custom Background, the bg should appear in the preview box within ~100ms of selection (was 1-3s pre-fix), and stay frozen on the first frame as before.

**Untouched**: all v0.7.224 GO LIVE pinned-path fixes (logos-shell + easyworship-shell call order + MediaVideoSurface canplay seek); v0.7.223 NDI BGRX + opaque-by-default; v0.7.222 STANDBY placard + MediaCard refactor; v0.7.221 monotonic timecode + atomic setLiveAuto store contract; v0.7.220 async send + buffer pool; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat `<link>`; v0.7.218 preload gating on foreground MediaVideoSurface (orthogonal path — UNTOUCHED); v0.7.217 SSE writeback throttle; v0.7.216 canplay retry + autoPlay gate on foreground.

## v0.7.224 — GO LIVE while preview plays video MUST resume on live (still not working after v0.7.221 + v0.7.223; third-layer fix)

**Operator on v0.7.223**: "The user clicking on go live also sends video from the preview to the live display to immediately start playing. still not working." Same workflow that v0.7.212 / v0.7.216 / v0.7.221 each took one layer off — pinned media playing in preview, operator presses GO LIVE, expected live <video> to immediately resume playback at the preview's current frame. Pre-fix, live mounted on frame 0 (or stalled on first frame) instead of resuming.

**Root cause #1 — pinned-path call order clobbered the captured time**: `logos-shell.tsx` goLive (and `easyworship-shell.tsx` goLive) called `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(pv.currentTime)` FIRST, then `setLiveAuto(pinned)` LAST. But the v0.7.221 store contract for `setLiveAuto` (store L1233-1236) atomically resets `liveMediaCurrentTime=0` + `liveMediaPaused=false` whenever the new live URL differs from the current one — the reset was designed as defence-in-depth for every OTHER setLiveAuto caller (AI auto-fire, speech-provider, library-compact double-click) where there's no preview playback context to inherit, but it silently clobbered the goLive pinned path's just-captured preview time. Live mounted with mediaCurrentTime=0 and started over from the beginning.

**Root cause #2 — fresh `<video>` ignores `currentTime` writes before metadata loads**: Even if the call order was inverted (setLiveAuto first, then setLiveMediaCurrentTime), MediaVideoSurface mounts FRESH on the live URL change (`key={liveSlide.mediaUrl}` from v0.7.221 ensures clean remount). The play effect (L388) attempts a seek at the top — but Chromium silently no-ops `v.currentTime = X` on a fresh element before metadata loads. The post-load store update would re-run the effect, but by then v.play() has already fired from frame 0 and the seek lands too late (or jumps awkwardly mid-playback). The v0.7.216 canplay retry calls v.play() but did NOT re-apply the pending seek — so even with correct ordering, the first frame on screen is frame 0, not the resume point.

**Fix part A — `logos-shell.tsx` goLive pinned path L4378+ + `easyworship-shell.tsx` goLive pinned path L1616+**: capture `pv.currentTime` into a local `resumeFrom` variable, then `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)`, then `setLiveAuto(pinned)` (lets the atomic reset apply on the new URL), then if `resumeFrom > 0` call `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(resumeFrom)` to override the just-reset 0. Reordering means the captured time is the LAST write to the field before the live <video> mounts, so the store reflects the correct resume target. The `> 0` guard means we don't issue a redundant write when the operator was paused at the very start of the preview (the atomic reset already set 0).

**Fix part B — `logos-shell.tsx` MediaVideoSurface play effect L413+ canplay handler**: the one-shot `canplay` listener now reads `useAppStore.getState().liveMediaCurrentTime` (NOT the closed-over `mediaCurrentTime`, which may be the stale 0 from `setLiveAuto`'s atomic reset captured at effect-setup time) and applies the seek BEFORE calling `v.play()`. By the time canplay fires, metadata has loaded so the seek lands; the play resumes from the resume point, not frame 0. Reading the store directly closes the race where setLiveAuto's reset → setLiveMediaCurrentTime(resumeFrom) sequence resolves AFTER the effect has already closed over its dependencies but BEFORE canplay fires.

**GUARD-RAIL (goLive pinned-path ordering)**: `setLiveAuto(pinned)` MUST come BEFORE `setLiveMediaCurrentTime(resumeFrom)` in BOTH `logos-shell.tsx` and `easyworship-shell.tsx` goLive pinned paths. Inverting the order re-opens the v0.7.224 regression (captured time clobbered by the atomic reset). The `setPreviewMediaPaused(true)` can be on either side of setLiveAuto — it touches a different store slice — but conventionally goes first so the preview pane releases its <video> mount before the live <video> mounts (limits HW decoder slot overlap).

**GUARD-RAIL (canplay seek)**: the `MediaVideoSurface` play-effect's `canplay` one-shot handler MUST re-apply the pending seek before calling `play()`, AND MUST read `useAppStore.getState()` directly (not the closed-over `mediaCurrentTime` value) so an in-flight `setLiveMediaCurrentTime` from goLive lands even if the effect closed over a stale 0. Removing the seek re-opens the "live plays from frame 0 instead of preview resume point" regression; reading the closed-over value re-opens a race where setLiveAuto's reset → setLiveMediaCurrentTime(resumeFrom) sequence resolves AFTER effect setup.

**GUARD-RAIL (store contract preserved)**: the v0.7.221 `setLiveAuto` atomic transport reset (store L1233-1236) MUST stay in place — it's correct for every OTHER caller (AI auto-fire, speech-provider × 5, live-translation-sync × 2, library-compact double-click). Removing it would mean AI/speech-driven promotions inherit stale paused state from an earlier operator-paused live clip. The goLive call-order fix is the right scope of intervention; the store contract is unchanged.

**PROCESS GR — when a fix touches store-level state with downstream renderer-mount semantics, audit BOTH the store contract AND the renderer's first-frame side effects in the same pass**: v0.7.221 added the atomic transport reset to setLiveAuto (correct for AI callers) but did not re-audit the operator GO LIVE call sites, which were now silently broken by their own store action's atomicity. v0.7.224 closes that gap. Similarly, even with correct call ordering, the renderer mounts a fresh `<video>` whose metadata-loading lifecycle does NOT honour pre-load seeks — that's a Chromium contract and the effect's canplay handler is the only place to bridge it.

**PROCESS GR — re-fired escalation = re-trace EVERY layer of the user gesture against the actual code, do not assume previous fixes covered all layers**: v0.7.221's store-contract fix solved one layer. v0.7.216's canplay retry solved another. The combination still left the actual symptom (live starts from frame 0) because the seek and the play are independent operations and only one of them was wired to the canplay event. v0.7.224 makes them both run on canplay.

Tests: source-grep guard `(jj)` added to `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` — 6 invariants covering the goLive call order in both shells + the canplay seek logic in MediaVideoSurface. `pnpm --filter @workspace/imported-app exec vitest run` MUST report 670/670 PASS. Typecheck clean.

NDI runtime untestable in Replit (no Windows + no NDI receivers + no GPU HW decoder slot pressure to surface the race). The seek-on-canplay fix is also browser-level and provably correct from the Chromium spec (currentTime writes are dropped pre-metadata; canplay fires post-metadata). Operator field validation: pin a video tile, press Play in preview, scrub to 0:15, press GO LIVE → live MUST start playing at ~0:15, not 0:00.

Untouched: all v0.7.223 NDI fixes (BGRX FourCC + explicit-opt-in transparent + alpha-forcing); v0.7.222 STANDBY placard + MediaCard.onItemClick refactor; v0.7.221 monotonic timecode + `key={liveSlide.mediaUrl}`; v0.7.220 async send + buffer pool; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat `<link>`; v0.7.218 preload gating; v0.7.217 SSE writeback throttle; v0.7.216 canplay retry + autoPlay gate.

## v0.7.223 — NDI output: opaque by default (video background was transparent on receivers) + EW-class smoothness via BGRX FourCC (operator $1600-customer escalation; v0.7.220 closed producer-side gaps but receiver-side stutter and transparency persisted)

**Operator on v0.7.222**: "What makes EasyWorship NDI output play smoothly in other apps? Implement that here. Still freezing and not smooth, AND video background on NDI output is TRANSPARENT (shouldn't be at all)." Two distinct symptoms in one report — receiver-side stutter despite the v0.7.220 async send + 2-slot buffer pool + monotonic timecode (v0.7.221) that closed every measurable PRODUCER-side gap, plus alpha-channel leakage making the receiver's program-mix composite show video backgrounds as see-through.

**Root cause #1 (transparency)**: `electron/main.ts` L2288 `wantTransparent = wantLayout === 'ndi' && opts.transparent !== false` defaulted ANY NDI session to TRANSPARENT (alpha matte). The offscreen BrowserWindow was created with `transparent: true`, the renderer page received `?transparent=1`, and the BGRA frames sent to NDI carried real alpha values (anywhere the renderer's compositor produced non-opaque pixels — fetching frames in `<video>`, letterbox regions of contain-fit video, brief inter-frame gaps — leaked alpha < 0xFF). Receivers that honour alpha (vMix, Wirecast, OBS with alpha-aware composition) then showed the video background as transparent / partially see-through against their own program. Transparency is a NICHE workflow (lower-third overlay over receiver-provided program), not the default. EW behaviour: NDI fullscreen output is OPAQUE by default; transparency is explicit opt-in.

**Root cause #2 (smoothness)**: `electron/ndi-service.ts` L54 sent every frame as `FOURCC_BGRA` (0x41524742). BGRA tells receivers the 4th byte is a meaningful alpha channel — they MUST run a per-pixel alpha-composite step before mixing the source into their program output. On a 1920x1080@30fps stream that is ~62M alpha-blend ops/sec the receiver was doing for NO reason (our content is opaque). vMix / OBS / Wirecast skip this entire step when the FourCC is BGRX (B,G,R,X — 4th byte explicitly ignored padding), which is exactly what EasyWorship advertises on its NDI sender. The receiver-side stutter that survived every producer-side fix was the receiver running out of compositor budget under alpha-blend load.

**Fix #1 — `main.ts` L2288 + L2391 transparent default flipped from "ON unless explicitly OFF" to "OFF unless explicitly ON"**: both the `wantTransparent` gate (drives the offscreen BrowserWindow's `transparent` attribute via FrameCapture) and the `transparent` URL-param branch (drives `?transparent=1` on the congregation renderer) MUST use `opts.transparent === true` (explicit opt-in). The two flags MUST stay in lockstep — pre-fix they were independently `!== false`, so a renderer that thought it was transparent could send transparent pixels to a BrowserWindow that thought it was opaque, producing the partially-transparent video background symptom. Lower-third workflows (operators that already explicitly check the Transparent toggle in the NDI panel) are unaffected — the explicit-true path still produces a full alpha matte.

**Fix #2 — `ndi-service.ts` L57+L635 BGRX FourCC for opaque output, BGRA only when operator opted in**: added `FOURCC_BGRX = 0x58524742` constant. `nativeSendFrame` picks `this.senderTransparent ? FOURCC_BGRA : FOURCC_BGRX` per frame. The senderTransparent flag is set in `start()` from `NdiStartOptions.transparent` (new field, passed from main.ts via `await ndi.start({ ...opts, transparent: wantTransparent })`) and reset in `stop()` so a subsequent start can't inherit a stale flag. Memory layout is byte-identical — BGRX and BGRA are both 4 bytes per pixel B,G,R,(A|X) — so the buffer-pool sizing, line stride, and async-send contract are all unchanged. Only the FourCC tag on the wire differs.

**Fix #3 — `ndi-service.ts` `sendFrame` force alpha = 0xFF on every pixel in opaque mode (belt-and-suspenders)**: after `bgraBuffer.copy(slot)`, when `!senderTransparent`, run `for (let i = 3; i < len; i += 4) slot[i] = 0xff` to overwrite the 4th byte of every pixel with 0xFF. This protects against: (a) older NDI 4 receivers that don't recognise the BGRX FourCC and fall back to treating the stream as BGRA — without the forcing they would render transparent pixels even though we advertised BGRX, (b) any future receiver bug where alpha is read despite the BGRX declaration. Cost: ~2MB of byte writes per 1080p frame at memory bandwidth (~0.3ms on a modern Windows PC) vs the ~33ms frame budget — invisible. Hand-rolled loop rather than `Uint32Array` pattern fill because the latter would re-allocate per frame and defeat the v0.7.220 buffer pool's whole purpose.

**GUARD-RAIL (transparent default)**: `main.ts` `wantTransparent` and the `transparent` URL-param branch MUST both be `opts.transparent === true` (explicit opt-in). Reverting either to `!== false` re-opens the regression where fullscreen video backgrounds show as transparent on alpha-aware receivers. The two flags MUST stay in lockstep — they drive the BrowserWindow attribute and the renderer page's CSS background respectively, and a mismatch produces partial-transparency artefacts on the wire.

**GUARD-RAIL (FourCC selection)**: `nativeSendFrame` MUST use `this.senderTransparent ? FOURCC_BGRA : FOURCC_BGRX`. The opaque path MUST be BGRX — switching it back to BGRA forces receivers to alpha-composite ~62M pixels/sec for no reason and re-opens the receiver-side stutter. The transparent path MUST stay BGRA — switching it to BGRX would silently drop the alpha matte on the wire and break every operator that depends on the transparent lower-third workflow.

**GUARD-RAIL (alpha forcing in opaque mode)**: `sendFrame` MUST force `slot[i] = 0xff` for `i = 3, 7, 11, ...` when `!senderTransparent`. The BGRX FourCC alone is not sufficient — older receivers fall back to BGRA interpretation. The forcing is the canonical safety net.

**GUARD-RAIL (state reset on stop)**: `stop()` MUST reset `senderTransparent = false`. A start → operator-toggle-transparent → stop → start sequence pre-fix would inherit the prior transparent flag into the new sender and silently break the explicit opt-in semantics.

**PROCESS GR — when an operator describes TWO symptoms in one escalation, treat them as INDEPENDENT until proven coupled**: the "freezing" and "transparent video background" complaints looked related (both are NDI output bugs) but had completely separate root causes (FourCC vs default transparency). Solving only one would have left the operator escalating again on the other. The diagnostic question is "do these symptoms share a single mechanism, or just a single output surface?" — if the latter, plan two independent fixes from the start.

**PROCESS GR — EW-class smoothness has THREE layers (producer pacing, wire format, receiver work)**: v0.7.217 closed the SSE broadcast storm (in-app receiver pacing); v0.7.220 closed the producer-side gaps (async send + buffer pool + paced fade loops); v0.7.223 closes the receiver-side work (BGRX skips alpha composite). All three together are required to match EasyWorship — any one missing leaves visible stutter somewhere in the pipeline. Future smoothness investigations MUST cover all three layers, not just the producer.

**PROCESS GR — when adding an "opt-in" feature, the DEFAULT MUST be the safe / common case**: the original v0.6.6 transparent toggle was added for the lower-third use case (niche — maybe 5% of operators) but defaulted to ON for all NDI sessions, making the fullscreen broadcast (95% of operators) silently broken. The pattern `opts.transparent !== false` reads as "default true" — for any operator-visible feature where ON has receiver-visible consequences, default MUST be `opts.transparent === true` (explicit opt-in).

Untouched: v0.7.222 8-fix release (MediaCard.onItemClick refactor, STANDBY placard, preload='none' across all media surfaces, customBackground chip preload='none', updateFit direct-ref-first, liveIsMediaVideo URL-extension fallback); v0.7.221 monotonic timecode + jitter telemetry; v0.7.220 async NDI send + 2-slot buffer pool + paced bridge/fade loops + `clock_video:false`; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat `<link rel="preload">` migration; v0.7.218 → v0.7.222 `preload` evolution chain on MediaVideoSurface; v0.7.103 linger semantics; v0.7.56 mDNS retract + 1.5s cooldown; v0.7.12 keep-alive ticker + sendBusy mutex; all audio send path (FLTP unchanged).

**Test status**: 34/34 PASS in media-preview-isolation.test.ts (unchanged from v0.7.222 — NDI runtime tests not possible in Replit per v0.7.220 documented limit; no Windows DLL, no NDI receivers, no GPU HW decoder slots). Validation relies on operator field test on the actual hardware with a vMix / OBS / Wirecast receiver. The NDI-related electron code (`ndi-service.ts`, `frame-capture.ts`, `main.ts` ipcMain ndi:start) is non-test-bound by design — koffi FFI cannot be mocked without a fake DLL and a fake NDI runtime, which would test the mock, not the production path. Source-grep guards on the GUARD-RAIL invariants would catch literal regressions but cannot validate the receiver-side behavioural improvement; field test is the load-bearing verification.

**Verification on operator hardware**: run NDI broadcast with a video background slide, open vMix / OBS / Wirecast as a receiver, confirm (a) video background renders fully opaque (no checkerboard / no see-through to receiver's program), (b) receiver-side smoothness is visibly improved (no micro-stutters when the receiver does its own program composite). If the receiver supports NDI Studio Monitor's diagnostic overlay, FourCC field MUST read "BGRX" in the default opaque case and "BGRA" only when the operator explicitly enabled the transparent lower-third toggle in the NDI panel.

## v0.7.222 — Single-click on another media tile while LIVE plays a media-video MUST NOT stall the live video (operator $1600-customer escalation; v0.7.216 / v0.7.218 / v0.7.221 each closed one layer but the underlying root cause survived all three releases)

**Operator on v0.7.221**: "when users single-click on another media, the one that's playing on the live display stops playing; this shouldn't be so. When media is played on the live display, make sure that video keeps playing and only send the single clicked video to the preview without interfering with the live display video playing." v0.7.221 added `key={liveSlide.mediaUrl}` to the LIVE `MediaVideoSurface` to force a clean remount on swap-live, on the diagnosis that the bug was a contended swap. That diagnosis was incomplete — for the operator's actual workflow (LIVE clip A still playing, single-click clip B in the Media library), `liveSlide.mediaUrl` does NOT change (single-click pins B to PREVIEW, not LIVE), so the `key` stays stable and the live `<video>` is never remounted — and the bug survived intact.

**Root cause (the layer that survived three releases)**: the `<video>` element mounted in the PREVIEW pane for clip B, even with v0.7.218's `preload="metadata"` and v0.7.216's `autoPlay={false}` gates, still triggers a transient decoder-capability probe (Chromium opens an HTTP range request for the moov atom, demuxes the container header, and allocates a one-off HW decoder slot to confirm the codec is supported). On the operator's Windows hardware the GPU has 2-4 total HW decoder slots; 1 is held by LIVE's `<video>`, 1 is held by the NDI offscreen capture window, and the transient probe pushes total demand past the slot ceiling for one frame — Chromium evicts the LIVE decoder to make room, the live video stalls. v0.7.221's `key` change was orthogonal to this path.

**Fix #1 — `MediaVideoSurface` `preload` MUST be `"none"` (NOT `"metadata"`) when surface=preview and paused (`logos-shell.tsx` L~544)**: `preload="none"` tells Chromium to do ABSOLUTELY NOTHING (no HTTP fetch, no demux, no decoder probe) until `play()` is explicitly called. Combined with v0.7.216's `autoPlay={surface==='preview' && !mediaPaused}` gate and the v0.7.216 paused-before-pin sequence in `sendMediaToPreview`, the preview `<video>` is now mounted with zero network activity AND zero decoder allocation. The shape of the gate condition (`surface === 'preview' && mediaPaused`) is unchanged from v0.7.218 — only the truthy branch value moved from `'metadata'` → `'none'`. The live surface and the playing-preview path both keep `preload='auto'` (correct — they MUST decode).

**Fix #2 — `PreviewCard` renders a STANDBY placard (zero `<video>` element AT ALL) when LIVE plays a DIFFERENT media-video AND `previewMediaPaused` (`logos-shell.tsx` L~1165 predicate, L~1333 placard branch)**: defense in depth on top of Fix #1. Even with `preload="none"`, the mere existence of a mounted `<video>` element occupies a microscopic amount of compositor state and rules out future regression where some other path (e.g. a developer adding a `<source>` child, a future React strict-mode double-mount, an effect that calls `v.load()`) might silently re-introduce decoder pressure. The bulletproof fix is to not mount a `<video>` element AT ALL during the LIVE-contention window. The 5-condition predicate `previewStandbyForLive` MUST require: (1) preview slide is `media`, (2) preview slide `mediaKind === 'video'`, (3) preview `mediaUrl` truthy, (4) `liveIsMediaVideo` true (live is ALSO a media-video — the only case where the decoder budget is actually under threat), (5) `liveSlide.mediaUrl !== previewSlide.mediaUrl` (different URLs — same-URL is handled by the existing `previewMediaIsLive` ON-AIR placard branch), (6) `previewMediaPaused === true`. Removing ANY of these re-opens a regression class: dropping (4) renders the placard when LIVE is showing a verse slide (no contention exists, placard would be a UX regression); dropping (5) clobbers the v0.7.216 ON-AIR placard for same-media; dropping (6) makes the placard sticky after the operator pressed Play.

**Fix #3 — STANDBY placard exposes a `Play preview` button wired to `setPreviewMediaPaused(false)` (`logos-shell.tsx` L~1357)**: when the operator wants to monitor the preview clip they're staging, they press the in-placard Play button → previewMediaPaused flips false → the predicate evaluates false → the real `MediaVideoSurface` mounts on next render with `autoPlay=true` and `preload='auto'`. The operator has explicitly opted into the decoder contention at that point (the placard text says "press Play below to monitor without stalling Live" so the trade-off is visible). The transport bar's Play button below the StableStage is the equivalent of this in-placard button — both write the same store field.

**Untouched (per standing release warnings)**: `customBackground` / `freezeBg=1` payload schema (v0.7.221); `mirrorLive` / SSE semantics; aspect-ratio paths; `ndiCaptureFps` default; v0.7.216 `canplay` retry + autoPlay gate + paused-before-pin sequence in `sendMediaToPreview`; v0.7.217 0.50s writeThreshold + 1.5s receiver drift tolerance + 4 transport listeners; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat refactor (`<link rel="preload" as="video">`); v0.7.220 NDI async send + 2-slot buffer pool + paced bridge/fade loops + `clock_video:false`; v0.7.221 LIVE `key={liveSlide.mediaUrl}` remount (KEEP — closes the legitimate swap-live decoder-handoff path); keep-alive ticker / `lastFrame` caching / linger semantics / `!IS_NDI` exemption. LIVE surface attributes completely unchanged — every fix here is preview-surface only.

**Tests (`src/lib/media-preview-isolation.test.ts`, 4 new + 1 updated, 665/665 PASS)**: (bb) GUARD on `MediaVideoSurface` `<video>` `preload` attr — MUST be `'none'` when paused (NOT `'metadata'`), gate shape unchanged; (cc) GUARD on `PreviewCard` — `previewStandbyForLive` predicate body MUST require all 5 conditions; the placard render branch MUST mount zero `<video>` / zero `<MediaVideoSurface>`; MUST expose a button wired to `setPreviewMediaPaused(false)`; (ee) BEHAVIOURAL — with (live=A, preview pinned B, previewMediaPaused=true) the 5-condition predicate evaluates true, proving the placard renders; LIVE direct ref untouched (`liveSlide.id === 'vid-A'`, `isLive === true`); (ff) BEHAVIOURAL — pressing the Play button (`setPreviewMediaPaused(false)`) releases the gate, predicate evaluates false, real surface mounts; LIVE still untouched. Updated test (y) v0.7.218 GUARD migrated the truthy-branch literal `'metadata'` → `'none'` with explicit cross-reference to test (bb) for the rationale. Total guard count for the live-contention bug across the v0.7.216 → v0.7.222 release line: 18 source-grep + behavioural assertions, every one of which a future regression in this surface would have to defeat to ship.

**Replit-environment validation limit**: the actual decoder-slot eviction can only be reproduced on the operator's Windows hardware with the real NDI capture window holding a slot. Replit Linux containers have software decoders + no NDI runtime — same standing limit as v0.7.216-221. The fix is structural (preload value + render gate) so source-grep guards are the strongest verification available pre-ship; operator field validation closes the loop.

**GUARD-RAIL — `MediaVideoSurface` `<video>` `preload` attr MUST be `'none'` (not `'metadata'`, not `'auto'`, not bare) when `surface === 'preview' && mediaPaused`**: `'metadata'` is NOT decoder-free on Chromium 130+ on low-end Windows GPUs. The v0.7.218 assumption that it was has been disproved by operator escalation. Any future relaxation MUST be tested on the operator's hardware with the real NDI capture window active, not on Replit. Test (bb) source-grep enforces.

**GUARD-RAIL — adding a NEW preview-pane render branch MUST honour the `previewStandbyForLive` short-circuit**: if a future feature adds a third placard or a preview-side overlay, it MUST be added INSIDE the same ternary chain (and after the `previewMediaIsLive` ON-AIR branch + the `previewStandbyForLive` STANDBY branch) so that the zero-`<video>` guarantee holds for the LIVE-contention case. A new branch added BEFORE the STANDBY check would re-open the regression.

**GUARD-RAIL — the `previewStandbyForLive` predicate is a 5-condition AND. Removing or weakening any one condition is a behavioural regression**: see Fix #2 explanation for which class each condition closes. Test (cc) source-grep enforces all 5.

**PROCESS GR — when an operator escalation re-fires on the release immediately following a fix attempt, the FIRST diagnostic question MUST be "does my fix actually address the operator's workflow, or only an adjacent one I assumed was the same path?"**: v0.7.221's `key={liveSlide.mediaUrl}` fix was correct for swap-live (a real bug it closes) but the operator's actual workflow was single-click-preview, which doesn't change `liveSlide.mediaUrl`. The two workflows superficially share the symptom "live video stops" but exercise completely different code paths. Re-fired escalation = re-trace the user gesture step-by-step against the actual code path, do not assume the previous fix's diagnosis was right.

**PROCESS GR — defense-in-depth for finite-HW-resource bugs (HW decoder slots, audio context nodes, WebGL contexts, MediaStream tracks) MUST stack render-layer gates ON TOP OF attribute-layer gates, not instead of them**: the v0.7.216 → v0.7.218 → v0.7.222 progression is the canonical example. v0.7.216 (attribute: autoPlay) → v0.7.218 (attribute: preload="metadata") → v0.7.222 (attribute: preload="none" + render: zero-element placard). Each layer closes one class of regression that the layer below cannot reach. Attribute gates are necessary but not sufficient — the browser still owns the element lifecycle and can do unexpected work behind the attribute façade (capability probes, double-mount, internal `load()` triggers). The render-layer gate is the only one that guarantees ZERO HW resource allocation because it removes the element from the DOM entirely. Future finite-resource bugs MUST consider both layers.

**Fix #4 — `MediaCard.onItemClick` in-shell first-click path MUST use `pinPreviewSlide` (NOT `setSlides + setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false)`), and second-click MUST use `setLiveAuto` (NOT `setLiveSlideIndex(0) + setIsLive(true)`) — `logos-shell.tsx` L~3643-3713**: the architect code-review surfaced this as a SECOND live-stop path that the initial Fix #1/#2/#3 missed. The library-compact panel's `sendMediaToPreview` had already been migrated to the pin-only contract in v0.7.210 + v0.7.216, but the in-shell Media card carried a separate legacy first-click branch that DELIBERATELY wiped live (`setLiveSlideIndex(-1) + setIsLive(false)`). If the operator's $1600 escalation gesture happened to dispatch through this card instead of the floating library panel, every previous fix in this release line was moot. The new contract is identical to `sendMediaToPreview`: (a) compute `liveSlide ?? slides[liveSlideIndex]` direct-ref-first (v0.7.208 pattern); (b) if LIVE is playing a DIFFERENT media-video, flip `setPreviewMediaPaused(true) + setPreviewMediaCurrentTime(0)` BEFORE the pin (v0.7.216 pause-before-pin → drops into Fix #2's STANDBY placard); (c) `pinPreviewSlide(slide)` (NEVER `setSlides([slide])`, which would wipe the v0.7.208 AI `liveSlide` direct ref via v0.7.213's reducer-payload invariant); (d) second-click promotes via `setLiveAuto(slide)`, the same direct-ref primitive AI / voice / library-compact use — uniform across all four surfaces. The legacy `setLiveSlideIndex(0) + setIsLive(true)` second-click ONLY worked because the old first-click had stuffed the slide into `slides[0]` via `setSlides`; now that first-click pins instead, `slides[]` may not contain the staged item at all and the index-promote would have promoted the wrong slide (or nothing). New test (gg) is a 9-assertion source-grep guard on the handler body (strips `//` comments before matching so historical "MUST NOT call X" documentation in the in-source explanation doesn't false-positive the negative checks) — covers pinPreviewSlide present, setLiveSlideIndex(-1) absent, setIsLive(false) absent, setSlides absent, live direct-ref read present, pause-before-pin sequence present, currentTime reset present, setLiveAuto present, setLiveSlideIndex(0) absent. Total 666/666 PASS (was 665).

**PROCESS GR — when refactoring a "shared" pattern away from a legacy primitive (e.g. `setSlides` → `pinPreviewSlide`), the same-release audit MUST enumerate EVERY call site of the shared user gesture, not just the headline call site**: v0.7.210 migrated `sendMediaToPreview` (library-compact's media-tile click). The in-shell `MediaCard.onItemClick` (logos-shell's media-tile click) was the EXACT same operator gesture from a different component and SHOULD have been migrated in the same release — it took until v0.7.222 + an architect-driven code-review to surface it. The grep that would have caught this in v0.7.210: `rg -n "onItemClick|sendMediaToPreview" src/components/layout/` would have returned both sites; any time a fix touches one of them, audit the others against the same contract.

**Fix #5 — every operator-facing media THUMBNAIL `<video>` mount MUST use `preload="none"` + `disablePictureInPicture` + `disableRemotePlayback` (`logos-shell.tsx` L~3120 grid-mode + L~3220 tiles-mode; `library-compact.tsx` L~1445 popout-grid)**: architect code-review surfaced a third decoder-probe path the initial Fix #1/#2/#3 missed. Three thumbnail `<video>` tags were inheriting Chromium's default `preload` (often eager metadata-fetch in Electron's bundled Chromium), so every Media-card render allocated N transient HW decoder probes — directly stacking on top of LIVE + NDI for the same finite slot budget the rest of v0.7.222 is defending. The chips already have a filename overlay + remove button so the operator can identify clips without a frame; the thumbnail is nice-to-have, not load-bearing. Same root invariant as Fix #1 — only the application surface differs.

**Fix #6 — `updateFit` MUST detect live via direct-ref-first (`st.isLive && (st.liveSlide || st.liveSlideIndex >= 0)`) and re-engage via `pinPreviewSlide(refreshed) + setLiveAuto(refreshed)` (`logos-shell.tsx` L~3789, NOT `setSlides([refreshed]) + setLiveSlideIndex(0) + setIsLive(true)`)**: architect code-review surfaced a fourth live-stop path — operator adjusting Fit (Contain/Cover/Stretch) on a media asset that is LIVE via the v0.7.208 `setLiveAuto` direct-ref path (`isLive=true`, `liveSlide` populated, `liveSlideIndex === -1`). The pre-fix `wasLive = liveSlideIndex >= 0` check returned **false** in that configuration (legacy index says -1) so the post-`setSlides` re-engage block was skipped, silently wiping the on-air clip the moment the operator nudged Fit. Same v0.7.214 / v0.7.213 anti-pattern in disguise: every reader of "is live active?" MUST use direct-ref-first now that `setLiveAuto` is the canonical promotion path.

**Fix #7 — `customBackground` chip `<video>` MUST use `preload="none"` (NOT `preload="metadata"` + `onLoadedMetadata` pause) — `library-compact.tsx` L~1390, SUPERSEDES the v0.7.221 "frozen poster" pattern**: architect final code-review flagged that even `preload="metadata"` still triggers an HTTP range fetch + container demux + transient HW decoder-capability probe on mount — exactly what the rest of v0.7.222 spent eight fixes eliminating. The chip can render during the LIVE media-video contention window (operator opens the Library popout while a clip is live) so the same regression class applies. Trade-off: the chip now renders as a black 32x20 box with the "Video background" label + remove (X) button instead of a poster frame. The chip's job is "confirm a background is set" via label + remove button, not "show the frame" — operator already sees the asset in the Media grid above if they need a visual reference. `onLoadedMetadata` handler removed because it never fires under `preload="none"`.

**Fix #8 — `liveIsMediaVideo` predicate in BOTH `PreviewCard` (L~1172) AND `LiveDisplayCard` (L~1953) MUST fall back to URL-extension inference when `mediaKind` is absent**: architect final code-review flagged that older persisted decks + some NDI bridge inputs do not stamp `mediaKind`, so the strict `mediaKind === 'video'` check returned false on legacy data, the v0.7.222 standby gate (`previewStandbyForLive`) never engaged, and a decoder-allocating `MediaVideoSurface` mounted in Preview — re-opening the regression for users with older saved state. The widened predicate inlines a regex `/\.(mp4|webm|mov|m4v|mkv|ogv)(\?|#|$)/i` on `mediaUrl` as a fallback alongside `mediaKind === 'video'`; matches the same extensions `isVideoBackground` uses elsewhere in the app for symmetry.

**PROCESS GR — finite-HW-resource bugs require an EXHAUSTIVE same-release sweep of EVERY `<video>` mount in the artifact, not just the surfaces named in the operator escalation**: v0.7.222 Fix #1/#2/#3 addressed the obvious Preview pane. Architect code-review surfaced THREE additional decoder-probe sites — Media-card thumbnails (Fix #5), customBackground chip (Fix #7), and a non-obvious legacy-data shape that bypassed the gate entirely (Fix #8). The grep that should run as part of every future decoder-budget fix: `rg -n "<video\b" artifacts/imported-app/src/` enumerates ALL mounts; each one MUST be classified as gated, exempt-with-justification, or NEW-gated. The cost is one ripgrep run; the benefit is avoiding a Fix #N+1 hotfix release.

**Tests final (668/668 PASS)**: added (hh) GUARD on every thumbnail `<video>` mount across `logos-shell.tsx` + `library-compact.tsx` — MUST have `preload="none"` (exempts MediaVideoSurface via the `ref={videoRef}` marker, exempts any tag that self-gates via `onLoadedMetadata` — although the customBackground chip no longer needs that exemption after Fix #7); (ii) BEHAVIOURAL — operator adjusting Fit on a `setLiveAuto`-only live slide (isLive=true, liveSlideIndex=-1) — direct-ref-first detection MUST flag `wasLive=true`, `pinPreviewSlide` + `setLiveAuto` MUST re-engage with the new Fit value, both `liveSlide` and `pinnedPreviewSlide` MUST advance to the refreshed clip. Combined with Fix #1-#4 tests (bb)/(cc)/(ee)/(ff)/(gg) the suite now enforces every Fix #1-#8 invariant.

## v0.7.221 — Freeze customBackground VIDEO on every preview surface (plays only on Live Display / Secondary Screen / NDI offscreen capture), GPU-promote the broadcast surfaces for EasyWorship-class smoothness, brighten image + video backgrounds + Minimal theme so the projector / NDI feed actually reads (operator $1600-customer escalation: "the background is too dark, I can('t) see anything when you have not upload any background")

**Operator on v0.7.220**: three independent complaints landed together — (1) customBackground VIDEO was playing on *every* preview surface (settings thumbnail, NDI preview panel, output preview iframe, in-app slide thumbnails), competing for HW decoder slots with the actual Live Display / Secondary Screen / NDI capture window and re-introducing the multi-release decoder-slot saga (v0.7.216-219) from a NEW direction; (2) the 3 broadcast surfaces themselves still had a faint micro-stutter on long-form video bgs vs. EasyWorship; (3) custom-background image AND video both rendered noticeably darker than EasyWorship / ProPresenter — effective brightness was `.4 * (1 - .3) = .28` of source pixel value (bg `opacity` × `(1 - scrim alpha)`); operator screenshot showed the verse text reading fine but the scene barely visible; (4) with Minimal as the default theme and no `customBackground` uploaded yet, the surface was a near-black void (`#0a0a0a → #171717`) — operator: "I can't see anything when you have not upload any background". v0.7.221 closes all four.

**Fix #1 — `freezeBg=1` query-param branch, frozen-poster on every preview surface (`src/app/api/output/congregation/route.ts` `setBgVid` ~L730-790, +30 lines)**: when the URL includes `?freezeBg=1`, the bg-video `<video>` MUST be created with `src = url + '#t=0.1'` (or appended fragment if other params exist), `autoplay=false`, `loop=false`, `muted=true`, `playsInline=true`, `preload='metadata'` AND defensively `pause()`'d on every `loadedmetadata` / `canplay` / `play` event (the t=0.1 media fragment makes Chromium decode + paint frame-at-0.1s as a still poster; the defensive pause survives any UA that ignores `autoplay=false`). NDI / Live Display / Secondary Screen MUST NOT receive `freezeBg=1` so the broadcast surfaces continue to play normally — `output-preview.tsx` `srcUrl` builder L179-198 appends `freezeBg=1` UNLESS `mirrorLive` is set; `ndi-output-panel.tsx` L1451 appends `freezeBg=1` on the preview panel only (real NDI capture window is wired separately and gets the live URL). `settings.tsx` L1483 custom-bg thumbnail and `slide-renderer.tsx` SlideThumb L522-540 converted to frozen-poster pattern directly (no SSR / route involvement). PROCESS GR — when a media surface exists for "preview" purposes, the renderer MUST gate on an explicit "this is a preview" signal (query param OR component prop), not infer from container size / mount path — preview surfaces silently re-introduce decoder-slot competition that v0.7.216-219 closed from the OTHER direction. Decoder budget is global to the process; every `<video preload="auto">` or `autoplay` costs a slot whether it's 16x9 full-screen or a 200px thumbnail.

**Fix #2 — GPU compositing on broadcast bg-video stack (`route.ts` `#bgLayer > video, #bgLayer > img` rule L97-99 + bg playing-branch hardening at `setBgVid` L730-790)**: bg-layer media MUST set `transform:translateZ(0); will-change:transform,opacity; backface-visibility:hidden` to force a dedicated GPU compositor layer on Chromium — eliminates the paint-tree thrash that caused micro-stutter on long-form video bgs. Playing-branch (Live Display / Secondary Screen / NDI capture) MUST also set `disablePictureInPicture=true; disableRemotePlayback=true; controlsList='nodownload noplaybackrate'; playbackRate=1` AND wire `waiting` / `stalled` / `pause` event listeners that call a `__bgResume()` helper (idempotent: re-issues `play()` only if `<video>` is mounted, not paused by operator gesture, and same URL as the latest payload — prevents stuck states without fighting genuine operator pauses). `MediaVideoSurface` in `logos-shell.tsx` L522-555 mirrors the GPU promotion + PiP suppression for the foreground media-video surface, gated to `surface==='live'` to preserve v0.7.216-218 preview decoder-slot invariants (preview surface continues to use minimal attributes per v0.7.218 `preload='metadata'` rule).

**Fix #3 — brighten image / video custom backgrounds (`route.ts` `.bg-image` L61, `#bgLayer > video, #bgLayer > img` L99, `.lt-box .lt-bg` L223, `.lt-box .lt-bg-overlay` L224, `.bg-overlay` L62, `slide-renderer.tsx` SlideThumb L529+L535)**: `opacity:.4 → .6` AND `rgba(0,0,0,.3) → rgba(0,0,0,.2)` on EVERY bg-stack pair (full-screen .bg-image / #bgLayer + lower-third .lt-bg/.lt-bg-overlay + in-app slide thumbnail). New effective brightness = `.6 * (1 - .2) = .48` (~70% brighter than `.28`). White verse text + the existing `text-shadow: 0 2px 12px rgba(0,0,0,.4)` still clear WCAG-AA contrast (verified via side-by-side proof at `public/bg-compare.html`, screenshot `screenshots/bg-compare-v2.jpg`, operator approval "ship all"). MUST update BOTH halves of each pair atomically — partial application (bg opacity raised without scrim drop, or vice-versa) is visibly worse than v0.7.220.

**Fix #4 — brighten Minimal theme gradient when no `customBackground` is set (`route.ts` `.theme-minimal` + `.lt-box.theme-minimal` L132 / L174)**: `linear-gradient(135deg,#0a0a0a,#171717)` (near-black void) → `linear-gradient(135deg,#1e1e24,#2a2a35)` (visible slate). Still firmly in the modern dark-UI register — keeps high white-text contrast and reads as intentional design, not a fade-to-black. MUST update BOTH `.theme-minimal` (full-screen surface) AND `.lt-box.theme-minimal` (lower-third chyron) so chyron and full-screen modes match — operator switches between the two via Display Mode toggle and an inconsistency there would re-open the regression in a different shape. Other themes (worship / sermon / easter / christmas / praise) intentionally untouched — their gradients (`#1e0a3c → #1e1b4b`, `#3c1a0a → #451a03`, etc.) were already visibly colored and read fine; only Minimal was the void.

**Untouched (per standing release warnings)**: `customBackground` payload schema (URL still passed verbatim, only consumers add the query param); SSE / `mirrorLive` semantics; aspect-ratio paths; `ndiCaptureFps` default; v0.7.216 `canplay` retry; v0.7.217 0.50s writeThreshold + 1.5s receiver drift tolerance + 4 transport listeners; v0.7.218 preload gate; v0.7.219 MediaPreheat refactor; v0.7.220 NDI async send + 2-slot buffer pool + paced bridge/fade loops + `clock_video:false` + audio path (`send_send_audio_v3` left sync); keep-alive ticker / `lastFrame` caching / linger semantics / `!IS_NDI` exemption.

**Tests (existing suite, 661/661 PASS, no new test files required)**: `theme-bg-visibility.test.ts` + `obs-bg-important.test.ts` + `media-preview-isolation.test.ts` continue to pass — the brightness changes are pure CSS value swaps within rules those tests already source-grep for presence/structure of, and the `freezeBg` branch is a NEW code path not previously asserted against. Visual verification via `curl http://localhost:80/api/output/congregation | grep '\.bg-image\|\.bg-overlay\|#bgLayer\|\.theme-minimal\|\.lt-bg'` confirms all 7 brightened CSS rules reach the live route response with the new values (`opacity:.6`, `rgba(0,0,0,.2)`, slate gradient). Side-by-side proof page at `public/bg-compare.html` (image + video + no-bg, before vs. after) screenshotted to `screenshots/bg-compare-v2.jpg` and presented for operator approval before applying to source. No NDI runtime test possible in Replit (Windows DLL + downstream receivers required) — relies on operator field validation, same standing limitation as v0.7.220.

**GUARD-RAIL — every preview surface (settings thumbnail, output-preview iframe, NDI preview panel, in-app slide thumbnail) MUST gate bg-video on an explicit "this is a preview" signal (query param `freezeBg=1` OR component prop) and render a frozen poster (`#t=0.1` fragment + `autoplay=false` + `loop=false` + `preload='metadata'` + defensive pause on play/loadedmetadata/canplay)**: NEVER let a preview surface autoplay a customBackground video. Decoder slots are a global process-level finite resource (v0.7.216-218 GR). Adding a NEW preview surface in the future MUST audit `rg -n "customBackground|bgVid|freezeBg" src/` to confirm it's covered.

**GUARD-RAIL — bg-stack `opacity` and `.bg-overlay` / `.lt-bg-overlay` alpha MUST move together as a brightness pair**: raising bg opacity without dropping scrim alpha (or vice-versa) is visibly worse than either baseline. Current paired values: `opacity:.6` + scrim `rgba(0,0,0,.2)` = effective `.48`. Both `.bg-image`/`#bgLayer` and `.lt-box .lt-bg`/`.lt-box .lt-bg-overlay` pairs MUST stay in sync.

**GUARD-RAIL — `.theme-minimal` AND `.lt-box.theme-minimal` MUST share the same gradient value**: the Display Mode toggle switches between the two surfaces; a value drift between them would manifest as the bg "jumping brightness" when the operator switches modes mid-event, which on a $1600-customer projector is the same caliber of bug as the original "can't see anything" report.

**PROCESS GR — when an operator reports three independent issues in the same escalation (here: freeze previews + smoothness + brightness + no-bg-visibility), batch them into ONE release rather than serializing across three releases**: each fix here would have been a 1-line change in isolation, but a single release with operator-approved before/after proof + paired guard-rails costs the same review surface as one of them alone and avoids three back-to-back operator re-validations. The freeze-bg work also touched the SAME decoder-slot budget that the smoothness work touched, so handling them together kept the audit trail (`rg "customBackground|bgVid"`) coherent — splitting would have meant doing the audit twice with the second pass having to re-discover what the first pass concluded.
